2. `requiredVotes()` in MotionsModal (:103) hardcodes procedural thresholds and never reads settings.substantiveThreshold. I raised it as a "label that lies", but if the intent was that procedural motions genuinely follow a fixed rule regardless of the substantive threshold, then only the label needs changing, not the maths.
3. `handleNextSpeaker` is hard-gated at `speakersList.length < 2` for the Call First Speaker button while the Start button honours `gslRequireNextSpeaker`. With the setting off, a chair still cannot call their only speaker. Flagged as copy/consistency above; the underlying condition is a functional inconsistency with the documented setting.
4. GSL progress `(speakerTimeRemaining / committee.speakerTimeLimit) * 100` (:2179) exceeds 100 once extra time is granted; the bar is clipped by overflow-hidden so it just pins full, but the value is wrong and the same expression is used for the colour threshold.
5. `caucusMaxReachedMsg` uses a bare setTimeout with no cleanup on unmount (:2981).
6. MobileGate's heading uses a straight apostrophe ("Don't") where the rest of the app uses typographic quotes.


---

# Entry and setup flows (/, /sessions, /join, /create, interstitials and their shared chrome)  
_101 findings_


## Global — typography

- **`src/app/layout.tsx:100`** — Outfit is the typeface of the entire design system (neu.tsx exports OUTFIT = "'Outfit', sans-serif", globals.css:51 sets it as the body font, and it is named inline in hundreds of style objects across every entry surface) but it is NEVER LOADED. The <head> links only Playfair Display (ital@1) and Noto Sans Arabic; there is no next/font import, no @font-face and no Google Fonts link for Outfit anywhere in src/ or public/. Every page therefore renders in `system-ui` — SF Pro on Mac, Segoe on Windows. All the tuned 900/800/700 weights, the -0.01em tracking and the optical sizes were designed against a typeface nobody sees. → _Add Outfit via next/font/google (weights 400,500,600,700,800,900, display:'swap') in layout.tsx and expose it as a CSS variable the OUTFIT token reads. This single change will visibly re-set every screen in the audit._  `[consistency]`
- **`src/app/layout.tsx:102`** — 'DM Mono' is named in 34 places (session code fields, quorum readouts, PRE-SESSION chips, the rejoin code badge, the create-page language chip) and is also never loaded — those elements fall back to the generic `monospace`, i.e. Courier on many systems, next to Outfit-intended UI. Meanwhile the conferences half of the app has formally banned monospace: shared.tsx:26-29 states "Monospace is eliminated on the conferences side" and aliases MONO to Outfit. The two halves of the product now disagree about whether a code face exists at all. → _Pick one: either load DM Mono properly and keep it strictly for codes/timers, or extend the conferences rule to sessions and replace `'DM Mono', monospace` with `OUTFIT` + `fontVariantNumeric:'tabular-nums'` + letter-spacing. Do not ship a third state where it is requested but absent._  `[consistency]`
- **`src/app/layout.tsx:102`** — Playfair Display is loaded as `ital@1` only — the italic 400 face and nothing else. MobileGate.tsx:42-44 asks for `'Playfair Display', serif` at fontWeight 700 with NO italic, so the mobile block screen's 28px headline silently falls back to Times/Georgia. It is the only Playfair usage in the app that is not italic. → _Either add `wght@700` to the Playfair request or set MobileGate's headline to italic 400 to match the hero treatment on / and /sessions._  `[polish]`

## Global — page shell

- **`src/app/layout.tsx:110`** — `<link rel="preload" as="image" href="/Conferences.webp" fetchPriority="high">` fires on EVERY route. /join renders /GavellingLogo.png (join/page.tsx:333), /create renders /GavellingLogo.png (create/page.tsx:582), /sessions renders /GavellingSessionsApp.webp — none of them paint Conferences.webp. So the three entry surfaces spend high-priority early bandwidth on an image they never show, while the logo they DO show is unpreloaded and flashes (the very flash the comment above the tag says it is fixing). → _Move the preload into the conferences-area layouts, or make it conditional. At minimum add a matching preload for /GavellingLogo.png in the join and create layouts, which already exist as server components._  `[polish]`

## Global — design system

- **`src/app/join/page.tsx:357`** — The entry flows do not use the design system at all. Verified by grep: nothing under src/app/join, src/app/create, src/app/HomeClient.tsx or src/app/sessions imports from '@/components/neu'. They use a flat card language — `#FAF8F3` fill with a 1px/1.5px/2px `#DDD4C0` or `#C8BAA8` border — while the benchmark surface (manage/[slug]/applications/page.tsx, 113 NEU references) uses `NEU.surface` #F0EBDD with the dual `NEU.out` / `NEU.in` shadow pair. A chair who creates a committee and then opens the organiser dashboard crosses a visible style border. → _Port the four containers that matter on each entry screen (code field, found-committee card, role cards, primary CTA) onto NeuInset / NeuCard / NeuButton. If the flat language is the intended one for sessions, then document that split explicitly — right now it reads as drift, not decision._  `[consistency]`

## Landing (/) — whole page

- **`src/app/conferences/landing-lab/VariantStagefront.tsx:388`** — gavelling.com has NO path to joining a live session. Verified by grep: the string 'join' appears exactly once in the 1344-line landing composition, inside `.join(', ')`. The hero offers only a conference search field and 'Organising one? List it free'. A delegate told "go to gavelling.com, code is 4KJ2P9" lands here, sees a conference directory, and must notice the 'SESSIONS' pill in the floating nav, click through to /sessions, then wait out a 2.3s animation before the code field appears (see the /sessions findings). This is the single largest time-to-first-value defect in the area. → _Add a compact 'Have a session code?' entry to the landing — either a second field beside the hero search or a persistent thin bar — posting straight to /join?code=. It costs one row and removes two navigations plus a 2.3s wait from the most time-critical flow in the product._  `[wasted-effort]`

## Landing (/) — page shell

- **`src/app/page.tsx:107`** — The server-rendered crawlable conference index `<nav>` is emitted AFTER `<StagefrontClient />`, and VariantStagefront ends with `<LabFooter />` (VariantStagefront.tsx:834). So the visual page order is: hero → sections → footer → a bare 13px link list on a #FAF8F3 slab → nothing. Content below the footer reads as a rendering mistake to every human visitor. → _Pass the server-fetched conference list into the composition and render the index just above LabFooter (or style it as a genuine footer directory column), so the footer stays the last element on the page._  `[hierarchy]`

## Landing (/) — circuit in numbers

- **`src/app/conferences/landing-lab/VariantStagefront.tsx:474`** — The three headline statistics are inflated by hardcoded constants — `conferences.length + 100`, `expected_delegates + 20000`, `countries + 30` — and rendered at up to 74px as the section's only content. The inline comment concedes they are "display-only launch inflation". A visitor comparing '100+ conferences on the board' against a directory that lists a dozen will notice, and the whole landing's credibility rides on these three numbers. → _Either drop the offsets and let the real figures grow, or reframe the strip as forward-looking copy that is true ('Delegates expected this season', 'Countries represented') without a fabricated base. Do not present a fabricated integer as a measured one._  `[copy]`

## Landing (/) — hero

- **`src/app/conferences/landing-lab/VariantStagefront.tsx:986`** — HeroSearchBar's typeahead is rendered as `absolute left-0 right-0 top: calc(100% + 8px)` inside the hero, not portaled. The house rule requires every floating layer to go through @/components/Portal at fixed viewport coordinates computed from getBoundingClientRect, with edge flipping — the applications PaymentMenu being the reference. This one has no flip, no clamp and no reposition-on-scroll, and it lives inside a section that carries `height:100svh; overflow` context plus a masked backdrop. → _Port it onto the DatePicker/PaymentMenu pattern: Portal + fixed coords + clamp + flip + reposition on capture-phase scroll and resize._  `[rule-violation]`

## /sessions — hero

- **`src/app/HomeClient.tsx:569`** — The subtitle, the primary CTA and the entire join-code box are set to inline `opacity: 0` and revealed by CSS animations delayed 1.9s, 2.1s and 2.3s (`.text-reveal-3/4/5`). For the first two and a third seconds after load, a delegate on /sessions sees a headline over a video and nothing else — no way to start a committee and no way to join one. There is no `prefers-reduced-motion` override, so motion-sensitive users wait the full 2.3s too, and if the stylesheet or the animation ever fails to apply the inline opacity:0 leaves the CTA permanently invisible. → _Cut the whole ladder to ≤400ms total (0 / 80 / 160ms), drive it from a `.loaded` class added on mount rather than inline opacity:0 so a failure degrades to visible, and add a `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` block that sets opacity:1 with no animation._  `[hierarchy]`
- **`src/app/HomeClient.tsx:699`** — On the page whose entire job is 'get 30 people into a room', the join affordance is the smallest thing in the hero: a 10px uppercase mono label, a `w-44` (176px) input and a `px-5 py-3` button, sitting below a `px-10 py-5 text-xl` START YOUR COMMITTEE button. One chair per session presses the big button; thirty delegates hunt for the small one. → _Give the two paths equal visual weight — a two-up split with the code field at the same scale as the CTA, or make the code field the hero primary and demote 'Start your committee' to a secondary outline button. Widen the input to at least 240px so a 6-character code at `tracking-widest` is not cramped._  `[hierarchy]`
- **`src/app/HomeClient.tsx:624`** — `<video autoPlay muted playsInline>` has no `loop`, no `poster` and no `preload` hint. The hero backdrop therefore shows an empty gradient until the first frame decodes, plays once, then freezes on its last frame for the rest of the visit — while the surrounding gradients are tuned as if a moving image were behind them. Both sources (webm + mp4) are fetched at default priority against the h1 and the fonts. → _Add `loop`, a `poster` frame so the hero is never blank, and `preload="metadata"`. If the video is not meant to loop, freeze deliberately on a designed final frame rather than an arbitrary one._  `[missing-state]`
- **`src/app/HomeClient.tsx:722`** — The JOIN button is `disabled` when `joinCode.trim().length < 4` and styled only with `disabled:opacity-40`. A 40%-opacity forest button next to a live one reads as broken, and nothing anywhere tells the user a code is 6 characters. There is no length hint, no character counter and no inline validation. → _Replace the opacity dim with an explicitly-unavailable treatment (muted fill + `cursor: not-allowed` + `aria-disabled`) and add a 11px hint under the field: 'Six characters, from your chair.' Consider keeping the button enabled and validating on click so the failure is explainable instead of silent._  `[missing-state]`
- **`src/app/HomeClient.tsx:713`** — The hero code input has no `inputMode`, `autoComplete="off"`, `autoCorrect="off"`, `autoCapitalize` or `spellCheck={false}`. On iOS Safari — the device most delegates use — the keyboard will offer autocorrect suggestions and a red squiggle over a six-character nonsense string, and the OS may substitute characters on space/return. → _Add `autoComplete="off" autoCorrect="off" autoCapitalize="characters" spellCheck={false} inputMode="text"` here and on the /join code field (join/page.tsx:350), which has the same gap._  `[polish]`

## /sessions — how it works

- **`src/app/HomeClient.tsx:759`** — The three step descriptions — the only explanation of how the product works — are `opacity-0 group-hover:opacity-100`. On any touch device they never appear at all, so mobile visitors get three numerals (01/02/03) and three two-word titles with no body copy. The hover gate also hides them from anyone scanning without a mouse over the exact column. → _Show the descriptions by default and use hover only for the gold glow accent. If density is the concern, shrink the 7xl step numeral rather than hiding the sentence that explains the product._  `[missing-state]`

## /sessions — feature showcase

- **`src/app/HomeClient.tsx:793`** — The six feature selectors are `<div onClick>` with no `role`, no `tabIndex` and no keyboard handler, so the entire showcase is unreachable by keyboard. Separately, the active pill uses `py-2.5` and the inactive ones `py-2`, so clicking a pill grows it by 4px and shunts every pill beneath it down — the list visibly jitters on each selection. → _Make them `<button type="button">` in a `role="tablist"` with `aria-selected`, and keep the padding constant across states — express selection with the background/border change that is already there._  `[polish]`
- **`src/app/HomeClient.tsx:156`** — The six demo cards are pixel-faithful fakes built from real interactive elements: MotionsCard renders '✓ ACCEPT', '✗ REJECT' and 'EDIT' as real `<button>`s (:156-158), SpeakersCard renders START / NEXT / ADD TIME (:226-228), DocumentsCard mounts a live `<iframe src="/UNSC_RESOLUTION.pdf">` (:274). All of them are focusable and clickable and all of them do nothing — roughly 15 dead buttons plus a scrollable PDF viewer sit in the marketing page's tab order. → _Wrap each demo card in `aria-hidden` with `pointer-events: none` and `tabIndex={-1}` on every control, or replace the cards with static images/SVG. The iframe in particular should become a rendered page image — a browser PDF viewer's own chrome inside a designed card is off-system._  `[polish]`
- **`src/app/HomeClient.tsx:439`** — The carousel container is a fixed `height: '520px'` while every card is `minHeight: '460px'` — a permanent 60px of dead space under whichever card is showing, and a hard clip if any card ever grows past 520px (the absolutely-positioned wrapper has `inset-0`). → _Let the container size to the active card (grid with all cards in cell 1/1, or animate height), or set both numbers from one constant so they cannot drift._  `[density]`
- **`src/app/HomeClient.tsx:385`** — 'Archives' is one of six equal feature pills, and selecting it reveals a card whose only content is a 'Coming Soon' badge, a placeholder icon and three non-functional menu rows. A prospect evaluating the product is invited to click a feature that does not exist, which devalues the five that do. → _Drop Archives from the pill list until it ships, or move it into a single small 'On the roadmap' line beneath the carousel. A feature tab should never resolve to a Coming Soon panel._  `[copy]`

## /sessions — rejoin modal

- **`src/app/HomeClient.tsx:925`** — The auto-opening 'Session in progress' modal prints the chair password in plain text — `Password: <span className="font-black">{rejoinData.chairSuffix}</span>` — in a full-screen dialog that appears unprompted for 18 hours after a session. A chair who opens gavelling.com on the projector to show delegates where to go broadcasts the chair credential to the room. → _Mask it by default with a click-to-reveal or copy-to-clipboard control ('Chair code ••••  Copy'), and never render it until the user asks. The session code above it is the one delegates need and can stay visible._  `[polish]`
- **`src/app/HomeClient.tsx:870`** — The rejoin prompt is a blocking `fixed inset-0 bg-black/40 backdrop-blur-sm` modal with no Escape handler and no click-outside dismissal, shown automatically on every visit to /sessions for 18 hours. A user who wants to read the marketing page, or a delegate on a shared laptop, must first deal with someone else's session. Its only exits are two equally-weighted buttons. → _Demote it to a dismissible card docked bottom-right (or a slim bar under the nav) — it is a convenience, not a decision that must be made before the page can be used. Add Escape and backdrop-click dismissal regardless._  `[hierarchy]`
- **`src/app/HomeClient.tsx:873`** — The modal is `width: '320px'` with no `maxWidth: '100%'` and no horizontal padding on the fixed overlay. On a 320px-wide phone it fills the viewport edge to edge with zero margin; the committee title (`text-lg font-black`) plus a 56px logo leaves ~200px for names like 'GA First Committee (Disarmament)'. → _Change to `width: '100%', maxWidth: 340` and give the overlay `px-4`. Apply `committeeDisplayName()` to the title so a long name becomes 'DISEC' with the full name small beneath (see the acronym finding)._  `[responsive]`
- **`src/app/HomeClient.tsx:12`** — `getCommitteeAcronym()` is a hand-rolled 15-branch string matcher that returns 'UNGA' for anything it does not recognise (:27), and REJOIN_LOGOS then paints /logos/un.svg. A rejoin card for 'Crisis Committee', 'FIFA Congress', 'House of Commons' or any custom committee shows the United Nations emblem. The app already has `deriveCommitteeAcronym` + `matchPresetEmblemEntry` + `PRESET_LOGOS` in src/lib/presetNames.ts, which handle all 37 presets and fall back to a monogram. → _Delete getCommitteeAcronym and REJOIN_LOGOS; use `PRESET_LOGOS[name]` with `deriveCommitteeAcronym` for the monogram fallback, exactly as CommitteeIdentityBadge does._  `[consistency]`

## /sessions — account-deleted toast

- **`src/app/HomeClient.tsx:600`** — The 'Your account has been deleted.' notice is `fixed top-4 left-1/2 -translate-x-1/2 z-[100]` — the exact position occupied by SiteNav's floating pill nav (`fixed top-0 left-1/2 -translate-x-1/2 z-40 h-[72px]`, SiteNav.tsx:99). The toast lands squarely on top of the nav links. It also never auto-dismisses, and its only control is a bare 'DISMISS' text label with no hit padding. → _Move it below the 72px nav band (`top-20`) or dock it bottom-centre, auto-dismiss after ~6s, and give the dismiss control a 32px square tap target._  `[polish]`
- **`src/app/HomeClient.tsx:606`** — 'Your account has been deleted.' and its 'DISMISS' label are hardcoded English on a page where every other string goes through `t()`. A Spanish or Arabic user gets one English sentence in the middle of a translated page — and it is the sentence confirming an irreversible action. → _Add `account_deleted_notice` and `dismiss` keys to translations.ts and route both strings through `t()`._  `[copy]`

## /sessions — footer

- **`src/app/HomeClient.tsx:849`** — The LinkedIn icon is a `<span>` with `color: '#C8BFB0'`, `cursor: 'default'` and `aria-label="LinkedIn (coming soon)"` — a permanently greyed-out social icon that reads as a broken link. The identical dead span is duplicated in LabFooter (landing-lab/shared.tsx), so it appears on both / and /sessions. → _Remove it from both footers until the profile exists. An absent icon costs nothing; a dead one costs trust._  `[polish]`
- **`src/app/HomeClient.tsx:856`** — Two near-identical footers exist with divergent content: HomeClient's (:820-861) uses the sessions logo, a translated `t('home_footer_copy')` and a Privacy Policy link; LabFooter (landing-lab/shared.tsx) uses the conferences logo, a hardcoded English '© {year} Gavelling. Built for the MUN community.' and no privacy link of its own. Same layout grid, same Instagram SVG, same dead LinkedIn span, copy-pasted. → _Extract one `<SiteFooter variant="sessions" | "conferences">` component. Both currently drift on translation coverage and link sets._  `[consistency]`

## /join — code entry

- **`src/app/join/page.tsx:218`** — The code field is unforgiving of exactly the input a room of 30 people will produce. `handleCodeChange` does only `val.toUpperCase().slice(0, 20)` — no trim, no whitespace strip, no dash handling. A pasted code with a trailing space, a code typed as '4KJ 2P9', or the full chair code 'ABC123-4821' that the placeholder itself advertises all fail with the same flat 'Committee not found. Check the code and try again.' The mount-time lookup (:78) does not trim either. → _Normalise on entry: `val.replace(/\s+/g,'').toUpperCase()`, split on the first '-' and use the head for lookup while pre-filling the chair password from the tail. Also fold O→0 / I→1 / L→1 before lookup so a code read off a projector survives transcription._  `[wasted-effort]`
- **`src/app/join/page.tsx:369`** — 'Committee not found. Check the code and try again.' names the problem but not the recovery. It does not say how long a code is, that codes are case-insensitive, that a chair code has a dash, or what to do if the chair has not started the session yet — all of which are the actual causes when 30 people are typing at once. → _Rewrite to give the next action: 'No session with that code. Codes are 6 characters (letters and numbers) — check with your chair, or ask them to start the session.' Keep the placeholder and the error consistent about the accepted format._  `[copy]`
- **`src/app/join/page.tsx:363`** — While looking up, the only feedback is a 20px spinner tucked at `right-4` inside the input, and the debounce is 350ms on top of the network round-trip. Below it, the three 160px role cards and the disabled JOIN button are already rendered, so the page looks finished and unresponsive rather than busy. There is no skeleton for the committee card that is about to appear, so the layout jumps ~64px when it resolves. → _Reserve the committee-card slot with a skeleton the moment `lookingUp` is true, so the spinner has somewhere meaningful to live and the page does not shift when the result lands._  `[missing-state]`
- **`src/app/join/page.tsx:370`** — `suffixError` is declared (:48), rendered with its own bespoke red-panel styling (:370-372) and cleared in one place (:223) — but it is never set to a non-empty value anywhere in the file. It is a dead error surface carrying a third distinct error treatment (red panel with border and background) alongside the plain red text at :369 and the red bordered panels at :472/:482. → _Delete `suffixError` and its block, then unify the remaining error presentations into one `<FieldError>` treatment. Three visual grammars for 'something is wrong' on one 512px-wide page is two too many._  `[polish]`

## /join — role choice

- **`src/app/join/page.tsx:514`** — `const enabled = true;` is hardcoded inside the role-card map, then five properties branch on it — `opacity: enabled ? 1 : 0.35`, `cursor: enabled ? 'pointer' : 'not-allowed'`, `disabled={!enabled}`, and a guard inside onClick. Roughly ten lines of unreachable disabled-state styling that hides the fact that no role can ever be unavailable. → _Delete the flag and its branches. If a role genuinely can be unavailable (e.g. advisor on an ended session), drive it from real state and give it a real explanatory tooltip rather than a silent 35% dim._  `[polish]`
- **`src/app/join/page.tsx:317`** — `const tabs` is declared with labels 'Delegate' / 'Re-join as Chair' / 'Faculty Advisor' and emoji icons 🌐 🪑 👁️, and is never rendered — the page uses `roleCards` (:506) with the translated labels 'DELEGATE' / 'CHAIR' / 'FACULTY ADVISOR' instead. Two competing label sets for the same three roles live in one file, and the dead one is the more descriptive. → _Delete `tabs`. If 'Re-join as Chair' is the clearer label — it is, because it tells a first-time chair they are in the wrong place — move that wording into `join_role_chair` and drop the dead array._  `[polish]`
- **`src/app/join/page.tsx:512`** — The role cards are `grid grid-cols-3 gap-4` with a hard `height: '160px'` and no responsive prefix. Inside FitToScreen the page's effective CSS width is `window.innerWidth / scale`, which on a 390px phone is roughly 379px — so each card is about 105px wide and must hold 'FACULTY ADVISOR' at `text-sm font-black` plus a two-line description at `text-xs`, all centred in 105px. That is the primary role selector on the app's most mobile-critical screen. → _Use `grid-cols-1 sm:grid-cols-3` with a compact horizontal card layout below sm (icon left, label + description right, ~64px tall). The three cards then read cleanly at any width and the page gets ~200px shorter, which also improves the FitToScreen scale._  `[responsive]`
- **`src/app/join/page.tsx:505`** — The three 160px role cards render before a code has even been entered, so the first thing on the page after the code field is 480px of forest cards asking a question that cannot yet be answered, followed by a disabled JOIN button. The committee-found card and the fields that actually depend on the role appear later, pushing the role choice further from its consequence. → _Hide the role cards until `foundCommittee` resolves (they already have a `checkingConference` suppression, so the mechanism exists), and show a single quiet line in their place: 'Enter your code to continue.' The page then reveals exactly one decision at a time._  `[hierarchy]`
- **`src/lib/translations.ts:81`** — `join_role_chair_desc: 'Re-join a chair panel committee session'` is MUN-internal jargon squared — 'chair panel' is not a term delegates or new chairs use, and 'Re-join' will read as an error to a co-chair joining for the first time. `join_role_advisor_desc: 'Observer view for advisors'` is circular (it defines 'advisor' with 'advisors'). → _Rewrite for a first-timer: chair → 'Run the committee from the dais'; advisor → 'Watch the room — read-only, no controls'; delegate → 'Represent a country on the floor'. These are the three sentences that decide whether 30 nervous people pick correctly._  `[copy]`

## /join — delegate step

- **`src/app/join/page.tsx:550`** — Country selection is a bare native `<select>` — no flag, no search, no custom styling beyond a border, and `focus:outline-none` strips even the OS focus ring. Every other country picker in the product is a flagged typeahead (create/page.tsx:701 shows flag + name + Enter hint; ConferenceRosterPicker does the same). For a UN General Assembly committee this is a native 190-item scroll wheel on a phone, with no way to type 'ger' to reach Germany. → _Reuse the create-page typeahead: search input, flag + localised name rows, keyboard-first, first match on Enter. It is already written and already matches the design system._  `[consistency]`
- **`src/app/join/page.tsx:557`** — If a chair created the committee with no delegations (perfectly reachable — `canProceed` in create/page.tsx:505 only requires name + topic), the select renders with just the placeholder, the found-card says '0 delegates registered', and the JOIN button stays permanently disabled with no explanation. There is also no indication of which delegations are already claimed, so two delegates can both pick France with nothing on screen suggesting otherwise. → _Add a zero-state inside the field's slot: 'Your chair has not added delegations yet — ask them to add yours.' And mark already-joined delegations in the list (dimmed + 'in the room') so duplicates are a visible choice rather than an invisible accident._  `[missing-state]`

## /join — chair step

- **`src/app/join/page.tsx:659`** — Four strings in the chair path are hardcoded English on an otherwise fully translated page: the 'Chair Password' label (:659), the 'Enter password…' placeholder (:665), 'Incorrect password. Ask your head chair.' (:283) and 'This name is already in an active session. Select a different name to join as a view-only co-chair.' (:650). A French or Arabic chair hits English at exactly the step that blocks them. → _Add `join_chair_password_label`, `join_chair_password_placeholder`, `join_chair_password_wrong` and `join_chair_name_taken` to translations.ts (all four locales) and route them through `t()`._  `[copy]`
- **`src/app/join/page.tsx:659`** — The credential is called three different things in three places the same user sees within a minute: 'Chair Password' on this field, 'chair code' in Settings → Access and in the tutorial ('Co-chairs need the longer chair code instead'), and 'ABC123-4821' in the /join placeholder implying it is part of the session code. The join-page error then says 'Ask your head chair' while the tutorial says it lives in Settings. → _Pick one noun — 'chair code' — and use it in the field label, the placeholder, the error, Settings → Access and the tutorial. Change the error to name the location: 'Wrong chair code. Your head chair can find it in Settings → Access.'_  `[copy]`
- **`src/app/join/page.tsx:620`** — The head/co-chair picker is shown to every chair including the very first one, and its explanatory notes are pure insider jargon: 'You'll take the gavel: any current head chair becomes view-only' / 'You'll join view-only; you can take the gavel later from the gavel chip at the top-right of the session.' A chair who has never used Gavelling cannot know what a gavel chip is, and 'view-only' sounds like a downgrade rather than a normal co-chair mode. → _Default it: if `chairNames.length === 0` (or nobody holds the gavel) skip the picker entirely and join as head. Otherwise label the options by consequence — 'Run the committee (takes control from {currentHead})' vs 'Assist {currentHead} (you can take over any time)' — and name the current holder rather than referring to an unseen chip._  `[copy]`
- **`src/app/join/page.tsx:644`** — The 'name already active' warning says 'Select a different name to join as a view-only co-chair' — but the head/co picker directly above it is a separate control the user has already set, possibly to 'Head chair'. The warning implies the name choice determines the role, contradicting the picker. → _Say only what is true: 'Another device is already signed in as {name}. Pick a different name so both of you stay signed in.' Leave the role to the role picker._  `[copy]`
- **`src/app/join/page.tsx:278`** — Chair-path validation errors render in the wrong place. `setError(t('join_select_name'))` ('Please select or enter your chair name.') is displayed by the `{error && ...}` block at :369 — directly beneath the CODE input, potentially 400px above the chair-name field it refers to and below the fold once the role cards and name list are expanded. → _Give each field its own error slot (the password field already does this correctly at :669) and reserve the code-field error strictly for lookup failures._  `[hierarchy]`
- **`src/app/join/page.tsx:577`** — The existing-chair-name list renders `foundCommittee.chairNames.map(...)` unbounded — one full-width 42px button per name ever used, plus a '+ New name' button, plus the role picker, plus the password field. A committee that has cycled through eight chair names produces ~380px of stacked identical buttons before the password field, and inside FitToScreen that extra height scales the whole page down for everyone. → _Cap the list at 4 with a 'Show all ({n})' expander, or switch to a compact wrap of name chips. Sort most-recent-first so the likely answer is first._  `[density]`

## /join — join button

- **`src/app/join/page.tsx:696`** — The primary button's colours are applied through a `ref` callback that imperatively writes `el.style.backgroundColor` and `el.style.color` on every render, while hover is handled by separate onMouseEnter/onMouseLeave handlers writing the same property. Any re-render while hovering (which the debounced lookup, presence channel and allocation effect all trigger) re-runs the ref and stamps the base colour back over the hover colour — the hover state flickers off under the cursor. → _Compute `disabled` once and put both colours in the `style` prop with a `hovered` state variable, like every other button on the page. Nothing here needs a ref._  `[polish]`
- **`src/app/join/page.tsx:675`** — The JOIN button has no pending state. In the chair-head path it fires `updateCommitteeHeadChairInDB(...).finally(goChair)` (:290) and in the conference path `addChairName(...)` then a push (:251-252) — during the write plus the Next.js route transition the button looks completely inert, so a nervous user taps it repeatedly. → _Add a `joining` state that disables the button and swaps the label to a spinner + 'Joining…'. This is the single most-tapped control in the product._  `[missing-state]`
- **`src/app/join/page.tsx:692`** — `transition-all` on the primary button and on all three role cards (:521) animates every changing property including background-color, transform, box-shadow, border-color and layout-affecting values, which is exactly the pattern the polish guidance rules out. The role cards animate a `scale(1.04)` and a shadow change through `transition-all` with no duration or easing specified, so they inherit Tailwind's 150ms linear rather than the house `cubic-bezier(0.22,1,0.36,1)`. → _Replace with explicit property lists at the house easing, e.g. `transition: 'background-color 200ms cubic-bezier(0.22,1,0.36,1), transform 200ms cubic-bezier(0.22,1,0.36,1), box-shadow 260ms cubic-bezier(0.22,1,0.36,1)'`. Same fix applies to the 10 `transition-all` occurrences in create/page.tsx._  `[polish]`

## /join — whole page

- **`src/app/join/page.tsx:521`** — Every interactive element in the join flow carries `focus:outline-none` with no replacement ring: the role cards (:521), the chair-name buttons (:583, :595), the head/co picker (:626), the country `<select>` (:553) and the JOIN button (:692). Only the code input compensates, and it does so with a JS onFocus border-colour swap (:358) rather than a ring. A keyboard or switch user has no visible focus anywhere on the page that gets thirty people into a room. → _Add a shared `focus-visible` treatment — `outline: 2px solid #B6871F; outline-offset: 2px` — to all of them. If the outline conflicts with a card's radius, use `box-shadow: 0 0 0 3px rgba(182,135,31,0.45)` instead._  `[contrast]`
- **`src/app/join/page.tsx:344`** — Five different border radii on one 512px column: code input `rounded-2xl` (16px), found-committee card and conference badge and country select and chair-name buttons `rounded-xl` (12px), role cards `borderRadius: '20px'`, JOIN button `rounded-2xl` (16px), conference sign-in panel `borderRadius: 12`. Three different border widths in the same stack: 2px (code input, role cards), 1.5px (found card, country select), 1px (conference badge, sign-in panel, chair-name buttons). → _Fix two values for the page — radius 16px for inputs/cards and 20px for the primary CTA, hairline 1px everywhere except the focused/active state — and apply them from constants rather than per-element literals._  `[consistency]`
- **`src/app/join/page.tsx:383`** — The found-committee card renders `{foundCommittee.name}` raw inside a `truncate` span. The house rule requires a long committee name to display as its ACRONYM with the full name small beneath, via `committeeDisplayName()` / `deriveCommitteeAcronym()`, and says it applies 'everywhere committees render'. 'Disarmament and International Security Committee' here becomes 'Disarmament and Internation…' inside a ~380px card, next to the topic appended on the same line with a '·'. → _Render `deriveCommitteeAcronym(name)` as the primary label with `committeeDisplayName` logic deciding whether to show the full name on a second small line — the exact treatment CommitteeIdentityBadge already implements. Move the topic to its own line rather than appending it to a truncating name._  `[rule-violation]`
- **`src/app/join/page.tsx:331`** — There is no language switcher anywhere on /join. The nav holds only the logo and 'Chair? Create Committee →'. Every other entry surface has one (SiteNav on /sessions, a bespoke globe menu on /create), and the whole page is translated into four locales — but a delegate who lands here in the wrong language has no way to change it without leaving the flow. → _Add the SiteNav globe control (or reuse the /create implementation) to the join nav. This is the page most likely to be opened by someone who has never used the site and never set a preference._  `[wasted-effort]`
- **`src/app/join/page.tsx:711`** — The same 'create a committee instead' CTA appears twice on a short page — in the nav as 'Chair? Create Committee →' (:335) and again at the bottom as 'Are you a chair? Create a committee instead' (:711-716). Both leave the flow. Meanwhile there is now a CHAIR role card in the middle of the page, so 'Are you a chair?' at the bottom actively contradicts it. → _Keep the nav link only, and reword it so it does not compete with the CHAIR role card: 'Start a new committee →'._  `[wasted-effort]`
- **`src/app/join/page.tsx:327`** — The page is wrapped in `FitToScreen fitContent`, i.e. `position: fixed; inset: 0; overflow: hidden` with a uniform scale of `window.innerHeight / max(820, contentHeight)`. It never scrolls — it shrinks. In the tallest reachable state (code + found card + conference badge + sign-in panel + 3 role cards + chair name list + role picker + password + button) content exceeds ~1200px, giving a scale near 0.70 on a 844px-tall phone: a 14px label renders at ~9.8px physical and the 11px role-picker note at ~7.7px. This is the app's most mobile-critical screen. → _Drop FitToScreen on /join and let the page scroll normally with a `max-w-lg` column. Nothing on this page is a fixed cockpit that must fit one viewport; it is a form._  `[responsive]`

## /join — conference session

- **`src/app/join/page.tsx:392`** — A conference-linked code stacks up to four separate status panels above the button — the forest CONFERENCE SESSION badge (:395), a spinner strip, the green VERIFIED ALLOCATION panel (:431), and one of the sign-in / not-associated panels (:452, :471) — each with its own fill, border colour and radius. The allocation panel alone prints three lines including 'You will join as {country}. This cannot be changed.' → _Collapse the badge and the allocation into one card: conference name + committee on top, then a single row 'Joining as 🇫🇷 France · locked by your organiser'. Reserve a separate panel only for the error states._  `[density]`
- **`src/app/join/page.tsx:705`** — The button reads `JOIN AS ${allocatedCountry.name.toUpperCase()} →`. Country names are already uppercased by the styling context in most places; forcing `.toUpperCase()` in JS produces 'JOIN AS UNITED ARAB EMIRATES →' at `text-base font-black` with `letterSpacing: '0.06em'` in a 512px column — it will wrap or overflow for the long names, and it duplicates information already stated verbatim two panels above ('You will join as {name}'). → _Use the CSS `textTransform: 'uppercase'` rather than mutating the string, and shorten to 'JOIN SESSION →' since the allocation is already confirmed above. If keeping the name, add `truncate` and a max width._  `[copy]`

## /create — type selection

- **`src/app/create/page.tsx:237`** — The first screen a chair sees is a full-viewport chooser with three 260×420px video cards, of which two are permanently unavailable ('Coming Soon', 'Coming H2 2026') and non-interactive. There is exactly one possible action — press START on the middle card — so this screen adds a click, a full page of layout and three autoplaying `<video>` elements (CardVideo, :214) to the critical path with zero decision value. → _Skip straight to the build screen and surface 'Regular Debate / Crisis — coming soon' as a one-line note under the committee-name field. If the roadmap teaser is worth keeping, make it a small strip, not a gate._  `[wasted-effort]`
- **`src/app/create/page.tsx:354`** — The card row is `height: '460px'` with `overflow-x-auto` on mobile, while the cards are 420px tall and hover applies `transform: scale(1.08) translateY(-8px)` from `transformOrigin: 'bottom center'` — 420×1.08 = 453.6px plus the 8px lift = 461.6px, which exceeds the 460px row. `overflow-x: auto` also makes overflow-y a scroll container, so the top edge of the hovered card and its 80px-blur shadow are clipped on every hover below md. → _Raise the row to 500px (or add ~40px top padding), and use `overflow-x: auto; overflow-y: visible` is not valid — instead give the scroller vertical padding so the scaled card has room inside the clip._  `[polish]`
- **`src/app/create/page.tsx:341`** — `onMouseLeave={() => setHovered('mun')}` means moving the cursor off any card snaps the MUN card back to `scale(1.08)` while the card you just left drops to `scale(1)` — two simultaneous 180ms transforms in opposite directions on every mouse-out. Hovering the two dead cards is also fully rewarded with a lift and a deep shadow even though they cannot be clicked (`cursor: 'default'`), which reads as an invitation. → _Leave MUN visually primary at rest and do not re-trigger its scale on mouse-out; suppress the lift/shadow entirely on the two unavailable cards and give them a genuinely inert treatment (reduced saturation, no transform)._  `[polish]`

## /create — build screen

- **`src/app/create/page.tsx:639`** — The whole build screen is desktop-only with no breakpoints and no MobileGate (MobileGate is mounted only on /chair/[code]). `grid grid-cols-3` for name/chair/topic and `grid grid-cols-2` for the search/delegates columns are unconditional. Inside FitToScreen a 390px phone yields an effective width around 379px, so the three top fields become ~110px each — narrower than their own `px-4` padding plus placeholder — and the two body columns become ~175px each. → _Add `grid-cols-1 md:grid-cols-3` and `grid-cols-1 lg:grid-cols-2`, drop FitToScreen below md so the page can scroll, and stack search-above-selected on narrow widths. Alternatively wrap /create in MobileGate too — but silently rendering an unusable layout is the worst of the three options._  `[responsive]`
- **`src/app/create/page.tsx:653`** — TOPIC is a hard requirement — `canProceed = committeeName.trim() && topic.trim()` (:505) — so a chair cannot open a room until they have typed a debate topic, even though the topic can be changed at any time from inside the session and is not needed to run roll call. It is one of only three fields on the critical path and the only one that is genuinely optional in practice (chair name is already marked optional and defaults to 'Chair'). → _Make topic optional with a placeholder default ('Topic to be announced') so the create path is one field long. Prompt for the real topic inside the session at the moment it first matters._  `[wasted-effort]`
- **`src/app/create/page.tsx:493`** — Creation ends with `router.push('/chair/{code}?chairName=...')` and nothing else. The 4-digit chair code is generated silently (committeeService.ts:157), written to localStorage, and never shown — so a head chair who needs to get a co-chair into the room must discover Settings → Access on their own. There is no confirmation moment, no 'share this code' screen, and no acknowledgement that two different codes now exist. → _Insert a short hand-off step (or a first-run card on the chair page): the session code big with a Copy button and 'Delegates enter this at gavelling.com', and beneath it the chair code with 'Co-chairs need this one too'. This is the highest-leverage 30 seconds in the whole product._  `[missing-state]`
- **`src/app/create/page.tsx:495`** — Both failure paths use a native browser `alert()` — 'Failed to create committee. Please try again.' and 'Something went wrong. Please try again.' A system dialog in the middle of a carefully art-directed forest/ivory flow, with copy that names no cause and offers no alternative (the underlying call has a 15-second timeout, so 'try again' may just repeat the wait). → _Replace with the in-page error treatment used elsewhere and say something actionable: 'Could not reach Gavelling — check your connection and try again.' Keep the entered committee data intact, which alert() already does but does not communicate._  `[polish]`
- **`src/app/create/page.tsx:848`** — During creation the button shows `create_creating` ('CREATING...') with no spinner and no progress, and `createCommittee` races a 15-second timeout (committeeService.ts:154). A chair can stare at a static text label for fifteen seconds with no indication that anything is happening or that it may fail. → _Add an inline spinner beside the label and, past ~4s, a second line: 'Still working — this can take a moment on a slow connection.'_  `[missing-state]`
- **`src/app/create/page.tsx:752`** — 'AUTO-MATCH & ADD →' is styled as a forest/gold `px-6 py-4 font-black uppercase tracking-widest` pill sitting in a 56px row that deliberately aligns with 'START SESSION →' in the right column. A minor list-parsing utility is given the same visual weight as the one irreversible primary action on the page, and both are the same colour. → _Demote Auto-Match to a secondary outline button (forest text on transparent with a 1px border) so START SESSION is the only filled forest control on the screen. Better still, parse the pasted list on blur and drop the button entirely._  `[hierarchy]`
- **`src/app/create/page.tsx:746`** — Pasting a delegation list takes three actions: paste → press AUTO-MATCH → confirm in a modal. The modal (:878) exists to let the user fix unmatched custom names, but for the common case — a clean list of country names where every row matches — it is a pure extra confirmation. → _Parse on paste and add the confident matches straight to the list; only open the review modal when at least one token failed to match a country, and title it for that case ('3 names we could not match')._  `[wasted-effort]`
- **`src/app/create/page.tsx:727`** — 'Quick Bundles' (P5, G7, BRICS+, G20, EU, NATO, ASEAN, Arab League) overlaps heavily with the committee presets in the name field — choosing 'NATO' or 'European Union' or 'G20' as the committee already populates exactly the same member list. The same eight logos appear twice on one screen with two different mental models, and pressing a bundle after choosing a preset silently merges two rosters. → _Cut the bundles that duplicate a preset (NATO, EU, G20, ASEAN, Arab League) and keep only the ones that are genuinely additive blocs inside another committee (P5, G7, BRICS+). Relabel the group 'Add a bloc' so the distinction from 'pick a committee' is explicit._  `[consistency]`
- **`src/app/create/page.tsx:775`** — The Selected Delegates panel has no search and no per-bundle removal. Choosing the UN General Assembly preset drops ~190 rows into an un-virtualised scroll list whose only bulk control is 'CLEAR ALL' (:771) — which is unconfirmed and irreversible. Removing one country from 190 means scrolling to find it and hovering the row to reveal an invisible ✕. → _Add a filter field above the list once it exceeds ~15 rows, make 'Clear all' confirm (or offer an undo toast), and show the source of each bulk add so a mis-pressed bundle can be removed in one action._  `[missing-state]`
- **`src/app/create/page.tsx:837`** — The remove ✕ and the rename pencil are both `opacity-0 group-hover:opacity-100`. On any touch device (and for anyone navigating by keyboard) there is no way to remove or rename a delegation — the controls exist but are permanently invisible. The remove ✕ is also a bare 14px glyph with no padding, giving roughly a 14×14px hit area on desktop. → _Keep both controls at ~35% opacity at rest and 100% on hover/focus-within rather than 0, and pad them to a 32px square hit target. Add `focus-visible` so keyboard users can reach them._  `[polish]`
- **`src/app/create/page.tsx:824`** — The observer toggle is a 15px Megaphone icon with only a `title` attribute ('Mark as observer'), shown on every one of up to 190 rows. There is no legend, no explanation of what an observer can or cannot do, and a megaphone is a poor metaphor for a non-voting seat — a megaphone connotes more voice, not less. The toggled state is a colour change from #9A8A78 to #B6871F plus a small OBSERVER chip. → _Swap to an eye or a mic-off glyph, add a one-line hint under the SELECTED DELEGATES label ('Observers can speak but not vote'), and enlarge the hit target to 32px. Consider hiding the toggle behind a per-row menu since it is a minority action on a list this long._  `[copy]`
- **`src/app/create/page.tsx:696`** — The search field shows the top match twice simultaneously: an inline chip inside the field ('↵ {name}', :697) and the first dropdown row highlighted with 'Enter ↵' on its right (:707). Two Enter hints for one action, ~200px apart, on the same interaction. → _Keep the highlighted first dropdown row with its Enter affordance and delete the in-field chip — it also steals horizontal space from the query on narrow widths._  `[density]`
- **`src/app/create/page.tsx:779`** — The empty-state hint reads 'Search countries or use Quick Bundles above to get started' — but the empty state lives in the RIGHT column and Quick Bundles are in the LEFT column, not above. Similarly the disabled button label 'ENTER COMMITTEE NAME & TOPIC ABOVE' (create_enter_details) points 'above' from the bottom of the right column to fields at the top of a different grid. → _Drop the directional words: 'Add delegations from the search or a bloc on the left' and 'Name your committee and topic to continue'. Directional copy breaks the moment the layout stacks on a narrow screen anyway._  `[copy]`
- **`src/app/create/page.tsx:650`** — Three different input fills and two different hairline colours coexist on one screen: the three top fields are `bg-white/70 border-[#C8BAA8]`, the country search is `bg-[#FAF8F3] border-[#DDD4C0]`, the paste textarea is `bg-[#FAF8F3] border-[#DDD4C0]`, and the search dropdown is `border-2 border-[#C8BAA8]`. `#C8BAA8` and `#DDD4C0` are doing the same job three inches apart, and pure white at 70% opacity appears nowhere else in the palette. → _Standardise every field on `#FAF8F3` with a 1px `#DDD4C0` hairline (or NEU.base + NEU.inSm if the neumorphic system is adopted) and delete `#C8BAA8` and `bg-white/70` from this file._  `[consistency]`
- **`src/app/create/page.tsx:650`** — Focus is expressed as `focus:ring-2 focus:ring-[#1B3828]/10` — a forest ring at 10% opacity over #FAF8F3, which is effectively invisible (roughly a 1.05:1 luminance step). Paired with `focus:outline-none`, keyboard users get a barely-perceptible border-colour change and nothing else across all four fields on the page. → _Raise to `focus:ring-2 focus:ring-[#B6871F]/60` with `ring-offset-2`, or drop the ring and use a solid 2px `#1B3828` border on focus. 10% opacity is not a focus indicator._  `[contrast]`
- **`src/app/create/page.tsx:188`** — Three dropdowns on this page are plain `absolute` layers, none portaled: the committee-name preset list (`absolute top-full left-0 right-0 z-30`, :188), the country search results (`absolute top-full ... z-20`, :701) and the nav language menu (`absolute right-0 top-full`, :599). All three sit inside `overflow-hidden` ancestors (the page root at :568 and the body column at :628) — the exact configuration the house rule forbids — and none of them clamps to the viewport or flips near an edge. → _Port all three onto the PaymentMenu/DatePicker pattern. Note the Portal caveat: Portal targets `#fit-root`, which is transformed on this page, so either give Portal an opt-out to document.body or lift /create out of FitToScreen first._  `[rule-violation]`

## /create — nav

- **`src/app/create/page.tsx:585`** — /create re-implements the language switcher from scratch (:585-619) instead of using SiteNav's. The two diverge: this one labels the current language in `'DM Mono' 11px 700`, SiteNav uses `Outfit 12px 700`; this one uses a `fixed inset-0 z-40` invisible click-catcher, SiteNav uses a document mousedown listener; this one adds a ✓ in `#B6871F` and a per-row `DM Mono` language code, SiteNav shows only the ✓. Meanwhile /join has no switcher at all. → _Extract SiteNav's globe control into a `<LanguageMenu>` and use it on /create, /join and SiteNav. Three implementations of one control across three consecutive screens in the same flow is the definition of drift._  `[consistency]`
- **`src/app/create/page.tsx:581`** — The logo destination differs on every entry surface: /create's logo → `/sessions` (:581), /join's logo → `/sessions` (join:332), SiteNav's logo → `/` (SiteNav:162). So the sessions-branded 'GAVELLING SESSIONS APP' mark in SiteNav navigates away from the sessions product to the conferences landing, and SiteNav's CONFERENCES nav link points to the same `/` — two adjacent controls with one destination. → _Make the logo always return to the current product's home (`/sessions` when the sessions logo is showing, `/` when the conferences lockup is), and let the CONFERENCES nav item be the only route to `/`._  `[consistency]`

## MobileGate

- **`src/components/MobileGate.tsx:65`** — 'OPEN ON DESKTOP' is a `<div>` styled exactly like the app's primary button — forest fill, ivory text, 600 weight, 0.08em tracking, 10×20px padding, 8px radius — but it is not a button, not a link, and does nothing. On the one screen whose entire purpose is to redirect the user, the most prominent element is a decoy. → _Either make it a real action (a 'Copy this link' button, or a mailto/share sheet that sends the URL to the user's own address so they can open it on a laptop) or restyle it as a flat label so it stops promising a click._  `[polish]`
- **`src/components/MobileGate.tsx:80`** — '(or just rotate your phone, we're not judging)' promises a workaround that cannot work: the gate is decided purely by `navigator.userAgent` (:13), not by viewport size, so rotating changes nothing and the user is left assuming the site is broken. The headline 'Don't you wanna try this on a computer instead?' is also phrased as a question the user cannot answer no to. → _Delete the rotate line. Make the headline state the fact rather than ask ('The chair's dashboard needs a bigger screen'), and add what the user can actually do next — the session code and a link to the delegate view, which does work on a phone._  `[copy]`
- **`src/components/MobileGate.tsx:30`** — The root sets `fontFamily: 'system-ui, sans-serif'` explicitly, opting out of the app's own stack, while the headline asks for Playfair and the body for nothing in particular. Combined with a raw 48px 🖥️ emoji as the hero graphic (:38) on a page whose sibling surfaces use lucide glyphs, gradient discs and a custom gavel SVG, this screen looks like it came from a different product. → _Drop the system-ui override, replace the emoji with the gavel mark or a lucide Monitor glyph in a NeuIconDisc, and use the NEU card treatment. This is a screen real users hit; it should not be the least designed one in the app._  `[consistency]`

## DemoGate

- **`src/components/DemoGate.tsx:11`** — `DEMO_GATE_ENABLED = false`, so all 233 lines — the gate UI, the shake keyframes, the cookie/localStorage helpers, the code normaliser, the body-scroll lock — ship in the client bundle of every route via layout.tsx:116 and render nothing. The component is still mounted globally and still runs a `usePathname()` subscription on every navigation. → _Delete the component and its layout mount, and keep the file only if a launch gate is genuinely coming back (in which case gate the import behind an env var so it tree-shakes out)._  `[polish]`

## CreditsWelcomeModal

- **`src/components/CreditsWelcomeModal.tsx:80`** — `onClick={onClose}` on the full-screen backdrop, and `onClose` writes `welcome_token_seen: true` to the profile (CreditsWelcomeGate.tsx:56-58). A single stray click anywhere outside the card permanently destroys the one-time onboarding — the modal can never be shown again. There is no Escape handler at all, and no `role="dialog"`/`aria-modal`/focus trap. → _Only persist the seen flag from the explicit dismissals ('×' and 'Maybe later') and the CTA; make backdrop clicks close without persisting. Add `role="dialog" aria-modal="true"`, an Escape handler and initial focus on the close button._  `[polish]`
- **`src/components/CreditsWelcomeModal.tsx:33`** — The whole design rests on a spotlight hole punched over `[data-credits-chip]`, but that chip lives inside SiteNav's `hidden md:flex` desktop actions row (SiteNav.tsx:278-281). Below the md breakpoint the element measures 0×0, `setHole(null)` fires, the pulsing ring never renders — and the modal still says 'The first credit is on us' next to a chip the user cannot see. The `measure()` effect also listens only to `resize`, never `scroll`, and the modal does not lock body scroll, so scrolling the page behind it leaves the hole stranded over empty space while the real chip moves away. → _When `hole` is null, render an inline credits chip inside the modal card so the object being introduced is always visible. Add a capture-phase `scroll` listener alongside `resize`, and lock `document.body.overflow` while the modal is open._  `[missing-state]`
- **`src/components/CreditsWelcomeModal.tsx:110`** — The card uses `borderRadius: 26` while NeuCard's system radius is 22 and NeuInset's is 16; the well inside uses 18. Its row dividers are `borderTop: '1px solid #DDD4C0'` — a hardcoded hex, because NEU exports no hairline token at all, which is why the same literal appears dozens of times across the entry flows. → _Add `NEU.hairline = '#DDD4C0'` (and a forest-tone counterpart) to neu.tsx and replace the literals. Bring the card to radius 22 so it matches every other NeuCard the user has already seen._  `[consistency]`

## SiteNav

- **`src/components/SiteNav.tsx:112`** — Nav active state is exact-match only: `const active = pathname === link.href`. On /conferences/explore, /conferences/[slug], /manage/*, /account/* and every blog post, no nav item is highlighted, so the user loses their place in the site. Worse, `/` is both the logo destination and the CONFERENCES href, so on the landing page the CONFERENCES pill is rendered in the gold-on-forest 'active' style beside a logo that goes to the same place. → _Match by section prefix (`pathname === href || pathname.startsWith(href + '/')`, with `/` special-cased to conferences-area prefixes) so the nav reflects where the user actually is._  `[polish]`
- **`src/components/SiteNav.tsx:301`** — The '✨ NEW' badge on the credits chip is `fontSize: '7px'` with `letterSpacing: '0.08em'` and `lineHeight: '13px'`, positioned at `top: -8px` overhanging the pill. 7px is below any legible floor on a desktop display, it is `pointer-events: none` so it cannot be dismissed, and there is no expiry — it will still say NEW next season. → _Raise to 9px minimum, or replace the text with a plain gold dot which reads at any size. Gate it on an account-age or feature-flag date so it retires itself._  `[contrast]`

## SiteNav — mobile menu

- **`src/components/SiteNav.tsx:422`** — The mobile drawer is animated with a magic `maxHeight: menuOpen ? '480px' : '0px'`, but for a signed-in user the content is 4 nav links (~176px) + 2 dividers (~34px) + the language row (~53px) + the user block (~52px) + the credits row (~44px) + SIGN OUT (~48px) + `py-4` (32px) + inter-item gaps (~32px) ≈ 470px — and the language buttons are guaranteed to wrap on narrow phones. The drawer will clip SIGN OUT, its last and most important item, with no scroll to recover it. → _Replace the fixed maxHeight with a measured height (scrollHeight) or a grid-rows 0fr→1fr transition, and add `overflow-y: auto; max-height: calc(100dvh - 72px)` so nothing is ever unreachable._  `[responsive]`
- **`src/components/SiteNav.tsx:474`** — The four language buttons are `flex-1` in a single row and labelled 'EN: English', 'ES: Spanish', 'FR: French', 'AR: العربية'. On a 375px screen minus `px-6` and `px-2` and three `gap-2`s, each button gets roughly 78px for an 11-character label at `text-sm font-bold` — every one of them wraps to two lines or overflows. → _Use a 2×2 grid, or drop the redundant code prefix and show just the endonym ('English', 'Español', 'Français', 'العربية') which is both shorter and more useful to the person who needs to switch._  `[responsive]`

## ProfileDropdown

- **`src/components/ProfileDropdown.tsx:115`** — The account MENU opens on pointer hover with a 200ms grace timer. The house rule reserves hover for read-only informational hints and states plainly that 'Click-to-toggle is reserved for menus and actions'. This is an actions menu (Sign out, Upgrade, navigation) opening on hover, so it fires whenever the cursor crosses the top-right corner on its way to the window controls. → _Make it click-to-open like every other menu, and keep the 200ms grace only for closing. If hover-open is a deliberate exception, record it in AGENTS.md so the rule and the code agree._  `[rule-violation]`
- **`src/components/ProfileDropdown.tsx:55`** — `place()` always sets `top: r.bottom + 8` with no flip and no height clamp, while the panel can reach ~430px (header 68 + 4 menu rows 128 + conferences header 26 + 5 rows 160 + all-conferences row 32 + sign-out 48). On a 700px-tall laptop window the bottom of the menu — including SIGN OUT — runs off screen with no scroll. DatePicker.tsx:78-83 solves exactly this problem with flip + maxHeight + internal scroll; the two implementations of the same house pattern disagree. → _Copy DatePicker's placement maths verbatim (space-below vs space-above, flip, `maxHeight` + `overflowY: auto`), or better, extract it into a shared `usePopoverPosition()` hook so all four popovers in the app behave identically._  `[polish]`
- **`src/components/ProfileDropdown.tsx:277`** — Casing is mixed inside one 280px menu: 'MY PROFILE' / 'MUN CV' / 'CONFERENCE CALENDAR' / 'UPGRADE TO UNLIMITED' / 'CREDITS & SUBSCRIPTION' / 'SIGN OUT' are uppercase at 12px with 0.05em tracking, while the conference rows ('WORLDMUN 2026' + role tag) and 'All conferences' are sentence/mixed case at 11–12px. Two rows also point at the same destination `/account/unlimited` with two different labels and two different trailing '✦' treatments (bare gold glyph vs gold pill), and the nav chip beside the trigger is a third entry point to the same page. → _Settle on sentence case for menu rows (uppercase micro-labels belong on section headers like YOUR CONFERENCES), and collapse the two /account/unlimited rows into one, letting the copy vary by `unlimited_status` rather than the row count._  `[consistency]`
- **`src/components/ProfileDropdown.tsx:451`** — SIGN OUT uses `#8B2020`, a red that exists in no design token — NEU has no danger colour at all. The same literal is repeated for every error state in the entry flows (join/page.tsx:369, :473, :483, :646, :669; SiteNav.tsx:525; DemoGate.tsx:191). Seven files independently hardcoding the same semantic colour. → _Add `NEU.danger = '#8B2020'` plus a soft fill (`rgba(139,32,32,0.08)`) and a border tint to neu.tsx, and replace every literal. Do the same for the amber warning already covered by NEU.amber (#B8844A)._  `[consistency]`

## DatePicker

- **`src/components/DatePicker.tsx:13`** — The shared picker — mandated by the house rule for EVERY date input in the app — is English-only and Gregorian-Monday-first regardless of locale: MONTHS and DOW are hardcoded English arrays (:13-14), and `fmt()` produces '12 Mar 2026' (:27-29). In a product translated to es/fr/ar (with RTL applied at the document level), an Arabic user gets a left-to-right English calendar. → _Build the labels from `Intl.DateTimeFormat(locale, {month:'long'})` and `Intl.Locale.weekInfo.firstDay`, and format the trigger with `toLocaleDateString(locale, {day:'numeric', month:'short', year:'numeric'})`. Take the locale from LanguageContext._  `[copy]`
- **`src/components/DatePicker.tsx:161`** — The calendar header mixes two raw native controls into an otherwise fully custom surface: a `<select>` for the month styled `border: none; background: transparent` (so it renders as text with no affordance that it is clickable, and drops the browser's own arrow) and an `<input type="number">` for the year (which shows Chrome's spinner arrows on hover, a control that appears nowhere else in the design system). → _Replace both with the house pill/menu pattern: a month button opening a 12-cell grid, and ◂ ▸ year steppers or a small year grid. If the native select stays, at least render a ChevronDown beside it so it reads as interactive._  `[consistency]`
- **`src/components/DatePicker.tsx:129`** — The trigger has `focus:outline-none` with no replacement — the only focus cue is a border colour that changes on `open`, not on focus — and there is no keyboard navigation inside the grid at all (no arrow-key day movement, no Escape to close, no Home/End). Disabled days are signalled solely by `color: '#CFC6B4'` on `#FAF8F3`, roughly a 1.7:1 contrast ratio, which reads as a rendering glitch rather than an unavailable date. → _Add a `focus-visible` ring on the trigger, implement arrow/Home/End/Escape handling on the grid with a roving tabindex, and give disabled days a second cue (strikethrough or a lighter inset well) so they do not rely on a near-invisible colour step._  `[contrast]`
- **`src/components/DatePicker.tsx:188`** — Once a date is chosen there is no way to unset it — no Clear control — and no 'Today' shortcut, so a user who picks the wrong month must navigate back manually. The placeholder ('Select a date') therefore becomes unreachable state after the first interaction, which matters for any optional date field. → _Add a footer row with 'Today' and, when `value` is set, 'Clear' (calling `onChange('')`). Both are one line each and remove a class of dead-end interactions._  `[missing-state]`
- **`src/components/DatePicker.tsx:73`** — `const height = menuRef.current?.offsetHeight ?? 372;` — on the first open the menu is not yet mounted, so the flip decision always uses the 372px guess. If the real panel differs (it will: `maxHeight` is applied afterwards and the header wraps at some locales), the first open can flip the wrong way and then correct itself on the next scroll/resize, which reads as a jump. → _Measure after mount with a layout effect and re-place once, or render the menu invisibly for one frame to measure before positioning._  `[polish]`

## wizard — CardSelect

- **`src/components/wizard.tsx:453`** — A searchable CardSelect sets `maxHeight: 360; overflowY: 'auto'` on the grid, while `cardBaseStyle` hover applies `translateY(-6px) scale(1.05)` plus a 46px-blur, 20px-offset shadow (`CARD_LIFT_GLASS`). `overflow-y: auto` also clips horizontally, so every hovered card in a searchable grid has its lift and its glow sliced off at the container edges — and the `padding: '6px'` allows for none of it. → _Raise the container padding to ~16px so the lifted card and its shadow stay inside the clip, or move the scroll to a wrapper and leave the grid itself unclipped._  `[polish]`

## wizard — cards

- **`src/components/wizard.tsx:186`** — On hover, `cardBaseStyle` REPLACES the selected state's border: `hovered ? '2px solid rgba(255,255,255,0.8)' : selected ? '2px solid NEU.forest' : ...` and likewise swaps the background from `NEU.surface` to `rgba(255,255,255,0.6)`. So hovering the card you already picked makes it look unpicked — only the 24px gold check survives. Selection should be the more durable signal, not the one that yields. → _Compose the two states instead of branching: keep the forest border when selected and add the frost fill/lift on top, e.g. `border: selected ? forest : hovered ? frost : rest` with the hover lift applied independently._  `[polish]`
- **`src/components/wizard.tsx:203`** — `backdropFilter: 'blur(16px) saturate(1.2)'` is applied per card on hover. In a searchable CardSelect this can be a grid of 190 country cards; every mouse-over creates and destroys a compositor-level backdrop filter, which is one of the most expensive properties available. `transition` also animates `background-color` into and out of a backdrop-filtered state, so the blur pops in unblended. → _Drop the backdrop-filter for the compact (`size='default'`) tier and keep it only on the two large TwoTabPick cards where the effect is actually visible and the count is 2._  `[polish]`
- **`src/components/wizard.tsx:282`** — TwoTabPick's buttons carry `className="... focus-visible:ring-2"` with no ring colour specified (so it falls back to currentColor) while `cardBaseStyle` sets `outline: 'none'`; CardSelect's buttons (:463-473) have no focus-visible class at all. Two components in the same file, built for the same wizard, disagree about whether keyboard focus is visible — and CardSelect, the one with up to 190 targets, is the one without it. → _Add a shared `focus-visible` treatment to `cardBaseStyle` itself — `boxShadow: 0 0 0 3px rgba(182,135,31,0.5)` alongside the existing shadow — so both components inherit it and it cannot drift again._  `[contrast]`

## wizard — TwoTabPick

- **`src/components/wizard.tsx:263`** — `gridTemplateColumns: 'repeat(2, minmax(0, 1fr))'` with `minHeight: 400` and a 25px label is unconditional — no breakpoint. On a 375px phone the two onboarding cards are ~165px wide each and 400px tall, holding a 248px-tall cover image and a 25px/15px text block. `CardSelect`'s `columns` prop has the same problem: `repeat(${columns}, ...)` with columns defaulting to 3. → _Stack TwoTabPick to one column below sm (the cards can shorten to ~220px with a 140px image), and make `columns` responsive — `repeat(auto-fit, minmax(140px, 1fr))` handles every width with no prop._  `[responsive]`

> **Method / limits:** METHOD: 100% source read, no dev server, no browser (per instructions). Every file in scope read end-to-end: src/app/page.tsx, src/app/sessions/page.tsx, src/app/HomeClient.tsx, src/app/join/page.tsx, src/app/create/page.tsx, src/app/layout.tsx, src/app/globals.css, src/app/conferences/StagefrontClient.tsx, src/app/conferences/landing-lab/VariantStagefront.tsx + shared.tsx, src/components/{DemoGate,MobileGate,CreditsWelcomeGate,CreditsWelcomeModal,SiteNav,Brand,ProfileDropdown,DatePicker,wizard,CommitteeNameInput,FitToScreen,Portal,neu}.tsx, plus src/lib/{translations,presetNames,committeeService}.ts for copy and code-generation checks. Pixel/contrast claims are derived from the literal style values in source, not from rendering.

ROUTE VERIFICATION: confirmed `/` renders StagefrontClient → VariantStagefront (conferences landing), and `/sessions` renders HomeClient. HomeClient is NOT the root page. MobileGate is mounted ONLY on /chair/[code] — /join, /create, /delegate, /advisor and /voting are ungated on phones. DemoGate has DEMO_GATE_ENABLED = false, so it renders nothing today.

COVERAGE LIMITS: I did not audit the sub-components the landing composes (ConferenceCard, ConferenceRosterPicker, CommitteeEditorModal) or /auth/onboarding beyond wizard.tsx itself — those belong to adjacent areas. I did not evaluate real rendered contrast or real text metrics.

INCIDENTAL NON-UI OBSERVATIONS (not in findings):
1. FUNCTIONAL BUG — chair codes cannot be pasted into /join. `join_code_placeholder` is 'ABC123 or ABC123-4821' and HomeClient:517 routes a dashed code to `/join?code=ABC123-4821&mode=chair`, but join/page.tsx:204 calls `getCommitteeByCode(upper)` which does `.eq('code', upperCode)` (committeeService.ts:197) against a 6-char dash-free code (generateCode, :21-23). The dashed form always resolves to "Committee not found." The code must be split on '-' before lookup, with the suffix pre-filling the chair password.
2. `generateCode()` = `Math.random().toString(36).substring(2, 8)` can return fewer than 6 characters when the random float stringifies short (e.g. 0.5 → "5"), and includes 0/O/1/I/L which are ambiguous when read aloud.
3. `Portal` (Portal.tsx:9) mounts into `#fit-root` when it exists. On FitToScreen pages (/join, /create, /chair) that node carries a CSS transform, so anything portaled there becomes position-fixed relative to the transformed box while its coordinates were computed in real viewport space. DatePicker and ProfileDropdown both compute `window.innerHeight`-relative coordinates and portal — neither is used on /join or /create today, but any future "fix the clipped dropdown by portaling it" change on those pages will land misplaced. This is the same class as the 257px modal-header bug.
4. HomeClient:500 `const eightHours = 18 * 60 * 60 * 1000;` — the identifier says 8, the value is 18.
5. src/components/CommitteeNameInput.tsx is imported by nothing (verified by grep); /create has its own richer copy inline at create/page.tsx:130. Two divergent COMMITTEE_PRESETS arrays now exist (the component's has 14 entries, /create has 37).
6. join/page.tsx:481 renders an `allocationError === '__wrong_role__'` panel, but nothing ever sets that value — the allocation effect only sets `'__signin__'` and `'__not_associated__'`. Dead branch.
7. HomeClient:492 reads two localStorage keys and migrates one to the other on every homepage load.


---

# Design system, shared primitives and marketing pages  
_104 findings_


## Design system — typography (site-wide)

- **`src/app/layout.tsx:102`** — The Outfit typeface is NEVER loaded. layout.tsx <head> links only Playfair Display (ital@1) and Noto Sans Arabic. There is no next/font import anywhere in src/, no @font-face rule, and no font file in public/. Meanwhile `fontFamily: "'Outfit', sans-serif"` appears 755 times in inline styles and globals.css:51 sets `body { font-family: 'Outfit', system-ui, sans-serif }`. Result: every one of those 755 declarations falls back to the generic `sans-serif` (Helvetica/Arial) while any element WITHOUT the inline style inherits body's `system-ui` (SF Pro / Segoe UI). The app currently renders in two different unintended typefaces at once, and the entire visual identity described in neu.tsx never ships. → _Add Outfit via next/font/google (weights 400,500,600,700,800,900 — all six inline weights are in use) and expose it as a CSS variable, or add the Google Fonts <link>. Then make the fallback stack identical everywhere: `var(--font-outfit), system-ui, sans-serif`. Verify by rendering a NeuStatTile at fontWeight 900 — Arial has no 900._  `[consistency]`

## Design system — typography

- **`src/app/layout.tsx:102`** — Playfair Display is loaded as `ital@1` only (italic, weight 400). MobileGate.tsx:42 uses Playfair with `fontWeight: 700` and no `fontStyle: 'italic'`, so that headline gets no real Playfair at all — the browser either synthesises or falls straight to the generic `serif`. → _Either add the roman/700 axis to the Playfair request, or change MobileGate's headline to `fontStyle:'italic', fontWeight:400` so it matches every other Playfair use in the app (About banner, LegalPage banner, Contact hero, CreditsWelcomeModal)._  `[polish]`
- **`src/components/SettingsPanel.tsx:881`** — 'DM Mono' (33 declarations across 11 files) and 'Poppins' (7 declarations across 7 files) are also never loaded — same problem as Outfit. Poppins in particular appears as `"'Poppins','Outfit',sans-serif"` on FeedbackLogPanel, ScoreboardPanel, TutorialOverlay, SettingsPanel and the chair page's GSL label, i.e. a fourth family declared but silently unrendered. → _Decide on the real type system — one sans (Outfit) + one mono (DM Mono) + one display italic (Playfair) — load exactly those three, and delete every Poppins declaration. The mixed Poppins/Outfit stacks read as leftovers from an earlier design._  `[consistency]`

## Design system — tokens

- **`src/app/globals.css:99`** — There are two complete, unrelated token systems in the codebase. globals.css ships the stock shadcn palette (`--primary: oklch(0.205 0 0)`, `--muted-foreground: oklch(0.556 0 0)`, `--ring: oklch(0.708 0 0)`, five identical grey `--chart-*`, a full `.dark` block) plus a `--radius: 0.625rem` scale — none of which has any relationship to the forest/ivory NEU palette. NEU itself is a JS object, so it is unavailable to CSS. Nothing in the app is dark-mode capable, yet the whole `.dark` block ships. → _Publish NEU as CSS custom properties in `:root` (`--neu-base`, `--neu-surface`, `--neu-ink`, `--neu-forest`, `--neu-gold`, `--neu-muted`, `--neu-out`, `--neu-in`…) and map the shadcn `--primary/--muted/--ring/--border/--destructive` onto them so the two systems agree. Delete the `.dark` block and the five grey `--chart-*` vars until a dark mode or a chart actually exists._  `[consistency]`
- **`src/app/globals.css:172`** — `@layer base { body { @apply bg-background text-foreground } }` sets the page background to `--background: oklch(1 0 0)` = pure white, and `* { @apply border-border outline-ring/50 }` gives every element a default grey border colour and a grey focus outline. The ivory background only appears because layout.tsx:112 overrides it with `bg-[#EDE7D8]`. Any surface that forgets flashes white, and every default focus ring on the site is mid-grey on ivory. → _Set `--background: #EDE7D8`, `--foreground: #1C1410`, `--border: #DDD4C0`, `--ring: #B6871F` in `:root` so the defaults are already correct and the ivory is not one override away from disappearing._  `[consistency]`
- **`src/app/globals.css:10`** — `--font-heading: var(--font-sans); --font-sans: var(--font-sans);` inside `@theme inline` is a circular self-reference and `--font-sans` is never defined in `:root`. `html { @apply font-sans }` therefore resolves to an invalid value. → _Define `--font-sans` once from the loaded Outfit variable and drop the self-referential line._  `[polish]`

## Design system — colour

- **`src/components/neu.tsx:24`** — 200 distinct 6-digit hex colours exist in src/. The NEU token object is imported by only 61 of 241 .tsx files (25%), so the same values are re-typed as literals thousands of times: #1B3828 ×1388, #9A8A78 ×915, #1C1410 ×823, #EED98A ×440, #EDE7D8 ×358, #B6871F ×237. Worse, near-duplicates have accumulated: EIGHT muted browns (#9A8A78 915, #6A5A4A 223, #6B5F52 50, #6E5F4E 36, #6B5E4E 10, #8A7D6C 7, #7A6E5E 7, #6B5D4B 5), FIVE forest greens (#1B3828 1388, #2A5A3C 213, #3D7A52 117, #16301F 17, #2F6644 5), EIGHT golds (#EED98A 440, #B6871F 237, #8A6614 41, #9A6B2F 19, #F3E3A1 11, #7A5A20 11, #6B4F12 6, #3A2A08 4) and FIVE reds (#8B2020 346, #7A1C1C 7, #A03030 5, #DC3545 3, #FCA5A5 11). → _Add the missing roles to NEU (`inkSoft: #6A5A4A`, `border: #DDD4C0`, `card: #FAF8F3`, `danger: #8B2020`, `forestDeep: #16301F`, `forestMid: #2A5A3C`), then codemod the literals to token references and add an ESLint rule banning raw hex in .tsx. Pick ONE muted, ONE mid-forest, ONE mid-gold and one red, and delete the rest._  `[consistency]`
- **`src/components/neu.tsx:26`** — `NEU.surface` (#F0EBDD) — the documented card colour of the whole system — is used only 11 times in src/. The colour that actually paints most cards is #FAF8F3 (335 uses), which is not a token at all. Every card in the manage area, blog, legal pages and the shared ConfirmModal/DatePicker is #FAF8F3 with a `1px solid #DDD4C0` border, i.e. a flat bordered card, not the neumorphic extruded card neu.tsx describes. → _Decide which card is canonical. If it's the bordered #FAF8F3 card, promote it to `NEU.card` and `NEU.border` and update the neu.tsx header comment, which currently claims #F0EBDD is the card surface. If it's the extruded NeuCard, migrate the bordered ones. Right now the doc-comment and the shipped UI disagree._  `[consistency]`

## Design system — shadows

- **`src/components/neu.tsx:35`** — neu.tsx exports 6 shadow tokens (out, outHover, outSm, outSmHover, in, inSm). The codebase contains 134 DISTINCT inline `boxShadow` string literals plus 5 Tailwind named shadows (shadow-2xl ×16, shadow-lg ×13, shadow-xl ×12, shadow-sm ×9).
