Many are one-off near-duplicates: `0 8px 22px rgba(27,56,40,0.14)`, `0 8px 24px rgba(27,56,40,0.08)`, `0 8px 24px rgba(27,56,40,0.07)`, `0 8px 28px rgba(27,56,40,0.1)`, `0 8px 24px rgba(27,56,40,0.22)`, `0 8px 24px rgba(27,56,40,0.28)` all exist separately. → _Define an elevation ladder of 4 — `NEU.e1` (resting card), `NEU.e2` (hover), `NEU.e3` (popover/dropdown), `NEU.e4` (modal) — plus the two inset tokens, and replace the 134 literals. Nobody can tell 0.07 from 0.08 alpha; the variety is pure entropy._  `[consistency]`

## Design system — radii

- **`src/components/neu.tsx:148`** — There are 24 distinct numeric `borderRadius` values in inline styles (999, 9999, 12, 16, 14, 10, 8, 20, 24, 18, 11, 22, 2, 6, 1, 4, 3, 26, 0, 9, 7, 5, 28, 13) plus 20 distinct Tailwind `rounded-*` classes (rounded-xl ×497 = 14px, rounded-full ×472, rounded-lg ×222 = 10px, rounded-2xl ×155 = 18px, rounded-md ×30, rounded-[22px] ×16, rounded-[20px] ×13, rounded-3xl ×12, rounded-[24px] ×8, rounded-[10px] ×7, rounded-[30px], rounded-[26px], rounded-[18px], rounded-[11px]…). NeuCard hardcodes 22, NeuInset hardcodes 16, SectionCardSkeleton uses rounded-[20px], the blog article uses 20px, LegalPage uses rounded-2xl (18px). Five different radii for 'a card'. → _Publish a 5-step radius scale on NEU (`r1: 8, r2: 12, r3: 16, r4: 22, pill: 999`), have NeuCard/NeuInset/SectionCardSkeleton read from it, and normalise the 20+ arbitrary Tailwind values onto it. Note the shadcn `--radius: 0.625rem` ladder (xl=14, 2xl=18, 3xl=22) does not line up with the neu numbers at all — pick one._  `[consistency]`

## Design system — type scale

- **`src/components/neu.tsx`** — 33 distinct inline `fontSize` values are in use (11 ×165, 10 ×135, 13 ×108, 12.5 ×91, 12 ×90, 10.5 ×68, 11.5 ×67, 9 ×60, 14 ×41, 15 ×30, 13.5 ×29, 9.5 ×28, 8.5 ×8, 8 ×4, plus 18/16/17/19/19.5/20/21/22/24/25/26/27/28/30/34/46/48/100), 25 distinct `letterSpacing` values, and 6 font weights (400/500/600/700/800/900). The half-pixel sizes (12.5, 11.5, 10.5, 9.5, 13.5, 14.5, 8.5) are 275+ declarations that snap to different rendered heights per browser. → _Ship a 7-step scale on NEU — e.g. `xs:10, sm:11.5, base:13, md:15, lg:18, xl:24, display:34` — with 3 weights (600 body-strong, 800 label, 900 numerals) and 3 tracking values (0, 0.04em, 0.12em uppercase). Drop every value not on the scale. Half-pixel sizes are the single loudest signal that the type scale was never designed._  `[consistency]`

## Design system — focus

- **`src/components/neu.tsx:534`** — `focus:outline-none` appears 843 times across src/, plus 27 inline `outline: 'none'`. Only 35 `focus-visible` usages exist in the entire app, and 90 files use focus:outline-none with NO replacement ring of any kind. The shared primitives are among the offenders: NeuPill (neu.tsx:534), NeuButton (:578), NeuChecklistRow (:641), DatePicker trigger + every day cell + both month arrows + the year input, ConfirmModal's Cancel and Confirm, Toast's dismiss, SiteNav's language trigger, every language option, the credit chip, the account trigger and SIGN IN. The app is effectively unusable by keyboard and fails WCAG 2.4.7. → _Add one token — `NEU.focusRing = '0 0 0 3px rgba(182,135,31,0.45)'` — and a `.neu-focus { &:focus-visible { box-shadow: <ring>, <own shadow> } }` utility in globals.css. Apply it in the ~15 shared primitives first (that covers most of the surface), then codemod `focus:outline-none` → `focus:outline-none neu-focus`._  `[rule-violation]`

## Design system — motion

- **`src/components/ui/button.tsx:8`** — `transition-all` appears 95 times across 25 files and inline `transition: 'all …'` 10 more times. Three of those are in SHARED primitives that everything else composes from: ui/button.tsx:8 cva base, ui/badge.tsx:8 cva base, ui/progress.tsx:24. `transition: all` animates layout properties (width, height, padding, border-width) on every state change and is the classic cause of jitter on hover. → _Replace with explicit property lists — `transition: box-shadow 260ms var(--ease), transform 160ms var(--ease), background-color 200ms var(--ease)` — which is exactly what neu.tsx already does correctly (NeuCard :151, NeuButton :593, NeuPill :524). Start with the three ui/* primitives, then the 10 inline `all` sites._  `[rule-violation]`
- **`src/app/globals.css:238`** — `prefers-reduced-motion` is honoured for exactly two things: the ambassador marquee (:238) and the skeleton shimmer (:257). Nothing else — no guard for the dozens of `transform: translateY/scale` hover lifts, the 600ms NeuProgress width tween, the 700ms NeuRing stroke tween, the 300ms Disclosure max-height animation, the Toast slide-in, or the mobile-menu max-height. → _Add a global fallback in globals.css: `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) { *, *::before, *::after { animation-duration: 0.01ms !important; animation-iteration-count: 1 !important; transition-duration: 0.01ms !important; scroll-behavior: auto !important } }` and keep the two bespoke rules for the cases that need a different treatment._  `[missing-state]`

## Design system — contrast

- **`src/components/neu.tsx:33`** — `NEU.muted` (#9A8A78) on `NEU.base` (#EDE7D8) is 2.72:1 — below the 4.5:1 WCAG AA minimum for body text and below even the 3:1 large-text minimum. It is used 915 times, and almost always at 9–13px: FooterLegal's whole legal strip (11px, FooterLegal.tsx:70,89,95), the blog byline (13px, every post's `s.meta`), the LegalShell copyright (LegalPage.tsx:91), Contact's reply-time and all three 'other ways' descriptions (11–12px, ContactClient.tsx:341,361,376,392), the SiteNav mobile email line, the ProfileDropdown section headers at 9–10px, NeuStatTile labels at 10.5–11px. → _Introduce two muted tokens: `NEU.muted` stays for decorative/disabled only, and a new `NEU.mutedText = #6A5A4A` (5.36:1 on #EDE7D8, already used 223 times) becomes the one used for any text a user is meant to read. Sweep every #9A8A78 under 14px onto it._  `[contrast]`

## Design system — missing primitives

- **`src/components/neu.tsx`** — neu.tsx offers Card, Inset, IconDisc, StatTile, Progress, Ring, Pill, Button, ChecklistRow — and nothing else. There is no Toggle, no Input/Field, no Select, no Modal, no Tooltip, no Tabs, no EmptyState. Consequently: at least 4 bespoke toggle switches with different geometry (SettingsPanel.tsx:554 is 40×22 track / 18px knob / #1B3828 on #DDD4C0 with no role="switch"; VotingRulesPanel.tsx:398 is 36×20 / 14px knob / #3D7A52 on rgba(255,255,255,0.16) WITH role="switch"; communications/page.tsx:343 is 34×19; grain-dev is w-10 h-5); 21 files hand-roll their own `role="dialog"` overlay; 2 bespoke tooltip components (SettingsPanel.tsx:105 forest-on-gold pill, VotingRulesPanel.tsx:263 InfoDot) coexisting with 169 native `title=""` attributes. → _Promote the best existing implementation of each to neu.tsx and delete the copies: `NeuToggle` (VotingRulesPanel's — it has role/aria-checked and the 0.96 active scale), `NeuModal` (CommitteeEditorModal's ModalOverlay, which ConfirmModal already reuses), `NeuTooltip` (VotingRulesPanel's InfoDot — it is the only one that portals, opens on hover AND focus, and repositions on scroll). One toggle geometry, one modal chrome, one tooltip._  `[consistency]`

## Design system — primitives

- **`src/components/neu.tsx:450`** — Two exported primitives are effectively dead: `NeuRing` has 1 call site and `NeuChecklistRow` has 1. `NeuStatTile` has 8 uses in 3 files and `NeuProgress` 6 in 4. Meanwhile `NeuCard` (68), `NeuIconDisc` (77) and `NeuButton` (48) carry the system. → _Either adopt NeuRing/NeuChecklistRow more widely (the ring would suit quorum and the caucus clock; the checklist row would suit the chair pre-session setup) or delete them. A design system with two single-use components teaches contributors that the system is optional._  `[consistency]`
- **`src/components/neu.tsx:569`** — NeuButton picks its text colour by REFERENCE equality: `gradient === NEU_GRADIENTS.gold ? NEU.forest : NEU.gold`. Any caller who passes a literal `['#EED98A','#B6871F']` — the same colours, a different array — gets gold text on a gold gradient, i.e. invisible. 10 call sites already pass a computed/prop gradient (`gradient={cell.gradient}`, `gradient={meta.gradient}`, `gradient={saved ? … : …}`) so this is one refactor away from shipping. → _Compare by luminance instead — the file already has a `darkStop()` helper doing exactly that at :65. Use `lum(gradient[0]) > 128 ? NEU.forest : NEU.gold`._  `[polish]`
- **`src/components/neu.tsx:583`** — NeuButton's disabled state is `background: rgba(27,56,40,0.14)`, `color: NEU.muted`, `boxShadow: 'none'`, `transform: 'none'`. A flat, shadowless grey slab on a neumorphic page reads as a rendering failure rather than 'not available yet' — every other element on the page is extruded. The same pattern recurs in ConfirmModal (#DDD4C0 fill + #9A8A78 text) and the About/Contact submit buttons (opacity 0.38, which just makes the label unreadable). → _Give disabled a deliberate look: keep the gradient at ~35% saturation, keep `NEU.inSm` (pressed-in reads as 'locked'), and set text to `NEU.muted` at full opacity. Never use `opacity` for disabled — it degrades text contrast as a side effect. Pair with a `title`/hint saying WHY it is disabled._  `[polish]`
- **`src/components/neu.tsx:143`** — NeuCard, NeuPill, NeuButton and NeuChecklistRow all track hover in React state (`const [hovered, setHovered] = useState(false)` + onMouseEnter/onMouseLeave). With 68 NeuCards and 77 NeuIconDiscs on a dashboard this is a re-render on every pointer move across the grid, and it means hover is dead during hydration and unavailable to `:focus-visible`. → _Move the hover/press treatment to CSS — a `.neu-card` class with `:hover`/`:active`/`:focus-visible` variants using the CSS-variable shadow tokens. Keep React state only where the hover value feeds logic (e.g. the ChevronRight nudge)._  `[polish]`
- **`src/components/neu.tsx:79`** — Emoji3D fetches every icon from `https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/gh/microsoft/fluentui-emoji@main/...` at runtime — 23 call sites across 9 files, all inside NeuIconDisc, i.e. the primary iconography of the organiser dashboard. On a cold or throttled connection every disc is empty for a beat and then pops; the lucide fallback only fires on a hard error, not on slowness. There is no preload and no self-hosting. → _Vendor the ~20 emoji actually used into `public/emoji/` (they are a few KB each as PNG) and serve them locally, or at minimum add `<link rel=preconnect href="https://cdn.jsdelivr.net">` to layout.tsx and render the lucide fallback immediately, swapping to the 3D PNG once decoded._  `[missing-state]`

## Design system — decoration

- **`src/components/LegalPage.tsx:14`** — The grain overlay is an inline 300-character data-URI SVG copy-pasted into 24 files (33 occurrences), and it has already drifted: `opacity: 0.18` (×7), `0.14` (×1), `0.07` (×1), and `mixBlendMode: 'multiply'` (×13) vs `'overlay'` (×2). Separately, `public/background-grain.png` exists and is used only by the /grain-dev tuning page. → _Extract `<Grain tone="ivory" | "forest" />` into a shared component with the two sanctioned settings baked in, and replace all 33 inline copies. Delete the unused PNG or make the component use it._  `[consistency]`

## ui/ (shadcn layer)

- **`src/components/ui/alert.tsx`** — 8 of the 14 files in src/components/ui/ have zero importers anywhere in src/: alert, avatar, badge, carousel, field, input-group, progress, separator (separator has one importer inside ui/ only), spinner. The other 6 are imported by exactly two pages — AboutClient and ContactClient — so the entire shadcn layer exists to serve two marketing forms. → _Delete the 8 unused files. Then either convert the About/Contact forms to neu.tsx primitives (NeuButton already exists; a NeuInput does not — add one) and delete the ui/ folder entirely, or keep ui/ but retheme its tokens onto NEU. Right now it is a second design system maintained for two files._  `[consistency]`
- **`src/components/ui/input.tsx:10`** — `Input` is `h-8` (32px tall) with `text-base` (16px) and a focus ring of `ring-ring/50` where `--ring: oklch(0.708 0 0)` = mid-grey. On the public Contact and About forms this produces 32px-tall fields whose 16px text nearly fills the box, and a GREY focus glow on an ivory/forest page. Compare the shared DatePicker trigger, which is `px-4 py-3` (~48px) with a forest border on focus. → _Set `--ring: #B6871F` in :root so the focus glow is gold, and raise the default input to `h-11` (44px) so it matches the DatePicker and clears the 44px touch target. Both fixes are one line each and fix every ui/ consumer at once._  `[consistency]`

## DatePicker

- **`src/components/DatePicker.tsx:132`** — The shared DatePicker hardcodes #FAF8F3, #DDD4C0, #1B3828, #1C1410, #9A8A78, #B6871F, #EED98A and #CFC6B4 (a colour used exactly once in the whole codebase, for disabled days) instead of importing NEU. Its own local `const OUTFIT` duplicates the one neu.tsx already exports. → _Import `NEU`, `OUTFIT`, `EASE` from '@/components/neu' and use `NEU.muted` for disabled days. #CFC6B4 vs the app-wide #9A8A78 is a distinction no user can perceive._  `[consistency]`
- **`src/components/DatePicker.tsx:161`** — Inside a bespoke, carefully-portaled calendar, the month selector is a raw native `<select>` and the year is a raw `<input type="number">` with `width: 58`. Both are unstyled OS controls (native dropdown arrow, native spinner buttons) sitting in the middle of the neumorphic panel — exactly the 'fighting a native OS control' problem the file's own header comment says it exists to avoid. → _Replace the month select with a 12-cell month grid reached by clicking the header label (same pattern as the day grid), and the year input with ‹ › year steppers or a year grid. Keep the native select only as the mobile fallback if you want._  `[consistency]`
- **`src/components/DatePicker.tsx:181`** — No 'Today' shortcut and no 'Clear' action. To pick a date 3 years back (date of birth, past conference) the user clicks ‹ 36 times or fights the bare number input. To un-set a date there is no affordance at all. → _Add a footer row to the panel: `Today` (jumps + selects) and `Clear` (calls onChange('')). Both are one line and remove the two most common dead ends._  `[wasted-effort]`
- **`src/components/DatePicker.tsx:187`** — The day grid has no keyboard navigation. Each day is a separate tab stop (42 tab stops per month) with `focus:outline-none`, so a keyboard user tabs through 42 invisible buttons. Arrow keys do nothing, PageUp/PageDown do nothing, Escape does not close (only outside mousedown does). → _Make the grid a single roving-tabindex composite: arrows move the focused day, PageUp/PageDown change month, Home/End jump to month bounds, Enter selects, Escape closes and returns focus to the trigger. Add a visible focus ring._  `[polish]`

## ConfirmModal

- **`src/components/ConfirmModal.tsx:116`** — Cancel and Confirm are both `flex-1` — exactly 50/50 — with identical radius, identical height and identical 14px bold caps. On a `danger` confirm the only differentiator is that one is filled #8B2020 and the other is an outline. A destructive action and its escape hatch carry equal visual weight, and Enter is bound to Confirm (:65), so a stray Return key deletes. → _Make Cancel a quieter text/ghost button at natural width and Confirm the sized primary, or reverse the order so the safe action is leftmost and default-focused. For `danger`, do not bind Enter to Confirm — require a click or a typed confirmation (the `confirmDisabled` prop already supports type-to-confirm)._  `[hierarchy]`
- **`src/components/ConfirmModal.tsx:126`** — `{cancelLabel.toUpperCase()}` and `{confirmLabel.toUpperCase()}` force uppercase in JS. For the Arabic locale this is a no-op (fine) but for accented Latin (Spanish/French labels like 'Añadir', 'Créer') `toUpperCase()` produces locale-incorrect forms, and it makes any label with a proper noun shout. → _Drop `toUpperCase()` and use CSS `textTransform: 'uppercase'` so the accessible name and the copy-paste text stay as authored — or just use sentence case, which the rest of the modal already uses for its title and body._  `[copy]`
- **`src/components/ConfirmModal.tsx:83`** — `width: 400, maxWidth: '90vw'`. When ConfirmModal is rendered inside a FitToScreen tree, `vw` resolves against the real viewport while the parent #fit-root is CSS-transformed and 820px tall — so the '90vw' cap is computed against a different coordinate space than the box it constrains. → _Use `maxWidth: 'min(400px, calc(100% - 32px))'` so the cap is relative to the actual containing block, not the viewport. Sweep the same fix through DefaultEmailPreviewModal (`min(94vw, 680px)`, `88vh`), CommitteeEditorModal (`min(920px, calc(100vw - 32px))`, `85vh` ×2) and CVEntryModal (`calc(100vh - 48px)`)._  `[responsive]`

## Toast

- **`src/components/Toast.tsx:155`** — `ToastHost` is mounted in exactly ONE file — src/app/manage/[slug]/assignment/page.tsx:4419 — and `toast()` is imported by that same one file. The header comment says it 'replaces the old inline flash banner', but the rest of the app still runs its own bespoke inline flash banners and layout-shifting notices. → _Mount `<ToastHost />` once in the root layout (it renders null when empty) and migrate the remaining inline flash banners. A feedback mechanism used by 1 of 241 files is not a system._  `[consistency]`
- **`src/components/Toast.tsx:162`** — ToastHost is portaled through `Portal`, which targets `#fit-root` when it exists (Portal.tsx:9). #fit-root carries a CSS `transform`, which makes it the containing block for `position: fixed` descendants — so `top: 20, right: 20` anchors to the top-right of the scaled 820px-tall cockpit, not the viewport, and `maxWidth: 'min(92vw, 380px)'` on the card (:116) is still measured against the real viewport. Inside any session page the toast lands in the wrong place at the wrong width. → _Give Portal an explicit target prop — `<Portal to="body">` for viewport-fixed layers (toasts, modals, dropdowns) and the #fit-root default only for things that must scale with the cockpit. Then replace the 92vw with a container-relative cap._  `[polish]`
- **`src/components/Toast.tsx:23`** — `DISMISS_MS = 2200` applies to error toasts as well as success toasts. 2.2 seconds is not enough to read a failure message, and the only way to keep it is to notice it and get the pointer onto it before it leaves. → _Keep 2.2s for `kind: 'ok'` and make `kind: 'err'` sticky (or ~8s) with a visible close affordance. Errors should never expire before the user has decided what to do._  `[missing-state]`
- **`src/components/Toast.tsx:55`** — The duplicate-collapse only inspects `toasts[toasts.length - 1]`, so two identical toasts separated by one different toast both render. With MAX_STACK 4 and no grouping, a bulk action that fails 4 times produces 4 identical stacked cards. → _Collapse on any matching (kind, msg) already in the stack and show a `×N` count badge on the existing card instead of pushing a new one._  `[polish]`

## Skeleton

- **`src/components/Skeleton.tsx:16`** — The shared skeleton primitive has exactly 3 consumers, 2 of which are conference pages (ConferenceDetailClient, ParticipantView). Everywhere else loading is either a bare `animate-spin` circle (49 files) or the literal string 'Loading…' (6 sites, e.g. AdminClient.tsx:103, jobs/page.tsx:162, create/page.tsx:937 which says 'Loading...' with three dots while every other site uses the ellipsis character). ProfileDropdown.tsx:367-368 hand-rolls its own static grey placeholder blocks instead of using SkeletonBlock. → _Pick skeletons as the house loading pattern for anything with known shape (lists, cards, tables) and spinners only for indeterminate button-level work. Convert ProfileDropdown's placeholder rows to SkeletonBlock, and replace the 6 'Loading…' strings. Also normalise '...' vs '…'._  `[consistency]`
- **`src/components/Skeleton.tsx:64`** — `SectionCardSkeleton` uses `rounded-[20px]` + `border: '1px solid #DDD4C0'` + `NEU.outSm`, i.e. it combines the bordered #FAF8F3 card language AND the neumorphic shadow, at a 20px radius that matches neither NeuCard (22) nor rounded-2xl (18). The skeleton therefore never matches the card it stands in for. → _Have SectionCardSkeleton render an actual `<NeuCard>` (or the real card component) with SkeletonBlocks inside, so the placeholder is guaranteed to be the same box as the content that replaces it — no layout shift on resolve._  `[consistency]`

## FlagImg

- **`src/components/FlagImg.tsx:18`** — `onError` sets `display: 'none'`. A failed flag silently collapses to zero width, so a delegate row reflows and the country loses its only visual identifier — with no fallback glyph, even though the component already imports `<Emoji>` and uses 🌐 for the empty-code case one line above. There is also no reserved box, so every flag load nudges layout. → _On error, swap to the same 🌐 Emoji fallback used for the empty case, keeping the `size × size` box. Also wrap the img in a fixed `width/height` span so the row height is stable before the sprite arrives._  `[missing-state]`
- **`src/components/FlagImg.tsx:15`** — `alt={code}` means a screen reader announces the raw ISO code — 'F R', 'D E' — next to the delegation name it is already labelling. That is noise, not information. → _The flag is decorative when the country name is adjacent: use `alt=""` + `aria-hidden`. Where the flag stands alone, pass a real `alt` of the country's display name (getCountryDisplayName already exists)._  `[copy]`

## Emoji

- **`src/components/Emoji.tsx:11`** — The CODEPOINTS table maps 32 emoji to Twemoji SVGs, but 104 raw emoji characters are rendered across 45 files and several common ones are NOT in the table: 🎉 (×6), 🏆 (×3), 📝, 🔨, 📘, 🖥, ✨. Those fall through to the platform font, so the same screen mixes flat Twemoji artwork with Apple/Windows 3D emoji at a different weight and colour temperature. → _Either add every emoji actually used to CODEPOINTS (a 10-line change — grep gives you the list) or add a dev-time console warning when `getEmojiUrl` returns null so new unmapped emoji get caught. Consistent emoji artwork is one of the cheapest polish wins available._  `[consistency]`
- **`src/components/Emoji.tsx:85`** — The rendered `<img>` sets both `alt={children}` and `aria-hidden="true"`. The alt is therefore never announced, so the two attributes contradict each other and any emoji carrying real meaning (🎙 for 'currently speaking', 🪑 for 'chair') is invisible to assistive tech. → _Add a `decorative` prop, defaulting true (alt="" + aria-hidden). When false, drop aria-hidden and take an explicit `label` prop ('Currently speaking'), never the raw glyph._  `[polish]`

## MobileGate

- **`src/components/MobileGate.tsx:30`** — The container sets `fontFamily: 'system-ui, sans-serif'` — the only such declaration in src/ — so the body copy and the 'OPEN ON DESKTOP' badge render in SF Pro/Segoe while the rest of the app (nominally) uses Outfit. The headline separately reaches for Playfair. → _Use the OUTFIT constant for the container and Playfair-italic only for the accent word, matching the About/Legal banner treatment._  `[consistency]`
- **`src/components/MobileGate.tsx:65`** — 'OPEN ON DESKTOP' is a `<div>` styled exactly like a primary button (#1B3828 fill, #EDE7D8 text, 10×20 padding, 8px radius, letter-spaced caps) but is not interactive. On the one screen where the user is stuck, the most button-shaped thing on the page does nothing when tapped — it reads as broken. → _Make it obviously a static status line (drop the fill, use a muted uppercase eyebrow) or make it real: a 'Copy link' button that puts the current URL on the clipboard so they can paste it on their laptop. That is the actual next action._  `[copy]`
- **`src/components/MobileGate.tsx:80`** — '(or just rotate your phone, we're not judging)' is false. The gate keys off `navigator.userAgent` (:13, deliberately, per the comment) and never re-evaluates on orientation change — rotating does nothing, ever. The one instruction the screen gives is a dead end. → _Delete the line, or make it true by also allowing through when `window.innerWidth >= 1024` on a re-check bound to `orientationchange`/`resize`. Telling a stuck user to do something that cannot work is worse than saying nothing._  `[copy]`
- **`src/components/MobileGate.tsx:50`** — Every string on the gate is hardcoded English ('Don't you wanna try this on a computer instead?', 'The chair yields the floor… to a real screen.', 'OPEN ON DESKTOP') in an app that ships en/es/fr/ar. A Spanish or Arabic delegate who opens a session link on their phone gets an English-only wall — and in Arabic it will render LTR. → _Route all five strings through `t()` with new keys, and let the RTL treatment come from the existing :lang(ar) handling._  `[copy]`

## FitToScreen (all session pages)

- **`src/components/FitToScreen.tsx:9`** — `BASE_H = 820` with `scale = window.innerHeight / 820`. On a 1440×900 MacBook the whole cockpit is scaled to 1.098×, and on a 1080p display to 1.317× — so every 9px, 10px and 10.5px label in the session UI (and there are 263 of them at ≤10.5px) is rendered through a fractional CSS transform. Fractional-scaled text is measurably softer than natively-sized text and small type suffers most. → _Clamp the scale to `Math.min(1, innerHeight / BASE_H)` so the cockpit never up-scales (it only shrinks to fit short viewports), and let the extra vertical room be real space. Alternatively design at the taller base so the common case is scale 1._  `[polish]`

## Portal

- **`src/components/Portal.tsx:9`** — `Portal` unconditionally prefers `#fit-root` over `document.body`. Because #fit-root has a `transform`, it becomes the containing block for every `position: fixed` descendant — which silently breaks the core promise of the AGENTS.md popover rule ('render the layer at fixed VIEWPORT coordinates'). Every consumer that computes `getBoundingClientRect()` (viewport coords) and then applies them as `position: fixed` inside the portal — DatePicker:147, Toast:162, ProfileDropdown, VotingRulesPanel InfoDot, SettingsPanel's tooltip, FilterPopover, NewDmPicker — is mixing two coordinate spaces whenever #fit-root exists. → _Add `<Portal to="viewport" | "fit">` with `viewport` (→ document.body) as the default, and opt the handful of cockpit-scaled overlays into `fit`. This is a one-file change that makes the whole popover rule actually hold._  `[rule-violation]`

## SessionsHeaderLogo / Brand

- **`src/components/SessionsHeaderLogo.tsx:27`** — Three different logo assets are in play for the same mark: SessionsHeaderLogo loads `/GavellingLogo.png` with `priority`, AboutClient.tsx:268 and ContactClient.tsx:408 load `/GavellingLogo.webp`, SiteNav loads `/Conferences.webp` (png fallback) or `/GavellingSessionsApp.webp`, and Brand.tsx composes `/gavel-mark.png` with live type while SiteNav's credit chip uses `/gavel-mark.webp`. The About/Contact footers then run the PNG through an 8-stage CSS `filter` chain (`brightness(0) saturate(100%) invert(18%) sepia(25%) …`) to recolour it. → _Ship one webp per lockup, use `next/image` everywhere (SessionsHeaderLogo already does), and recolour by shipping a forest-tinted asset rather than a filter chain — the filter approach is unreadable, fragile and costs a paint._  `[consistency]`

## Brand

- **`src/components/Brand.tsx:68`** — `onError` sets `display: 'none'` on the gavel mark. ## FooterLegal

- **`src/components/FooterLegal.tsx:70`** — The whole legal strip is hardcoded English — 'We process personal data under the UK GDPR and EU GDPR…', 'Terms of Service', 'Privacy Policy', 'Your Data & GDPR Rights' — on a site with a prominent es/fr/ar language switcher in the nav directly above it. ## About — founder photos

- **`src/app/about/AboutClient.tsx:127`** — Founder portraits are `/PeterPic.jpg` and `/Christian.jpg` rendered as raw `<img>` into a 260px circle, while all 39 ambassador photos are `.webp`. public/ also contains `Peter.png`, `PeterPic.png`, `Christian.png` — three variants of the same two images shipped. → _Convert both founder portraits to webp at 2× (520px), serve via next/image, and delete the unused png/jpg duplicates from public/._  `[polish]`

## Contact — hero split

- **`src/app/contact/ContactClient.tsx:79`** — The root is `md:h-screen md:overflow-hidden`; inside it the left green panel is `md:sticky md:top-0 md:h-screen`. The panel is therefore a full viewport tall inside a container that is viewport-height MINUS the 72px SiteNav and MINUS the ~120px footer — so roughly 190px of the green panel is clipped by the parent's `overflow-hidden` on every desktop. The clipped region is the bottom of the panel: the gold Playfair quote and its attribution (:147-164). → _Change the left panel to `md:h-full` (or `md:h-[calc(100vh-72px)]` if it must be sticky to the viewport) so its content ends where the container ends. ## Contact — error state

- **`src/app/contact/ContactClient.tsx:298`** — The submit error renders as `Error 500: Unknown error` or `Network error: Failed to fetch` in `text-xs font-mono text-red-500 bg-red-50 border-red-200` — raw HTTP status text in developer-console styling, using stock Tailwind reds (#ef4444 / #fef2f2 / #fecaca) that appear nowhere else in the palette (the app's red is #8B2020). It also never says what the user should do. → _Map failures to human copy — 'We couldn't send that. Try again, or email wearegavelling@gmail.com directly.' — with a retry button, styled with #8B2020 on `rgba(139,32,32,0.08)` (the treatment SiteNav.tsx:525 already uses for SIGN OUT). Log the status to console, don't render it._  `[copy]`

## Contact — subject pills

- **`src/app/contact/ContactClient.tsx:230`** — The four subject pills sit in `flex flex-nowrap overflow-x-auto` with `whiteSpace: nowrap`. At 375px the four localised labels overflow and the last one(s) are simply off-screen with no fade, no arrow, no scrollbar hint and no wrap — the user has no signal that more options exist. → _Either allow `flex-wrap` on small screens (there are only four, they fit in two rows) or add a right-edge mask-image fade so the cut-off pill is visibly a scroll cue._  `[missing-state]`

## Contact — submit button

- **`src/app/contact/ContactClient.tsx:308`** — The hover lift is implemented with `onMouseEnter`/`onMouseLeave` mutating `style.backgroundColor`, `style.transform` and `style.boxShadow`, guarded only by `if (!canSubmit) return`. `sending` is not checked, so hovering the button while a request is in flight still lifts it as if actionable. The transition is `all 200ms ease` (forbidden), and the disabled look is `opacity: 0.38`, which makes the gold label unreadable rather than communicating 'not yet'. → _Use NeuButton (it already has hover/press/disabled done correctly) or, at minimum, add `|| sending` to the guard, replace `all` with `background-color, transform, box-shadow`, and swap opacity-fade for a desaturated fill with full-contrast text._  `[polish]`

## Blog — all 33 posts

- **`src/app/blog/how-to-run-mun-committee/page.tsx:40`** — Each of the 33 post files declares its own local `const s: Record<string, React.CSSProperties>` with the identical 16-key typography sheet — `article` (33/33 byte-identical), `h1` (33/33), `p` (33/33), `cta` (33/33), etc. That is ~530 lines of copy-pasted design tokens. It has already drifted once: 27 of the 28 `callout` styles are `{ backgroundColor:'#EDE7D8', border:'1px solid #DDD4C0', borderLeft:'4px solid #1B3828', borderRadius:'8px', padding:'16px 20px' }` with italic text, while one post uses `{ borderRadius:'12px', padding:'20px 24px' }` with no left rule and `fontWeight: 600` instead of italic. → _Extract `src/app/blog/articleStyles.ts` (or better, an `<Article>` / `<H2>` / `<Callout>` component set) and import it in all 33. That fixes the one drifted callout, makes the next typography change a one-line edit, and removes 500 lines._  `[consistency]`

## Blog — all 34 pages

- **`src/app/blog/page.tsx:42`** — There is not a single `clamp()`, `@media` query or Tailwind responsive prefix (`sm:`/`md:`) anywhere in the blog index or any of the 33 posts. Fixed values only: page `padding: '48px 24px 80px'`, article `padding: '40px 40px 48px'`, `h1: 36px`, `h2: 22px`, `p: 16px`. On a 375px phone the text column is 375 − 48 − 80 = 247px wide, i.e. roughly 30 characters per line, at a 36px headline. These are the pages that take the most organic mobile search traffic in the whole app. → _Add responsive padding (`24px 16px` under 640px, article `24px`) and a fluid h1 (`clamp(26px, 7vw, 36px)`). This is one edit in the extracted style module and it fixes all 34 pages._  `[responsive]`
- **`src/app/blog/page.tsx:42`** — No blog page imports SiteNav and none imports FooterLegal (verified: 0 of 34). The 33 indexed article pages and the index have no navigation, no language switcher, no sign-in, no logo and no legal/GDPR footer — the only public surfaces in the app without any of it. The only exit is a text link '← MUN Resources' / '← Back to Gavelling' at the top. → _Wrap the blog in the same shell as the legal pages (`SiteNav` + the shared `SiteFooter`). It is the highest-traffic entry point to the product and it currently has no path to /sessions, /conferences or sign-up other than one CTA card at the very bottom of a 10-minute read._  `[consistency]`

## Blog — navigation

- **`src/components/SiteNav.tsx:12`** — `NAV_LINKS_CONFIG` contains SESSIONS, CONFERENCES, ABOUT US, CONTACT — no BLOG/RESOURCES entry. 33 SEO guides exist and are in the sitemap, but a visitor who lands on gavelling.com can never find them; the only inbound path is Google. → _Add a 'RESOURCES' (or 'GUIDES') entry pointing at /blog, in all four locales, and give it the same treatment as the others._  `[wasted-effort]`

## Blog — nav active state

- **`src/components/SiteNav.tsx:112`** — `const active = pathname === link.href` is an exact match. On /conferences/explore, /conferences/map, /conferences/roles, /manage/*, /account/*, /blog/* and every conference detail page, NO nav item is highlighted — the user loses their 'you are here' anchor as soon as they go one level deep. The label 'CONFERENCES' also points at '/', so the conferences section is never marked active even from its own subpages. → _Use section matching: `pathname === href || (href !== '/' && pathname.startsWith(href))`, with a special case mapping the conferences prefixes to the '/' entry (the `CONFERENCES_PREFIXES` array at :63 already exists for the logo swap and can be reused)._  `[polish]`

## Blog — index cards

- **`src/app/blog/page.tsx:55`** — The 33 index cards are `<Link>` elements with a static inline style and NO hover, focus or active treatment whatsoever — no lift, no border change, no background change. The same is true of the 5 RelatedGuides cards. Compare the benchmark applications page, where every row lifts and re-shadows on hover. → _Give them the NeuCard hover treatment (translateY(-2px) + outHover) or, since they are bordered flat cards, a border-colour shift to #B6871F plus a 1px lift. Cards that do not react are the most common 'is this clickable?' failure._  `[polish]`

## Blog — index

- **`src/app/blog/page.tsx:53`** — 33 cards, all visually identical (24px padding, 16px radius, #FAF8F3, 1px #DDD4C0, 17px/800 title + 14px description), in one flat undifferentiated column with no categories, no tags, no search, no reading time, no date, no images, and no separation between a chair how-to, a delegate tip and a competitor comparison. A chair looking for 'how to run a moderated caucus' must read 33 titles. → _Add a `category` field to `posts.ts` ('For chairs' / 'For delegates' / 'Procedure reference' / 'Comparisons'), group the index under those headings, and surface the reading time that each post already renders in its own byline. Feature the 3 flagship posts at the top with larger cards._  `[hierarchy]`

## Blog — RelatedGuides

- **`src/components/RelatedGuides.tsx:6`** — The heading says 'Related guides' but the selection logic is 'the next 5 posts in manifest order, wrapping' — the file's own comment admits 'No category field exists on posts'. So the awards guide recommends the technology guide. The heading claims a relationship the data cannot support. → _Add tags to posts.ts and select by overlap, or rename the section to 'More guides' until it can be honest. Also, the cards use `backgroundColor: '#EDE7D8'` — DARKER than the `#FAF8F3` article they sit inside, which inverts the app's convention that the raised element is lighter._  `[copy]`

## Blog — body links

- **`src/app/blog/how-to-run-mun-committee/page.tsx:160`** — Inline body links are `style={{ color: '#1B3828', fontWeight: 600 }}` with no underline, inside body text that is #1C1410 and already uses `<strong>` heavily for emphasis. Forest-green bold and ink bold are almost indistinguishable at 16px, so links are effectively invisible in a page whose whole job is internal linking. Colour alone is also a WCAG 1.4.1 failure. → _Add `textDecoration: 'underline'` with `textDecorationColor: 'rgba(27,56,40,0.35)'` and `textUnderlineOffset: 2`, plus a hover that darkens the underline. Move the style into the shared article stylesheet._  `[polish]`

## Blog — article CTA

- **`src/app/blog/how-to-run-mun-committee/page.tsx:193`** — Every post ends with `<a href="https://gavelling.com">Start your committee free at gavelling.com →</a>`. That is an absolute URL causing a full page reload out of the SPA, and gavelling.com is the CONFERENCES landing page — not the session creator. A reader who followed 'start your committee' lands on a conference-discovery page and has to find /create themselves. → _Change to `<Link href="/create">Start your committee free →</Link>` on all 33 posts (one edit once the CTA is a shared component). Repeating the domain name inside your own site is also redundant._  `[copy]`

## Blog — post meta

- **`src/app/blog/how-to-run-mun-committee/page.tsx:66`** — The byline is a hardcoded string per post — 'By Gavelling · June 2026 · 10 min read' — with 9 distinct hand-typed reading times (7–13 min). The JSON-LD alongside it sets `dateModified` equal to `datePublished` on all 33, so Google is told nothing has ever been updated. There is no `<time datetime>` element. → _Move `date` and `readingMinutes` into posts.ts (the index needs them too), render through a `<time dateTime>` element, and compute reading time from word count so it stops being a guess._  `[copy]`

## Blog — long-form articles

- **`src/app/blog/how-to-run-mun-committee/page.tsx:47`** — A 10-minute, 7-numbered-section article has no table of contents, no `id` attributes on any heading (grep: 0 `id=` in the file), no anchor links and no scroll progress. A chair who needs 'Voting Procedures' has to scroll and scan. The sections are already numbered 1–7, so the structure exists but is not exposed. → _Give each `<h2>` an id derived from its text, render a sticky/inline TOC from the same list at the top, and add a hover ¶ anchor. On desktop the 720px column leaves plenty of room for a left rail._  `[wasted-effort]`

## Blog — comparison tables

- **`src/app/blog/best-mun-software-2026/page.tsx:274`** — Feature comparison cells are the bare characters '✓' and '✗' in the default ink colour, with no colour differentiation and no text alternative. Visually a ✓ and a ✗ at 14px in the same colour are easy to confuse when skimming five columns; to a screen reader they announce as 'check mark' / 'multiplication x'. → _Render `<Check color={NEU.green}>` / `<X color="#8B2020">` lucide icons with a visually-hidden 'Yes'/'No' label. The colour is the whole point of a comparison table._  `[polish]`

## Legal pages (/privacy, /terms)

- **`src/components/LegalPage.tsx:205`** — The Disclosure panel animates with `maxHeight: open ? 4000 : 0` over 300ms. Any section taller than 4000px is clipped when open; every section shorter than that opens with a visible easing mismatch (a 400px panel finishes in ~30ms of the 300ms and then sits still). This is the classic max-height-magic-number artefact. → _Use `display: grid; grid-template-rows: 0fr → 1fr` on the wrapper with `overflow: hidden` on the child — it animates to the true content height with no magic number and no clipping ceiling._  `[polish]`

## Legal pages

- **`src/components/LegalPage.tsx:207`** — The comment at :141-143 states the collapsed content stays mounted 'so in-page search (Cmd-F) and crawlers still see every clause'. But the implementation sets `visibility: 'hidden'` plus `maxHeight: 0` + `overflow: hidden` — and browsers' find-in-page skips `visibility: hidden` subtrees. The stated goal is not achieved; the clauses are unfindable by Cmd-F exactly as if they were unmounted. → _Drop `visibility: hidden` (opacity + max-height already hide it visually) and add `hidden="until-found"` to the panel, which is precisely designed for this: findable by Cmd-F, and the browser auto-expands on match. Then wire `onbeforematch` to set `open`._  `[copy]`
- **`src/components/LegalPage.tsx:128`** — Legal body copy is `text-sm` (14px) with `leading-relaxed` inside `max-w-4xl` (896px) — roughly 110 characters per line, well past the 60–75ch readability range, on the two longest documents on the site (privacy is 670 lines). The blog, by comparison, uses 720px at 16px. The two long-form surfaces disagree and the legal one is the worse of the two. → _Narrow the legal `<main>` to `max-w-2xl` (672px) and raise body to 15px, matching the blog's measure. The banner can stay `max-w-4xl` for the wide headline._  `[density]`
- **`src/app/privacy/page.tsx`** — Neither /privacy (670 lines, ~12 numbered sections) nor /terms (358 lines) has a table of contents. The intro copy links to specific sections ('Your rights are in section 07') but there is no index, so someone looking for the data-deletion clause scrolls the whole document. → _Render a jump list from the LegalSection children at the top of `<main>` — the components already take `n`, `title` and `id`, so a `<LegalTOC sections={…} />` is nearly free. Sticky on desktop._  `[wasted-effort]`
- **`src/app/privacy/page.tsx:80`** — Cross-references use three different formats for the same thing: privacy says 'section 07', terms says 'section 5' (terms/page.tsx:32), and the rendered section labels are always zero-padded two-digit ('05', '07' — LegalPage.tsx:122). A reader told to find 'section 5' scans for a heading that reads '05'. → _Always write the padded form in prose, and make the anchors clickable chips that visually match the gold `01`/`02` markers so the link and the target look like the same object._  `[copy]`
- **`src/app/privacy/page.tsx:45`** — The legal pages introduce three more ink/muted shades that exist nowhere in NEU: `#3A2E26` (LegalPage body copy, :78/:128/:214), `#6B5B4B` (privacy SpecRow and RightRow notes, :44/:57) and `#6B5F52` (terms intro, :35). None matches NEU.ink (#1C1410) or the app's common secondary (#6A5A4A). → _Collapse to two: `NEU.ink` for primary and `#6A5A4A` for secondary. Three near-identical browns within one document body is drift, not hierarchy._  `[consistency]`
- **`src/components/LegalPage.tsx:39`** — LegalShell renders `<SiteNav />` with the language switcher visible, but /privacy and /terms are English-only documents. A user who switches to Español gets a translated nav bar sitting above 670 lines of English legal text, and the FooterLegal strip below is also English. → _Pass `hideLanguage` (the prop already exists, SiteNav.tsx:31, and is used for exactly this reason on untranslated conference surfaces) until the legal text is localised, or add a one-line note under the banner: 'Available in English only.'_  `[copy]`

## SiteNav — language menu

- **`src/components/SiteNav.tsx:241`** — The language dropdown is a plain `absolute left-0 mt-1 w-36` div with `zIndex: 50` — not portaled. It is the only floating layer in the shared chrome that ignores the Portal rule. It sits inside `<nav>` on pages whose root is `overflow-x-hidden` (AboutClient.tsx:105, LegalPage.tsx:33), which makes the ancestor a scroll container and can clip it; it also does not reposition on scroll and cannot flip near a viewport edge. → _Port it to the DatePicker/ProfileDropdown pattern: `getBoundingClientRect()` → `<Portal>` → `position: fixed` with left-clamping and up-flip. ProfileDropdown (same file, rendered 15 lines below) already does exactly this and can be copied verbatim._  `[rule-violation]`
- **`src/components/SiteNav.tsx:229`** — The language trigger has no `aria-expanded`, no `aria-haspopup`, the panel has no `role="menu"`, there is no Escape-to-close (only `mousedown` outside), no arrow-key navigation between the four options, and the trigger + all four options carry `focus:outline-none` with no replacement. The active language is marked only by a '✓' glyph at #B6871F plus a weight change. → _Add aria-expanded/haspopup/role=menu + menuitem, Escape and arrow handling, a visible focus ring, and mark the current language with `aria-current` in addition to the tick._  `[polish]`

## SiteNav — header

- **`src/components/SiteNav.tsx:99`** — The header is split across two independent layers with different scroll behaviour: the centred pill nav is `fixed top-0` (stays), while the logo, language toggle, credit chip, account button and SIGN IN live in a `relative` `<nav>` (scrolls away). Scroll down 100px on any marketing page and you are left with a floating tab bar and no logo, no account and no sign-in — half a header. → _Make the whole header one fixed bar (with a scrolled-state background), or make the pill scroll away with everything else. Splitting them means the two halves visually separate on scroll, which reads as a bug._  `[hierarchy]`

## SiteNav — credit chip

- **`src/components/SiteNav.tsx:301`** — The '✨ NEW' badge is `fontSize: '7px'` at `top: -8px` on the credit chip. 7px is below any legible threshold and it is permanent — no dismissal, no expiry date, no condition — so the chip will announce itself as new forever. → _Raise it to 9px minimum with more padding, and gate it: show it only until the user first visits /account/unlimited, or behind a hardcoded end date. A badge that never goes away stops meaning 'new' within a week._  `[polish]`
- **`src/components/SiteNav.tsx:319`** — The chip renders a gavel icon and a bare number with no label and no `title`. A new user sees '🔨 3' in the header with no way to learn what it counts. Worse, `creditsLoading || creditBalance === null` both render the same '—', so a genuinely-unknown balance is indistinguishable from a still-loading one. → _Add `title="Credits — 1 credit runs one committee session"` (or a hover card), and split the states: a SkeletonBlock while loading, '—' with a tooltip only for the genuine unknown._  `[copy]`

## SiteNav — desktop right actions

- **`src/components/SiteNav.tsx:282`** — When signed in, the credit chip and the account button are rendered as two adjacent pills with identical treatment: `backgroundColor: '#1B3828'`, `borderRadius: '9999px'`, `padding: '7px 14px'`, `fontSize: '13px'`, `fontWeight: 700`, `letterSpacing: '0.04em'` and the same `#2A5A3C` hover. Two visually identical forest capsules side by side with different meanings. → _Demote the credit chip to a lighter treatment — outline or `rgba(27,56,40,0.07)` fill with forest text — so the account button is unambiguously the primary control and the counter reads as ambient status._  `[hierarchy]`

## SiteNav — mobile menu

- **`src/components/SiteNav.tsx:459`** — The mobile language row is four `flex-1` buttons in one row inside `px-2`, each labelled `EN: English` / `ES: Español` / `FR: Français` / `AR: العربية` at `text-sm` (14px). At 375px that is ~78px per button for an 11-character string — the labels will overflow or clip, with no truncate and no wrap control. → _Use a 2×2 grid, or show just the code + flag with the full name only for the active one. Four full language names never fit on one phone row._  `[responsive]`
- **`src/components/SiteNav.tsx:422`** — The mobile menu animates `maxHeight: menuOpen ? '480px' : '0px'` with `overflow-hidden`. When signed in the content is 4 nav links (4×~45px) + divider + language row (~50px) + divider + name/email block (~52px) + credits row (~44px) + sign-out (~46px) + paddings ≈ 520px+. The sign-out button is clipped off on smaller phones and cannot be scrolled to. → _Replace the max-height animation with a grid `0fr → 1fr` transition (no ceiling), or set `maxHeight: '80vh'` with `overflow-y: auto`._  `[responsive]`

## SiteNav — copy

- **`src/components/SiteNav.tsx:378`** — The nav labels are localised through NAV_LINKS_CONFIG, but 'SIGN IN' (:378, :556), 'SIGN OUT' (:534), 'Credits & Subscription' (:512) and the 'Account' fallback (:360) are hardcoded English inside the same component. A French user gets 'SESSIONS · CONFÉRENCES · QUI SOMMES-NOUS · CONTACT' next to 'SIGN IN'. → _Route all four through `t()`. Half-localised chrome is more jarring than none._  `[copy]`

## SiteNav — sign in

- **`src/components/SiteNav.tsx:375`** — SIGN IN's hover is `onMouseEnter` → `style.textDecoration = 'underline'` in JS, and it carries `focus:outline-none` with no focus style. So the only signed-out call to action has a JS-driven hover, no keyboard focus indication, and no touch/active state. → _Use CSS `hover:underline` + a `focus-visible` ring. Better: make it a real button-shaped affordance — as the sole conversion action for a signed-out visitor it is currently the quietest thing in the header, quieter than the greyed LinkedIn icon in the footer._  `[polish]`

## ProfileDropdown

- **`src/components/ProfileDropdown.tsx:367`** — The 'YOUR CONFERENCES' loading placeholder is two hand-rolled static divs (`backgroundColor: 'rgba(27,56,40,0.08)'`, `height: '9px'`, widths 55%/70%) with no shimmer, while `SkeletonBlock` exists in the codebase for exactly this and provides the `.neu-shimmer` sweep plus the reduced-motion fallback. → _Swap both placeholder divs for `<SkeletonBlock height={9} radius={4} width="55%" />`. Same shape, one shared shimmer, one reduced-motion story._  `[consistency]`
- **`src/components/ProfileDropdown.tsx:407`** — The panel uses 8px, 9px, 10px, 11px and 12px type in one 280px-wide menu (`fontSize: '8px'` for the role badge at :407, `'9px'` for the conference date at :420, `'10px'` for the section header at :357, `'11px'` for the see-all link, `'12px'` for the menu rows), with the 9–10px lines in #9A8A78. Five type sizes and sub-legible minimums in a single component. → _Collapse to three sizes (11 / 12.5 / 14) and move the 9–10px muted text to #6A5A4A. 8px is not a font size, it is a decoration._  `[contrast]`

## grain-dev

- **`src/app/grain-dev/page.tsx:10`** — A developer tuning page ships at a public route. It is disallowed in robots.ts:32, but it is still reachable, unlinked, unstyled against the design system, and its `isDark` state is inverted (`isDark: true` produces the LIGHT palette `#F6F1E9`/`#FFFFFF`, :19-23), so the tool itself is mislabelled. It also references `/background-grain.png`, an asset nothing else uses. → _Delete the route and the unused PNG, or move it behind a dev-only guard. Once the grain becomes a shared `<Grain>` component this page has no reason to exist._  `[consistency]`

## Design system — hint affordances

- **`src/components/SettingsPanel.tsx:105`** — Two hover-hint components exist and both are correct per the AGENTS.md hover rule (portaled, onMouseEnter/onFocus, close delay) — but they look completely different. SettingsPanel's is a 13px circle with a #C5B9A8 border and #9A8A78 'i', opening a forest #1B3828 panel with #EED98A text at 11px, rounded-xl. VotingRulesPanel's InfoDot (:263) is a 15px circle on rgba(255,255,255,0.10) with a gold active state, opening a 230×116 panel. Alongside them sit 169 native `title=""` tooltips with OS styling and a ~1s delay. → _Promote one — VotingRulesPanel's InfoDot is the more complete (role, aria-describedby, Escape, scroll reposition, `cursor: help`) — into neu.tsx as `NeuHint`, retheme it for light surfaces, and use it everywhere. Convert the `title` attributes that carry real explanation; keep `title` only for one-word clarifications._  `[consistency]`

## Design system — empty states

- **`src/components/Skeleton.tsx:9`** — Skeleton.tsx's header comment lays down a rule ('An empty state is a conclusion about data that has actually resolved… never render an empty state as a stand-in for still loading'), but there is no `NeuEmptyState` primitive to render the conclusion with. Every surface hand-rolls its own empty copy and layout, so there is no shared icon size, no shared muted colour, no shared CTA placement. → _Add `<NeuEmptyState icon gradient title body action />` to neu.tsx (an IconDisc + 15px title + 12.5px muted body + optional NeuButton) and adopt it. Loading and empty are the two states most likely to be seen by a first-run user and neither currently has a house look._  `[consistency]`

## Design system — opacity

- **`src/components/neu.tsx`** — Opacity is used as an ad-hoc dimming token with 20 distinct values across the codebase (0.18 ×29, 0.5 ×21, 0.55 ×16, 0.6 ×15, 0.07 ×8, 0.85 ×7, 0.45 ×7, 0.045 ×7, 0.04 ×6, 0.4 ×5, 0.9 ×4, 0.7 ×4, 0.05 ×4, 0.82, 0.75, 0.72, 0.35, 0.12, 0.95, 0.8). Disabled controls variously use 0.38 (Contact submit), 0.45 (About submit), 0.4 (VotingRulesPanel RuleToggle) and 0.55 (ConfirmModal checkbox) — four different 'disabled' opacities. → _Define exactly three: `disabled: 0.5`, `secondary: 0.7`, `ghost: 0.08` (for tint fills), and sweep. Then stop using opacity for disabled text entirely — set a muted colour instead so contrast stays predictable._  `[consistency]`

## Design system — scrollbars

- **`src/app/globals.css:84`** — The custom scrollbar is 6px wide with a `#DDD4C0` thumb on an `#EDE7D8` track — a contrast ratio of about 1.15:1, i.e. effectively invisible. Users cannot tell that a panel scrolls, and cannot easily grab the thumb at 6px. There is also no `scrollbar-width`/`scrollbar-color` declaration, so Firefox gets the default OS scrollbar instead, making the two browsers look different. → _Widen to 8–10px, darken the thumb to `rgba(27,56,40,0.22)` (the value TutorialOverlay.tsx:377 already uses for exactly this), and add the Firefox standard properties: `scrollbar-width: thin; scrollbar-color: rgba(27,56,40,0.22) #EDE7D8`._  `[contrast]`

## Design system — dead CSS

- **`src/app/globals.css:180`** — `@keyframes skeleton-pulse` is declared and never referenced anywhere in src/ (the real shimmer is `neu-shimmer` at :248). Dead animation left over from a previous skeleton implementation. → _Delete it._  `[polish]`

> **Method / limits:** METHOD — every finding comes from reading source; no dev server was started and no browser tool was used, per the brief. Coverage: src/components/neu.tsx in full; all 14 files in src/components/ui/ (button/badge/input read in full, the rest inspected for importers); the named shared primitives Portal, DatePicker, ConfirmModal, Toast, Skeleton, FlagImg, Emoji, LogoDisc, FitToScreen, MobileGate, SiteNav, Brand, FooterLegal, SessionsHeaderLogo, DecorativeBleed in full, plus ProfileDropdown (rendered by SiteNav); src/app/globals.css and src/app/layout.tsx in full; /about (AboutClient), /contact (ContactClient), /privacy, /terms + src/components/LegalPage.tsx; /blog index, posts.ts, RelatedGuides, and 2 posts read in full (how-to-run-mun-committee, best-mun-software-2026) with the remaining 31 sampled by grepping every shared style key — sufficient to prove the 33 `s` objects are byte-identical and to find the one drifted callout.

COUNTS reported are from grep over `src/**/*.{ts,tsx,css}` and are reproducible: 200 distinct 6-digit hexes; 134 distinct inline boxShadow literals; 24 distinct numeric borderRadius values + 20 distinct Tailwind rounded-* classes; 33 distinct inline fontSize values; 25 distinct letterSpacing values; 6 font weights; 843 `focus:outline-none` (90 files with no focus-visible/ring anywhere) vs 35 `focus-visible`; 95 `transition-all` + 10 inline `transition: all`; NEU imported by 61 of 241 .tsx files; grain data-URI duplicated in 24 files (33 occurrences, 3 opacity variants, 2 blend modes).

THE HEADLINE. The single most consequential item is that **Outfit is never loaded** (finding 1). I checked layout.tsx, globals.css, every layout.tsx under src/app, next.config.ts, package.json, node_modules/shadcn/dist/tailwind.css, public/ and the whole repo for @font-face / next/font / a woff file — nothing. Because the 755 inline declarations say `'Outfit', sans-serif` while globals.css says `'Outfit', system-ui, sans-serif`, the page currently renders in two unintended faces simultaneously. Everything else in this audit is downstream of that: it is not worth doing type-scale work until the typeface actually ships. DM Mono and Poppins are in the same state.

COVERAGE LIMITS. I did not audit HomeClient/StagefrontClient, the session cockpit pages, /create, /join, or the conferences and manage areas beyond the grep sweeps used for the codebase-wide token counts — those belong to other agents' areas. The manage-area popover/modal counts I cite (21 hand-rolled dialogs, 4 toggle geometries) are grep-derived headcounts to size the "missing shared primitive" problem, not per-file critiques of Christian's code. Contrast figures are computed from the sRGB relative-luminance formula, not measured in a browser: #9A8A78 on #EDE7D8 = 2.72:1, #6A5A4A on #EDE7D8 = 5.36:1, #DDD4C0 on #EDE7D8 = ~1.15:1.

INCIDENTAL NON-UI OBSERVATIONS (not findings, for whoever owns them):
- src/app/grain-dev/page.tsx:19-23 — the `isDark` boolean is inverted: `isDark === true` yields the light palette (#F6F1E9 background, #FFFFFF text). The tool's own toggle is mislabelled.
- src/app/about/AboutClient.tsx:93 — `handleSubmit` awaits the `ambassador_applications` insert and never inspects the returned error, so a rejected write still renders the success screen. Applications can be silently lost.
- src/components/neu.tsx:569 — `gradient === NEU_GRADIENTS.gold` is reference equality on an array. Ten call sites already pass computed gradients; a caller passing an equal-valued literal gets gold text on gold. Latent, not yet shipped.
- src/components/LegalPage.tsx:141-143 — the comment asserts collapsed clauses remain findable by Cmd-F; `visibility: hidden` prevents that in Chrome/Safari. The behaviour and the documented intent disagree.
- src/components/MobileGate.tsx:13 — the gate is user-agent based and never re-runs, so iPads and Android tablets are blocked from the session UI outright while a 900px desktop window is allowed.
- src/app/sitemap.ts enumerates blog URLs by hand while src/app/blog/posts.ts is described as "single source of truth" — the two will drift on the next post.



---

# Live visual pass — driving the real app in a browser (chair, co-chair, delegate mobile, voting, join, landing) at 1280×800, 1512×1300 and 390×844  
_88 findings_


## Delegate — mobile header (390×844)

- **`src/app/delegate/[code]/page.tsx:1134`** — At 390px every tab label is truncated to one letter: the bar renders literally "S… D… C… S…". Measured in the live DOM: each tab button is 35px wide (`flex-1 min-w-0` + `<span className="truncate">`), because the GAVELLING wordmark eats ~300px and the GJ6U5H code chip another 65px of the 390px row. Two of the four tabs start with "S" (Session, Stats) so even guessing is impossible. 35px is also well under the 44px tap-target minimum. → _On <640px collapse the wordmark to the gavel mark only (~40px), drop the session-code chip entirely (a delegate already in the session never needs the join code), and move the four tabs to a fixed bottom bar with icon + full label. Failing that, show icons with the label underneath and never truncate below 3 characters._  `[responsive]`

## Chair — pre-session roll call

- **`src/components/RollCallPanel.tsx:669`** — Absent delegate rows are dimmed twice: the row wrapper carries `opacity: 0.35` and the name span adds `opacity-50` on top, giving an effective 0.175. #EDE7D8 text at 17.5% over the #1B3828 card computes to ~rgb(64,87,71) — a measured contrast ratio of 1.63:1. During roll call every delegate starts absent, so the very first operational screen of the app is effectively unreadable until the chair blindly taps rows. → _Pick one dimming layer, not two, and floor it at 4.5:1 — e.g. keep the row at full opacity and set the absent name colour to a fixed rgba(237,231,216,0.62) (≈4.6:1), reserving the opacity change for the flag/slider only._  `[contrast]`

## Chair — sidebar stat rings (all phases)

- **`src/components/RollCallPanel.tsx:553`** — Three donut rings render "10 7 6" with zero labels, zero `title`, zero `aria-label` (verified in the DOM). From the source they are: present count, ⅔ threshold, simple-majority threshold — and `MajorityPie` takes a FIXED `arcFill` (1, 2/3, 0.5) that has no relationship to the number beside it. So during roll call the first ring showed a fully-closed green circle next to the number "0", which reads as "everyone is here". → _Replace the three unlabelled donuts with one line of text — "10 present · 7 for ⅔ · 6 for majority" — or keep the arcs but put a 9px caption under each (PRESENT / ⅔ / 50%+1) and make ring 1's arc actually track present/total._  `[hierarchy]`

## Chair — moderated caucus at 1512×1300

- **`src/app/chair/[code]/page.tsx`** — Confirmed text collision by measuring getBoundingClientRect at 1512×1300: the caucus topic block sits at x 583–812 / y 582–674 and the "No Current Speaker" headline at x 673–1397 / y 581–657 — a 139×75px overlap. On screen the words "dual-use orbital" are struck through by the giant headline. Does not happen at 1280×800. → _Stop free-positioning the caucus identity block beside a centred headline. Put "MODERATED CAUCUS / topic" in the stage's own header row above the speaker area (full width, left-aligned), or give the centre column an explicit `margin-inline-start` equal to the caucus block's width so the two can never share a band._  `[responsive]`

## Chair — GSL, Add Time popover

- **`src/app/chair/[code]/page.tsx:3380`** — The Add Time popover is `className="fixed z-50"` with `style={{ top:'50%', right:'2rem', transform:'translateY(-50%)' }}` and it is rendered INSIDE `#fit-root` (verified: `inFitRoot: true`, `position: fixed`), which carries `transform: matrix(0.97561…)`. It is not portaled through @/components/Portal, so it is neither anchored to its trigger nor positioned in true viewport space: on screen it landed ~200px above and ~150px to the right of the ADD TIME button, floating over the progress bar with no tail. AGENTS.md UI RULE requires portaled fixed layers computed from the trigger's getBoundingClientRect with flip/clamp — GavelChip does this correctly and is the in-repo model. → _Port the Add Time and Right-of-Reply overlays onto GavelChip's pattern: Portal + `fitScale()`-divided coordinates from the trigger rect, with edge clamping and an arrow pointing at the button._  `[rule-violation]`

## Chair — GSL, add-speaker typeahead

- **`src/app/chair/[code]/page.tsx`** — The add-speaker suggestion list is `absolute bottom-full left-0 right-0 … z-10` inside the input wrapper — not portaled. Live it overlays and completely hides the TIME preset row (45s/60s/75s/90s + the custom seconds box) that sits directly above it, so the chair loses the time controls the moment they start typing a name. Same pattern in the MotionsModal "Proposed by" field, where the dropdown covers the Total time / per-delegate fields and is 33px narrower than the input it belongs to (right edges at 1354 vs 1387 — visibly misaligned). → _Route both typeaheads through @/components/Portal at fixed viewport coordinates derived from the input rect, matching the input's exact width, and flip above/below based on available space._  `[rule-violation]`

## Chair — GSL, current speaker card

- **`src/app/chair/[code]/page.tsx`** — The current-speaker flag is a ~200×140 rounded rect with a light tan border on the #EDE7D8 stage. For any flag with a white band — France, Italy, Nigeria, Ireland, Peru — the white stripe merges into the page and the flag reads as two disconnected colour blocks with a gap. France looked broken on screen. → _Give the hero flag an inner hairline (`box-shadow: inset 0 0 0 1px rgba(28,20,16,0.18)`) plus a 1px #1C1410/20 outer border, exactly as the sidebar FlagCircle already does — the sidebar version does not have this problem._  `[polish]`

## Chair — GSL, speaker control row

- **`src/app/chair/[code]/page.tsx:1076`** — Five controls, five treatments, measured live: restart ↺ (tan square, 47px), ▶ START (#2A5A3C fill, 124×47, 16px), NEXT → (#DDD4C0 fill, 108×47, 14px), ADD TIME (#EDE7D8, 51×47, **8px font**, label force-wrapped onto two lines via `t('gsl_add_time').split('\n')`), and RIGHT TO REPLY orphaned on a second row (130×41, 12px, #B8844A on a 15% amber wash ≈2.6:1 contrast). Three font sizes, four fills, two rows, no shared baseline. → _Normalise to one 44px control height and one 13px label size; give ADD TIME an icon + single-word label ("+ Time"); move Right of Reply into the same row as a tertiary ghost button; and darken its label to #8A5A2E so it clears 4.5:1._  `[consistency]`

## Chair — GSL, speaker timer

- **`src/app/chair/[code]/page.tsx:1043`** — The hero timer computes to `font-family: ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, Menlo, Monaco, Consolas…` at 80px — a system monospace stack, not OUTFIT. It is the single largest element on the chair's main screen and it is the only thing on the page set in a system font. Same for the caucus timer (text-9xl font-mono, :729), the CoW timer (:802) and the delegate caucus timer. → _Set every timer in OUTFIT with `font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums` (Outfit has proper tabular figures), or if a mono look is deliberate, load one webfont for it and use it consistently — never fall through to Menlo/Consolas, which changes face per OS._  `[consistency]`

## Chair — running speaker timer

Composited over the ivory page that resolves to ~#C89B37; white on that is 2.57:1 — a fail for a 16px bold label, and it is the primary transport control of the whole session. → _Use forest #1B3828 with gold #EED98A text for PAUSE (matching START's weight), and signal the running state with the icon (▶/⏸) plus the progress bar, not by swapping to a low-contrast gold fill._  `[contrast]`

## Chair — GSL progress bar

- **`src/app/chair/[code]/page.tsx`** — The speaker progress bar is a 7.8px flat #DDD4C0 rail with `box-shadow: none` and a solid gold fill — no inset track, no gradient. Same for the caucus TOTAL bar, the CoW bar and the RTR bar. → _Replace all four session progress bars with `<NeuProgress>` so the track reads as a pressed-in well and the fill uses NEU_GRADIENTS.gold — one component, four fewer bespoke bars._  `[consistency]`

## Create — New committee form

- **`src/app/create/page.tsx`** — The "PASTE COUNTRY LIST" textarea is ~350px tall and is the largest element on the page, dominating the left column above the fold — while the intended primary path (Search & Add + Quick Bundles) sits above it at a fraction of the size. After delegates are added the giant box stays, empty, for the rest of the flow. Its placeholder is also four lines of realistic-looking content ("France / Germany / Brazil, India… / Observer Delegation") that reads as pre-filled data. → _Collapse the paste box to a 3-row textarea behind a "Paste a list" disclosure under the search field, and replace the multi-line placeholder with a single line: "One delegation per line"._  `[hierarchy]`

## Create — Selected delegates list

- **`src/app/create/page.tsx:839`** — The per-row remove ✕ is `opacity-0 group-hover:opacity-100` — invisible until mouse hover, therefore unreachable on any touch device. Its hover colour is `text-red-500` (#EF4444), a Tailwind default that exists nowhere in the NEU palette. → _Show the ✕ at rest at 45% opacity (full on hover/focus), give it a 44×44 hit area, and use #8B2020 (the crimson already used elsewhere in the app) instead of red-500._  `[missing-state]`
- **`src/app/create/page.tsx`** — The per-row observer toggle is a **megaphone** icon (lucide-megaphone) with only `title="Mark as observer"` and `aria-pressed`. A megaphone means "speak/broadcast"; an observer is precisely the delegation that may not vote. The same megaphone glyph is reused in Chat for the "Everyone" broadcast thread, so one icon carries two unrelated meanings. There is also no visible on-state — pressed and unpressed are both #9A8A78. → _Use an eye icon for observer, give the active state a filled forest disc, and add a visible "OBS" chip on the row so the state survives a glance._  `[copy]`

## Create — Review allocations modal

- **`src/app/create/page.tsx:908`** — The per-row remove ✕ measures 10×20px (measured live) — roughly a fifth of the 44×44 minimum — and again uses `hover:text-red-500`. Nine rows also each carry an identical "COUNTRY" badge; the badge only carries information when it says CUSTOM, so nine of ten badges are pure noise. → _Wrap the ✕ in a 40×40 button, and badge only the exceptions (show "CUSTOM" on unmatched rows, nothing on matched ones)._  `[polish]`
- **`src/app/create/page.tsx`** — Pasting "United States of America" and "Russian Federation" silently renames them to "United States" and "Russia" with no indication a rename happened, while "Republic of Korea" — a real UN member-state name — fails to match at all and drops to CUSTOM with a generic globe. Matched rows are plain text (not editable); only the failed row gets an input, so a chair who wants "Russian Federation" back cannot fix it here. → _Add the common official long forms (Republic of Korea, DPRK, Russian Federation, USA, UK…) to the matcher, show "United States of America → United States" as strike-through-plus-result on renamed rows, and make every row's name editable, not just the unmatched ones._  `[wasted-effort]`

## Create / Chair / Landing — disabled buttons

- **`src/app/create/page.tsx:754`** — Four different disabled conventions were observed in one session: `disabled:opacity-30` (create page AUTO-MATCH), `disabled:opacity-40` (HomeClient JOIN, :722), `disabled:bg-[#DDD4C0] disabled:text-[#9A8A78]` (chair CALL FIRST SPEAKER — measured 2.1:1), and a full-colour button with `cursor: default` (create page START SESSION, which is enabled but never shows a pointer). #DDD4C0 is also the *enabled* fill of the chair's NEXT button, so the same tan means both "press me" and "you can't press me". → _Define one disabled token (e.g. bg #E4DDCB, text #7A6A58 at ≈4.6:1, cursor not-allowed) and one enabled-secondary token that is visibly different from it; add `cursor: pointer` to every enabled button._  `[consistency]`

The two most consequential actions in the app — suspending and ending a session — are the smallest, oddest-looking controls in the modal, at half the tap-target minimum. → _Move Suspend / End Debate out of the type grid into a separate labelled "Procedural" row below the caucus grid, at the same 44px height as everything else, using the forest outline treatment with a crimson label rather than a pink fill._  `[hierarchy]`

Only Topic carries the required asterisk, yet the CTA stays disabled until a proposer is chosen, with no indication which field is blocking it. → _One label colour (#1C1410) at one size; rename "/ delegate" to "Time per speaker"; mark both required fields with the asterisk; and change the disabled CTA label to name the missing field ("Add a topic to continue")._  `[consistency]`

- **`src/components/MotionsModal.tsx`** — Read-only computed values are styled exactly like editable inputs: "10 delegates can speak" and "Simple majority · Needs 6 of 10" are both white boxes with a border and a large bold number, identical to the "10 min" / "60 sec" fields beside them. A chair will try to type in them. → _Render computed readouts as plain text on the surface (no box, no border, muted label + ink value), reserving the boxed treatment exclusively for things you can type into._  `[consistency]`
