## Chair — Documents modal, empty state

- **`src/components/DocumentsModal.tsx:1029`** — "No Working Papers yet. / Submit the first one below." is separated from its CTA by ~90px of nothing, and the CTA "+ Submit New Working Paper" is a full-width cream ghost bar with a hairline border — the weakest control on the screen is the only action on the screen, and it reads as a drop zone. The modal title "Documents" is also 28px sentence case while the sibling Motions modal uses 36px all-caps black — two title styles for two modals opened from the same nav row. → _Make the empty-state CTA a filled forest button directly under the sub-line (24px gap, not 90px), and unify modal titles on one style._  `[hierarchy]`

## Chair — Documents modal

- **`src/components/DocumentsModal.tsx`** — The chair-side button says "+ Submit New Working Paper". Chairs do not submit working papers — delegates do; the chair is adding one on a delegation's behalf. "Submit" also collides with the delegate-side "SUBMIT TO CHAIR" action. → _Chair-side label: "+ Add a working paper" (with a delegation selector in the form). Keep "Submit to chair" for the delegate side only._  `[copy]`

## Chair — Scoreboard, Ranking tab

- **`src/components/ScoreboardPanel.tsx`** — Nine of ten delegations scored exactly 5 points, yet the list renders a hard 1…10 ordering with no tie indication — a chair glancing at it concludes Brazil (rank 2) outperformed the United States (rank 10) when they are identical. The score badge is also the same grey-green pill for the leader (15) as for everyone else, so nothing marks the top score. → _Render tied ranks as "=2" for every member of a tie and sort ties alphabetically; give the top-scoring row a gold badge and a subtle NEU.in seat._  `[missing-state]`
- **`src/components/ScoreboardPanel.tsx`** — "Export CSV" is the only saturated gold pill on the panel, in the header beside the ✕ — a utility action given more visual weight than the ranking it sits above. The per-row expand chevron "›" is a ~10px muted glyph at the far right, so the one interaction that reveals a delegate's point breakdown is nearly invisible. → _Demote Export CSV to a ghost/outline button; make the row itself the click target with a hover lift, and enlarge the chevron to 16px at #6A5A4A._  `[hierarchy]`

## Chair — Scoreboard, Matrix tab

- **`src/components/ScoreboardPanel.tsx`** — Nine column headers with no legend and no tooltips: GSL, Cauc, Time, Mot, RTR, WP, DR, ±, Total. "Cauc" is a truncation rather than an abbreviation, "±" is completely opaque, and the casing is inconsistent (three all-caps acronyms, two title-case words). With one motion raised the grid is 90 cells of which 88 are "0". → _Spell headers out (Speeches / Caucus / Time / Motions / Right of reply / Working papers / Draft resolutions / Adjustment / Total) with hover explainers, and render zeros as a muted "–" so the non-zero cells pop._  `[copy]`

## Chair — Scoreboard modal

- **`src/components/ScoreboardPanel.tsx`** — Switching Ranking → Matrix changes the modal from 935×745 to 935×580 and, because it is vertically centred, the whole panel jumps upward ~83px — the header moved from y=152 to y=235 between screenshots. The tab control you just clicked moves out from under the cursor. → _Pin the modal to a fixed height (or a min-height equal to the taller tab) so switching tabs swaps content without moving the chrome._  `[polish]`

## Chair — Chat

- **`src/components/chat/ChatThread.tsx`** — Chat is the only surface that takes over the entire viewport including the left sidebar — Motions, Documents, Scoreboard and Settings are all overlays. A chair who opens chat while a delegate is speaking loses the timer, the queue and the roster completely. The thread pane itself is then ~830×800px of empty cream with a 60px gold disc and two lines of text dead-centre. → _Render chat as a right-hand drawer (like Settings) at ~420px so the speaker card and queue stay visible, or at minimum keep the speaker/timer strip pinned above the chat pane._  `[hierarchy]`
- **`src/components/chat/ChatConversationList.tsx`** — The conversation column shows one item at the top and pins "+ New message" to the very bottom of an otherwise empty 780px column — the action that adds to the list is ~750px away from the list. "+ New message" is also the same pale cream ghost pill as the Documents empty-state CTA, i.e. the weakest possible treatment for a primary action. → _Move "+ New message" directly under the MESSAGES heading as a filled forest button, and fill the dead column space with a compact roster the chair can DM in one click._  `[density]`
- **`src/components/chat/ChatComposer.tsx:59`** — The send button is a 35×35 circle filled #DDD4C0 with a "↑" glyph — the same tan used for disabled buttons elsewhere in the app, so the send affordance reads as unavailable at rest. 35px is also under the 44px tap-target minimum. The thread header's "i" affordance is a ~12px muted glyph pinned at the extreme right edge, ~600px from the thread title it annotates. → _Make send 44×44, forest-filled with gold arrow when the field has content and a muted outline when empty; move the thread-info "i" next to the conversation name._  `[polish]`

## Chair — session header

- **`src/app/chair/[code]/page.tsx`** — The header renders the full committee name AND the topic concatenated with a colon in a single 12px #9A8A78 truncating span: "ZZTEST Disarmament and International Security Committee: Non-proliferation of nuclear weapons in outer space and the militarisation of low earth orbit". The AGENTS.md acronym rule (ACRONYM large, full name small beneath) is honoured in the sidebar ("DISEC" + full name) but not here. The header is also `hidden sm:block`, so on narrow screens the committee identity vanishes entirely. Meanwhile the /voting page header renders the same name full-length at 16px bold ink — three different renderings of the committee identity across three surfaces. → _Run every committee label through `committeeDisplayName()`: acronym at 13px ink, topic on a second muted line, and never concatenate the two with a colon. Keep the treatment identical on chair, voting, delegate and join._  `[rule-violation]`
- **`src/app/chair/[code]/page.tsx`** — The header uses `bg-[#FAF8F3]` and `border-[#DDD4C0]` — a fourth and fifth ivory outside the NEU tokens (base #EDE7D8, surface #F0EBDD). The three header controls (session code chip, trophy, gear) are unlabelled: the code chip has no "Code" label and no copy affordance, and the trophy/gear are bare 22px lucide strokes in #9A8A78 with only a `title`, giving 22×22 hit areas. → _Move #FAF8F3/#DDD4C0 into the NEU token set (or replace with surface/base), label the code chip "CODE GJ6U5H" with a copy icon, and wrap the two icon buttons in 40×40 hit areas with visible hover seats._  `[polish]`

## Chair — top nav row

- **`src/app/chair/[code]/page.tsx`** — "Roll Call | Motions | Documents | Chat" are four equal-width flex cells across ~1100px, so labels sit ~150px apart with thin 1px dividers between them — the bar reads as very sparse, and all four are styled identically despite behaving differently (Roll Call toggles the sidebar, the other three open overlays; Chat replaces the whole page). Three further tab patterns exist elsewhere: underline (Settings), filled-pill segmented (Documents), and outlined-pill (Scoreboard). → _Group the four into a centred pill group of intrinsic width (like the marketing nav), and settle on one tab component for the whole app._  `[hierarchy]`

## Chair — sidebar roster & queue

- **`src/components/RollCallPanel.tsx`** — Roster rows are 57–73px tall for a flag plus one word, so only ~9 of 10 delegations fit at 800px, and the list is hard-cut mid-row by the opaque filter bar at the bottom — "United States" is sliced in half with no fade or gradient mask. The same hard mid-row cut appears in the pre-session roll-call card and the /voting roll-call card, so it reads as a systemic finishing flaw. → _Drop row height to ~44px, and add a 24px `mask-image: linear-gradient(to top, transparent, black)` fade at the bottom of every scroll container so the list visibly continues rather than being guillotined._  `[density]`

## Chair — sidebar queue badges

- **`src/components/RollCallPanel.tsx:661`** — The currently-speaking delegate gets a ★ star badge over their flag while everyone else gets a number. A star conventionally means favourite/bookmark; AGENTS.md itself specifies a 🎙 microphone badge for the current speaker. The badge also sits at `-top-1 -right-1` so it clips the corner of the flag circle. → _Use the microphone glyph (or a small gold ring around the flag) for the current speaker, and inset the badge so it doesn't crop the flag._  `[copy]`

## Chair — GSL up-next strip

- **`src/app/chair/[code]/page.tsx`** — The queue is a row of flag tiles with names beneath, but only the first is labelled ("Up next"); the rest carry no position number, so the strip does not read as an ordered queue. The current speaker is duplicated — shown both in the strip (labelled "Speaking") and as the giant hero card 200px below. And the empty-state sub-line "Add delegates below, then call the first speaker." persists unchanged after four delegates have been added, and again inside a caucus. → _Number every tile 1..n, force single-line names with truncation + title, drop the current speaker from the strip (the hero card already owns it), and make the sub-line state-aware ("4 speakers queued · ~6 min of debate")._  `[hierarchy]`

## Chair — caucus footer

## Chair — caucus / all phases

## Chair — End Debate confirmation

- **`src/app/chair/[code]/page.tsx`** — "DOES THIS MOTION PASS?" is a full-screen ivory takeover with ~350px of blank above and below, for one binary question that permanently ends the committee. YES (green) and NO (crimson) are identical 220×115 slabs with 32px radius — a sixth radius value — so the irreversible option carries equal, arguably friendlier, weight. There is no summary of the consequence, no vote tally, and no visible cancel/back affordance. The only context, "END DEBATE · Japan", is 13px mono #9A8A78 (~2.9:1) — the least legible text on the screen. → _Put the consequence in the copy ("Ending debate closes the committee permanently. You'll follow along and can take over at any time."_  `[contrast]`

## Landing — /sessions hero

- **`src/app/HomeClient.tsx:650`** — The H1 is `clamp(48px, 13.5vw, 165px)` in pure white with `text-shadow: none` over a *light* gavel photograph. The brightest region of the image sits directly behind the word "MUN", so the M and N almost vanish. The subhead beneath is wrapped in a `rgba(246,241,233,0.55)` blur pill — an admission that the background is too busy — which makes the copy read as a UI chip rather than a sentence. → _Darken the hero with a forest-tinted gradient scrim behind the text block (or shift the crop so the headline sits over the dark base of the gavel), then drop the blur pill from the subhead._  `[contrast]`

## Landing — /sessions join box

- **`src/app/HomeClient.tsx:722`** — The JOIN button is `disabled` with `disabled:opacity-40` while the code field is empty, so on first paint the forest #1B3828 fill washes to a pale sage and the gold label nearly disappears — measured live as `opacity: 0.4, disabled: true`. Sitting immediately below the fully-opaque "START YOUR COMMITTEE →", the page's second CTA looks broken on arrival. The frosted join panel also lets the photo's olive wreath show through, further muddying it. → _Keep JOIN enabled and validate on submit (or use a solid muted fill instead of opacity), and raise the frosted panel's opacity so the wreath stops reading through the controls._  `[missing-state]`

## Landing — / conference cards

- **`src/app/conferences/StagefrontClient.tsx`** — Each conference card carries a gold gavel pin badge half-overhanging its top-right corner (the same corner-straddling pattern as the Motions position badge) — three of them stacked down the right edge, decorative but reading as a status or control. Inside each card the price chip ("$120", "₹3000", "₹2100") is gold-outlined right beside the gold "APPLY →" pill, so two golds compete in a 300px row, and the prices are shown in raw local currencies with no conversion or "from", making them impossible to compare at a glance. → _Inset the pin badge fully inside the card (or drop it); make the price a neutral ink chip so APPLY is the only gold; and show a converted approximate value ("₹3000 ≈ $36")._  `[polish]`

## Landing — / hero at 1512×1300

- **`src/app/conferences/StagefrontClient.tsx`** — At 1300px viewport height the hero is ~1160px tall for ~400px of content, leaving a ~330px band of empty photograph below the search bar before the gradient fade. The conference-card column is top-aligned and ends at y≈740 while the hero copy is vertically centred, so the two columns have visibly different optical centres and the right side ends 400px short of the left. → _Cap the hero at ~min(88vh, 900px) and vertically centre the card column against the copy block so both columns share a centreline._  `[responsive]`

## Chair — session-wide accent colours

- **`src/app/chair/[code]/page.tsx:729`** — Tailwind default palette colours are used for timer urgency throughout the session tree — `text-red-500` / `bg-red-500` at chair/[code]/page.tsx:729, 803, 808, 1048, 1129, 3501, 3507, CaucusPanel.tsx:96, voting/[code]/page.tsx:244 and 1203 (which also uses `text-yellow-500`). #EF4444 and #EAB308 are pure-hue web colours with no relationship to the forest/ivory system, and they sit right next to NEU.amber #B8844A and NEU.deepGold #B6871F in the same conditional chains, so the urgency ramp jumps from an earthy amber to a fluorescent red. → _Extend the palette with an urgent token (e.g. #A33A2A, a desaturated brick that still reads alarming on ivory) and replace every red-500/yellow-500 with palette values so the whole urgency ramp stays in family._  `[consistency]`

## Chair — border radii across the app

- **`src/components/neu.tsx`** — Six radii measured in one session: 10px (Extend, End Caucus, custom-seconds input), 14px (delegate rows, START/NEXT, create-page CTAs, Add-Time popover), 16px (NeuInset), 22px (NeuCard, scoreboard/stats cards), 32px (the YES/NO end-debate slabs) and 999px (pills). Sibling elements in the same row frequently disagree — e.g. Extend (10px) beside END CAUCUS (10px) beside a 14px NEXT elsewhere on the same screen. → _Publish a radius scale in neu.tsx (sm 10 / md 14 / lg 22 / pill 999), delete 16 and 32, and map every component onto it._  `[consistency]`

## Chair — transitions across the app

- **`src/app/chair/[code]/page.tsx`** — `transition-all` appears 95 times across src/ (11 in chair/[code]/page.tsx, 8 in RollCallPanel, 4 in delegate, 3 in SettingsPanel, 2 each in MotionsModal and DocumentsModal). It animates layout properties (width, height, padding, transform) alongside colour, which is why the create-page CTAs were caught mid-transition rendering as a washed olive rather than forest. The stat rings also use an inline `transition: stroke-dashoffset 0.3s` with a default ease rather than the system EASE cubic-bezier(0.22,1,0.36,1). → _Replace every `transition-all` with an explicit property list (`transition-[background-color,box-shadow,transform]`) and standardise duration/easing on the neu.tsx EASE token._  `[polish]`

## App-wide — focus states

- **`src/components/neu.tsx:534`** — `focus:outline-none` appears 301 times across the session tree and components with no `focus:ring` or `focus-visible` replacement — including on the primary CALL FIRST SPEAKER button, the Settings toggles, the chat send button, the time-preset number input and NeuPill/NeuButton in the design system itself. Keyboard operation of the chair panel leaves no visible focus anywhere. → _Add a single global `:focus-visible { outline: 2px solid #B6871F; outline-offset: 2px }` and strip the blanket `focus:outline-none`, keeping it only where a bespoke ring is actually provided._  `[polish]`

## App-wide — flag rendering

- **`src/components/FlagImg.tsx`** — The same twemoji asset renders four different ways across surfaces: 30px inside a circle with a 1.5px rgba(28,20,16) border (chair sidebar), 20px bare with `object-fit: contain` and no border (scoreboard rows), ~48px inside a white bordered tile with ~8px radius (GSL up-next strip), and ~125px as a bordered rounded rect (current-speaker card). The bare 20px scoreboard flags read noticeably flatter and paler than the bordered ones beside them elsewhere in the app. The unmatched "Republic of Korea" also falls back to a thin outline globe emoji inside a white tile, which reads as a broken image next to the filled flags. → _One `<Flag size="sm|md|lg">` component with a single border/radius rule at every size, and a filled forest-disc-with-initials fallback instead of the outline globe._  `[consistency]`

## Chair — caucus end / phase writes

- **`src/app/chair/[code]/page.tsx`** — When the DB write for a phase or caucus change fails, the console logs "Error setting phase: [object Object]" and "Error updating caucus: [object Object]" and the UI shows nothing at all — the optimistic update leaves the chair looking at an ended caucus while the co-chair's device (reading the true row) still shows it running. Observed live: the head chair's tab showed the GSL, the co-chair's tab showed MODERATED CAUCUS, and neither user was told anything. → _Surface a persistent amber toast on a failed structural write ("Couldn't save that change — retrying") with a manual retry, and log `error.message` rather than stringifying the object._  `[missing-state]`

## App-wide — modal focus management

- **`src/components/MotionsModal.tsx`** — With the Review Allocations modal and the Motions modal open, every control on the page behind them remains exposed in the accessibility tree (read_page listed the whole chair page's buttons and inputs alongside the modal's). There is no `inert`/`aria-hidden` on the background and no focus trap, so tabbing walks straight out of the dialog into the dimmed page. → _Add `inert` (or aria-hidden) to the app root while any overlay is open, trap focus inside the dialog, restore focus to the trigger on close, and close on Escape._  `[polish]`

> **Method / limits:** METHOD — I held the browser and drove the real app against the live Supabase project via the existing dev server (preview_start, gavelling-dev, port 3000). Everything reported here was seen on screen; every colour, pixel and contrast figure was measured in the live DOM with javascript_tool (getComputedStyle / getBoundingClientRect), not inferred from source. Where I cite a file/line it is to point the fix at the right place, not because the finding came from reading code. Screenshots composited fine throughout — no fallback was needed.

TEST COMMITTEE — I created exactly ONE committee: **GJ6U5H** ("ZZTEST Disarmament and International Security Committee", topic "Non-proliferation of nuclear weapons in outer space and the militarisation of low earth orbit", 10 delegations, chair code GJ6U5H-3626, head chair "Alexandra Konstantinopoulou", co-chair "Marcus Oyelaran-Whitfield"). CLEANUP CONFIRMED: I ended it through the app's own flow — Motions → End Debate → proposed by Japan → Raise → Accept → "DOES THIS MOTION PASS?" → YES. Reloading /chair/GJ6U5H afterwards shows "This committee has ended. / 1 hour until committee is deleted", so ended_at + expires_at persisted and pg_cron will delete it. I touched no other committee. (localStorage on this machine held settings for six other codes — 6QDJHR, 0BL4L4, MYCPY3, ZZTSC1, VAI7NJ, QF5WOY — from earlier sessions; I did not open or modify any of them.)

COVERAGE — Walked: /create (type select, form, Review Allocations modal), chair pre-session roll call, chair GSL empty + queued + running timer, Add Time popover, Motions modal raise + vote views, moderated caucus start/queue/end, Documents modal (Working Papers empty state), Settings drawer all four tabs, Scoreboard Ranking + Matrix, Chat, GavelChip (head-chair and co-chair states + its hover explainer), the view-only co-chair variant in a second tab, End Debate confirmation + End View, delegate view at 390×844 (Session-during-caucus, Stats, Submit Document), /voting/[code] (roll-call gate + no-DR state), /join (delegate and chair modes), /sessions (HomeClient) and / (StagefrontClient). Desktop surfaces were checked at both 1280×800 and 1512×1300.

NOT COVERED — the advisor view (/advisor/[code]), the delegate floor card in a non-caucus phase (the caucus was live and then the committee ended), the full 17-step tutorial (I stepped through 3 and ended it), a live vote with a real draft resolution (none could be introduced without uploading a document), suspend/resume, Right-of-Reply overlay, Tour de Table and Consultation of the Whole, and the /manage + /conferences interiors owned by Christian.

RUNTIME ERRORS OBSERVED (functional, not UI — flagged here per the brief) —
1. Console on the head-chair tab: `Error setting phase: [object Object]` ×2 and `Error updating caucus: [object Object]` ×2. The optimistic UI showed the caucus ended while the co-chair's tab (reading the real row) still showed MODERATED CAUCUS. The two devices diverged for the rest of the session and neither user was told. I filed the *missing error state* as a UI finding; the underlying write failure is a separate matter. Also note the log stringifies the error object, so the actual Supabase message is lost.
2. `gslRequireNextSpeaker` is `false` in the persisted settings for GJ6U5H, yet the GSL still showed "Only 1 delegate on the list. Add more before starting." and disabled CALL FIRST SPEAKER with a single speaker queued. Worth checking the default vs. the stored value.
3. On the ended committee, clicking the "SESSION VIEW" tab did not switch views — END VIEW stayed active and the content did not change (tested at 1512×1300).
4. Country matching: "Republic of Korea" (a real UN member-state name) does not match and falls through to CUSTOM, while "Ger" surfaces "Nigeria" as a suggestion because the matcher does a plain substring test with no prefix weighting.
5. AGENTS.md documents the Settings tabs as "Access | Motions | Voting | Points" with `access` as the default; the live UI labels the first tab **GENERAL**. The doc is stale on that point.

RULE CHECKS THAT PASSED — no native `<input type="date">` anywhere in src/ (only the comment in DatePicker.tsx). No vh/dvh/svh units in the chair/delegate/advisor/voting/join/create trees or in the modals rendered inside them (ScoreboardPanel carries an explicit comment explaining why it uses a % cap instead). Informational "i" hints open on hover, not click, in both places I found them (SettingsPanel → Motions → Custom, and GavelChip → WHO'S CHAIRING). GavelChip's Portal + fitScale() positioning is correct and is the model the Add Time / Right-of-Reply overlays should follow. The chair sidebar correctly applies the acronym rule (DISEC + full name beneath) — it is the header, join page, delegate page and voting page that don't.


---

# Completeness critique  
_43 findings_


## Whole product — any bad URL

- **`src/app/layout.tsx`** — There is NO not-found.tsx anywhere in src/app (verified: `find src/app -name not-found.tsx` returns nothing, and no page calls `notFound()`). Every mistyped conference slug, every dead blog link, every expired /manage/[slug], every stale session URL a delegate pastes wrong therefore renders Next.js's built-in 404 — a white page with a black serif '404 | This page could not be found'. No SiteNav, no logo, no ivory, no way back. Seven auditors covered error.tsx and global-error.tsx implicitly and nobody noticed the 404 is missing entirely. It is almost certainly the single most-hit unbranded screen in the product. → _Add src/app/not-found.tsx reusing error.tsx's card recipe (460px, #FAF8F3, 20px radius, 56px disc) with a 🔍/MapPin disc instead of the ⚠️, copy that names what was not found, and three exits: Browse conferences / Join a session (code field inline) / Go home. Also add a route-local not-found.tsx under conferences/[slug] and manage/[slug] so the message can name the conference._  `[missing-state]`

## /conferences/map — world phase

- **`src/app/conferences/map/MapClient.tsx:531`** — The entire interactive layer is six `<polygon fill="transparent" stroke="transparent">` hit zones (:545-548) over a background PNG. Nothing on screen indicates the map is clickable — and the hover feedback that was built is dead: `hoveredDef` (:447) and `tooltipPos = polygonCentroid(...)` (:448) are computed and appear at no other line in the file, so the tooltip never renders. A visitor sees a static world photograph with a cursor that changes to a pointer over six invisible unlabelled shapes. → _Render the computed `tooltipPos` label (continent name + live conference count) on hover, and give each polygon a visible resting affordance — a 1px `rgba(238,217,138,0.35)` stroke plus a gold pin at the centroid, brightening to `NEU.gold` on hover._  `[missing-state]`
- **`src/app/conferences/map/MapClient.tsx:517`** — The map image is `objectFit: 'cover'` (:522, which crops to fill) while the hit-zone SVG directly on top is `viewBox="0 0 100 100" preserveAspectRatio="none"` (:533-535, which stretches its 0–100 space to the same box). The two mappings only agree at world_map.png's native aspect ratio. On any other window shape the drawn continents and the clickable polygons drift apart — at 1512×1300 versus 1280×800 the divergence is large enough that clicking visually-Europe lands in the Africa polygon. → _Switch the image to `objectFit: 'fill'` or give the SVG the same `preserveAspectRatio: 'xMidYMid slice'` the image gets, so both use one mapping. Better: drop the PNG-plus-polygon technique for an inline SVG world map where the continent paths ARE the artwork and the hit zones._  `[responsive]`

## /conferences/map — continent transition

- **`src/app/conferences/map/MapClient.tsx:563`** — Selecting a continent plays `/map/cloud_transition.mp4` full-bleed at `zIndex: 40` with no `poster`, no `preload` and — verified by grep — no `prefers-reduced-motion` guard anywhere in the file. A motion-sensitive visitor gets a full-screen flying-through-clouds animation with no way to opt out, and on a slow connection the video has not decoded so the transition is a blank flash instead. → _Wrap the video in a reduced-motion check (the app already has `useReducedMotion` at ConferenceApplyClient.tsx:305 — export it to src/lib) and fall back to the existing 300ms opacity crossfade. Add `poster` and `preload="auto"` on hover of a continent so the first frame is ready._  `[missing-state]`

## Whole product — Arabic (dir=rtl)

- **`src/contexts/LanguageContext.tsx:31`** — NOBODY audited RTL, and RTL is genuinely switched on: `document.documentElement.dir = language === 'ar' ? 'rtl' : 'ltr'` (:31) flips the whole document. But the codebase is built almost entirely from physical properties — 53 inline `left:` and 51 inline `right:`, 38 `borderLeft` vs 6 `borderRight`, 52 `text-left` and 11 `text-right`, 24 inline `textAlign` — against only 7 `insetInlineStart` and 8 `insetInline` total. Every fixed popover anchored `right: 2rem` (the chair Add Time / Right of Reply / CoW panels), every `borderLeft: '4px solid #1B3828'` blog callout, every drawer that slides from the right, and every gold `▌` accent bar stays physically pinned while the text around it mirrors. → _Treat this as one sweep: replace physical `left/right` with `insetInlineStart/End`, `borderLeft` with `borderInlineStart`, `text-left/right` with `text-start/end`, and `ml-/mr-/pl-/pr-` with `ms-/me-/ps-/pe-` (Tailwind v4 supports all of these). Then screenshot the chair page, the delegate page, /join and the conference detail page under `dir=rtl` — those four will surface the rest._  `[rule-violation]`

## Whole product — Arabic typography

- **`src/app/globals.css:57`** — `:lang(ar) { font-family: 'Noto Sans Arabic', 'Outfit', system-ui, sans-serif }` is the app's only Arabic type rule, and it is defeated on nearly every element in the product. There are 755 inline `fontFamily: "'Outfit', ..."` declarations plus 1,648 inline `fontFamily: OUTFIT` — 2,403 inline styles, which beat any selector including `:lang(ar)`. So the Noto Sans Arabic webfont is downloaded on every page load (layout.tsx:103, five weights) and then overridden almost everywhere, leaving Arabic glyphs to render in whatever generic `sans-serif` the OS supplies. Three separate auditors reported 'Outfit is never loaded' and none noticed the Arabic face is loaded and then thrown away. → _Once Outfit is self-hosted via next/font, define ONE `--font-ui` custom property set on `html` (and swapped to the Arabic stack under `:lang(ar)`), and change the inline declarations to `fontFamily: 'var(--font-ui)'` — a single find-and-replace on the OUTFIT constant fixes 1,648 of the 2,403 in one edit._  `[rule-violation]`

## Whole product — printing

- **`src/app/globals.css`** — Zero print styling exists — `grep -rn '@media print\|@page' src` returns nothing. Yet several surfaces are things people demonstrably print or PDF: the chair Scoreboard (which has an Export CSV button but no printable ranking), the delegate placard (DelegationPlacard.tsx), the invoice ledger and individual invoices under /manage/[slug]/financials, the study guide roster, the public MUN CV at /cv/[id] (its entire purpose is being attached to an application), and the conference detail page a school admin prints for a permission slip. Printing any of them today yields forest-on-ivory blocks that eat toner, dark headers that render as grey mud, and floating fixed navs stamped on page one. → _Add a small `@media print` block in globals.css: force `background: #fff; color: #000` on body, `box-shadow: none` on all NEU surfaces, `display: none` on SiteNav / SideRail / floating chips / the grain overlay, and `break-inside: avoid` on cards. Then add `.print-only` page headers to /cv/[id], the invoice detail and the scoreboard._  `[missing-state]`

## Whole product — keyboard entry points

- **`src/app/layout.tsx`** — There is no skip-to-content link anywhere (grep: 0), and only 10 `<main>` elements across a codebase with 117 `<h1>`s — so on most pages a keyboard or screen-reader user tabs through the entire SiteNav pill row, the language menu, the credit chip and the profile menu before reaching content, on every single page, with no landmark to jump to. On the manage dashboard they also traverse the whole SideRail first. This compounds the 843 `focus:outline-none` finding: not only is focus invisible, there is no shortcut past the chrome. → _Add a visually-hidden-until-focused skip link as the first child of `<body>` in layout.tsx targeting `#main`, and wrap each page's content column in `<main id="main">`. Cheapest fix in the whole audit for the biggest a11y gain._  `[missing-state]`

## Whole product — live regions

- **`src/app/chair/[code]/page.tsx`** — The entire product has 4 `aria-live` regions and 4 `role="alert"` — in an app whose core interaction model is optimistic updates and transient feedback. Nothing announces: the speaker timer changing, 'next speaker is France', a motion being accepted or rejected, a toast (Toast.tsx is portaled with no live region), the 6-second 'Maximum speakers reached' amber strip, 'Copied' on any of the six copy buttons, or a form save. A chair driving the console by keyboard while looking at the room — the exact use case the chair UI is designed for — gets no audible confirmation of anything. → _Add one `aria-live="polite"` visually-hidden status node per major surface (chair page, delegate page, ToastHost) and write one sentence into it on every state change that currently only animates. Make ToastHost's container `role="status" aria-live="polite"` — that alone covers the manage dashboard._  `[missing-state]`

## Whole product — route-level loading

- **`src/app/conferences/[slug]/loading.tsx:6`** — All six `loading.tsx` files (account, my-conferences, conferences/explore, conferences/[slug], .../apply, .../pay) render the identical bare 28px spinning ring, and none was mentioned by any auditor. Worse, on /conferences/[slug] this produces THREE loading appearances in a row: the route-level spinner → ConferenceDetailClient's own SectionCardSkeleton shapes → content. The page ships a real skeleton and then covers it with a spinner. Skeleton.tsx's own header comment argues against exactly this, and it has only 2 importers in the whole app against 84 `animate-spin` instances in 50 files. → _Replace each loading.tsx with the same skeleton composition the client renders once it mounts (hero block + stat strip + two SectionCardSkeletons for the conference page; SkeletonBlock rows for the account tabs) so the shape is stable from first paint to hydrated. Then export the shapes so the client reuses them instead of redefining._  `[missing-state]`

## Design system — the two-halves problem

- **`src/components/neu.tsx`** — Here is the number that states the split precisely: **0 of the session route files import '@/components/neu' — chair, delegate, advisor, voting, join, create, sessions — while 37 files under conferences/, manage/, account/ and my-conferences/ do.** The session half is flat `1px solid #DDD4C0` on `#FAF8F3`/`#EDE7D8` with hardcoded hexes; the conferences half is `NEU.surface` + `NEU.out` at 22px radius. The film-grain overlay tells the same story from the other side: 24 files use it, only 3 of them (chair, delegate, advisor) are in the session tree — so grain and neumorphism are near-perfectly complementary sets. A chair who creates a committee, runs it, then opens the organiser dashboard crosses a visible product boundary twice. → _Pick the session cockpit as the migration target and do it panel by panel in this order (highest visual leverage first): the chair sidebar card → the GSL speaker card → the four modals (Motions/Documents/Scoreboard/Settings) → the delegate cards. Nothing else in this audit is worth doing before the surfaces agree on what a card is._  `[consistency]`

## Session tree — internal split

- **`src/components/GavelChip.tsx`** — The session half is no longer even internally consistent: the newest components DO import neu — ChatPanel, GavelChip, CommitteeIdentityBadge (plus Toast, Skeleton, wizard, CreditsWelcomeModal). So the chair page now renders a neumorphic extruded GavelChip and a neumorphic chat overlay on top of a flat-bordered page, and CommitteeIdentityBadge's NEU surfaces sit inside RollCallPanel's flat forest card. The uncommitted work in this working tree made the inconsistency worse, not better — it introduced the good language into a surface that still speaks the old one everywhere else. → _Treat the three new components as the beachhead and finish the surrounding surface rather than adding a fourth new neu component next to more flat ones. Concretely: RollCallPanel's card, the chair header and the GSL action row are the three shapes that touch all three new components._  `[consistency]`

## Whole product — navigation coherence

- **`src/components/SiteNav.tsx`** — SiteNav renders on 26 files and is absent from every session page (chair/delegate/advisor/voting), from /join, and from all 34 blog pages. Inside a live committee the ONLY navigation is `SessionsHeaderLogo`, whose link always goes to /sessions (SessionsHeaderLogo.tsx:41) — so from a running session there is no path to conferences, /my-conferences, the account menu, the language switcher or sign-in, and a chair who is also a delegation leader has to retype URLs. Conversely nothing in SiteNav points at a live session: NAV_LINKS_CONFIG has SESSIONS / CONFERENCES / ABOUT US / CONTACT and no 'Join a committee' entry, so the two halves of the product genuinely cannot reach each other. → _Add a compact session-mode chrome: keep SessionsHeaderLogo but put the account avatar + language globe beside it in the chair/delegate header (they fit in the 44px bar, the trophy and gear already live there). And add a 'JOIN' item to NAV_LINKS_CONFIG that opens the code field, so a delegate on any marketing page is one click from their committee._  `[wasted-effort]`

## Whole product — two headers

- **`src/components/SiteNav.tsx:99`** — The product ships two mutually exclusive header languages and a user crosses between them constantly. Conferences/marketing: a 72px band with a floating centred glass pill nav (`fixed top-0 ... h-[72px]`), a separate scrolling row for the logo and account, and a language globe. Sessions: a 44px flat bar, `bg-[#FAF8F3] border-b border-[#DDD4C0]`, with four equal-width text tabs, a code chip and two bare 22px lucide glyphs. Different height, different fill, different tab shape, different logo asset (`/GavellingSessionsApp.webp` vs `/GavellingLogo.png`), different behaviour on scroll. /create and /join then use a third: a bare logo row with a bespoke language menu and one text CTA. → _Define one header shell in a shared component with two densities (72px marketing, 44px cockpit) that share the pill vocabulary, the logo lockup and the account/language cluster. The session variant keeps the four session tabs; the marketing variant keeps the four site links._  `[consistency]`

## Whole product — language switching

- **`src/components/SettingsPanel.tsx`** — The language control lives in four different places and one of them does not exist: SiteNav's globe (marketing + manage), a bespoke re-implementation on /create (create/page.tsx:585-619, which the file's own comment describes as 'matching SiteNav'), inside the chair Settings DRAWER (a per-device preference in a panel that writes committee settings to the DB), and nowhere at all on /join — the single most language-sensitive screen in the product, since it is where 30 delegates first arrive. Compounding it: the whole conferences/manage/account/auth/invites half never calls `useT()` (verified across 16 files), so switching to Español translates the sessions half and leaves the conferences half in English. → _Move the switcher to one component used by all three chrome variants, add it to /join, and delete the /create copy. Then decide explicitly whether the conferences half is in scope for i18n — if not, hide the switcher on those routes rather than offering a control that half-works._  `[consistency]`

## Whole product — product naming and marks

- **`src/components/Brand.tsx`** — A user cannot reliably tell which product they are in. Five distinct marks are in play for one brand: `/GavellingLogo.png` (session headers, /join, /create), `/GavellingLogo.webp` (About/Contact footers, run through an 8-stage CSS filter chain to recolour), `/GavellingSessionsApp.webp` (SiteNav on sessions), `/Conferences.webp` (SiteNav on conferences, and preloaded with `fetchPriority="high"` on EVERY route including the three that never paint it), and `/gavel-mark.png` + `/gavel-mark.webp` (Brand.tsx, the credit chip). Meanwhile SiteNav's CONFERENCES link and the SiteNav logo both point at `/`, so the two adjacent controls have one destination. → _Settle on one SVG mark plus one wordmark, both self-hosted, with an optional product suffix ('Sessions' / 'Conferences') rendered as type rather than baked into separate raster assets. Drop the recolour filter chain, drop the global Conferences.webp preload, and point the logo at the half of the product the user is currently in._  `[consistency]`

## Whole product — committee identity

- **`src/lib/presetNames.ts`** — AGENTS.md's acronym rule was checked per-area but nobody counted it product-wide. `committeeDisplayName` / `deriveCommitteeAcronym` from src/lib/presetNames.ts has ZERO call sites under src/app/conferences/, and the same committee renders at least six ways: acronym + full name beneath (chair sidebar, correct), full name + topic concatenated with ':' in one truncating 12px span (chair header), full name across two lines at 24px (delegate mobile), full name un-abbreviated with the topic after a '·' (the /join found-committee card), full name at 16px bold (voting header), and `{c.name}` alone at 15.5px wrapping to three lines (conference committee cards) — while a bespoke hardcoded regex `abbreviateCommitteeName()` that only knows UNSC/UNGA/UNHRC/HRC is duplicated verbatim in delegate:45 and voting:64. → _Export one `<CommitteeName full acronym size />` component built on committeeDisplayName, replace all six renderings and both copies of the regex with it, and delete abbreviateCommitteeName._  `[rule-violation]`

## Whole product — Portal inside FitToScreen

- **`src/components/Portal.tsx:9`** — Portal is imported by 33 files and prefers `#fit-root` over `document.body`. Nine of those consumers can render inside the transformed 820px cockpit — voting/[code], ScoreboardPanel, VotingRulesPanel, DocumentsModal, MotionsModal, SettingsPanel, TutorialOverlay, chat/NewDmPicker and Toast — and exactly ONE of them, GavelChip, compensates for the transform (`fitScale()`, GavelChip.tsx:38, called at :119). Every other one computes `getBoundingClientRect()` in viewport space and applies it as `position: fixed` inside a scaled containing block. This is the same class as the 257px modal-header bug, and it is systemic rather than per-component — no single-area auditor could see that the fix already exists in one file. → _Move `fitScale()` out of GavelChip into Portal itself (or into a `usePortalCoords(rect)` hook) and have Portal divide incoming coordinates by the fit scale when it mounts into #fit-root. Then every current and future consumer is correct by default, and the AGENTS.md popover rule finally means what it says on session pages._  `[rule-violation]`

## /delegation/[societyId] — delegation portal

- **`src/app/delegation/[societyId]/DelegationPortalClient.tsx:584`** — 755 lines, an entire user role (head delegate / faculty advisor distributing block seats), and no auditor opened it. It has ZERO responsive prefixes — `grep -oE '(sm|md|lg|xl):'` returns 0 — and no `grid` at all; the whole page is a fixed `maxWidth: 960, margin: '0 auto'` column with `flex flex-wrap` rows (:306, :655). Head delegates do this work at the conference, on a phone, standing up, the morning allocations drop. On a 390px screen the seat cards and member rows wrap arbitrarily with no layout intent. → _Add breakpoints to the two flex-wrap rows and the seat grid, and give the member picker a full-width sheet treatment below `sm`. This is the highest-traffic uncovered surface in the product and it deserves a proper pass, not just breakpoints._  `[responsive]`

## /invites/delegation/[token] — success path

- **`src/app/invites/delegation/[token]/page.tsx:96`** — The failure state is a properly designed neu card. The SUCCESS state — which is what almost everyone gets — is `if (state !== 'failed') return <Spinner />`: a bare 28px spinning ring on ivory with no SiteNav, no logo, no text. A delegate who taps their head delegate's invite link sees an unbranded spinner and is then silently `router.replace`d to `/auth/signin?next=...` with no explanation of why they are being asked to sign in or what they were invited to. The same shape applies to the chair and organizer invite pages. → _Render an interstitial card on the success path too: the delegation/conference name, 'You've been invited to join {society} at {conference}', and either a redirect notice or an explicit CONTINUE button. Pass the society name into the sign-in page so /auth/signin can say 'Sign in to join {society}' rather than a generic form._  `[missing-state]`

## /invites/* — all three

- **`src/app/invites/chair/[token]/page.tsx`** — All three invite landings are hardcoded English (verified: none imports useT/useLanguage) — 'Invite not found', 'Invite not valid', "You've been invited to chair {committee} at {conference}", 'GO TO MY CONFERENCES', 'BROWSE CONFERENCES'. These are the FIRST screen a person ever sees of Gavelling when a chair or head delegate recruits them, and the product ships a four-language switcher on the nav directly above. There is also no language switcher reachable from the failure card. → _Either route these three through `t()` alongside the sessions half, or make the conscious call that the conferences half is English-only and remove the switcher from SiteNav on those routes. The current state — a translated nav over untranslated content — is the worst of both._  `[copy]`

## /conferences/[slug]/pay — the money page

- **`src/app/conferences/[slug]/pay/page.tsx:969`** — 2,341 lines, the surface where every delegate actually pays, and it was explicitly out of every auditor's scope. The page body is properly neumorphic (NeuCard, NeuIconDisc, NEU tokens) but all four of its modals revert to the legacy flat language — `backgroundColor: '#FAF8F3', border: '1px solid #DDD4C0'` at widths 460/400/420/380 (:969, :1234, :1405, :2268), with only two of the four carrying `maxHeight`. It also carries 39 `focus:outline-none` with no replacement, and the invoice selection checkboxes are native `<input type="checkbox">` styled only with `accentColor: NEU.forest` at 16×16 (:1897) — an OS control at a third of the tap-target minimum, used to select which invoices get charged. → _Fold this page into the modal-layer migration (it is four more instances of the same recipe) and replace the native checkboxes with the SelectBox primitive from applications/page.tsx:771, which is the benchmark's own answer at 20px. Given it handles money, it also deserves the focus-ring pass before anything cosmetic._  `[consistency]`

That is 469 lines of unreferenced chair UI that will drift and mislead the next person auditing this surface. → _Delete all three. If any of the three holds a layout worth keeping (SpeakersListPanel's list treatment in particular), lift that into the live chair page first, then delete._  `[consistency]`

## Documentation — CLAUDE.md architecture section

- **`CLAUDE.md:63`** — CLAUDE.md:63 states: 'Each panel (SpeakersListPanel, CaucusPanel, MotionsPanel, ResolutionsPanel, RollCallPanel, ChatPanel, SettingsPanel) is rendered by the chair page'. Of those seven, THREE are dead code (SpeakersListPanel, CaucusPanel, ResolutionsPanel) and one — `MotionsPanel` — does not exist as a file at all (`ls src/components/MotionsPanel.tsx` → No such file). Four of seven claims in a single sentence of the file that is supposed to orient every agent working in this repo. Two other doc drifts were found in the audits (AGENTS.md's four-colour delegate floor card that isn't built, and the Settings first tab documented as 'Access' while the live UI says GENERAL). → _Correct CLAUDE.md:63 to the actual set (MotionsModal, DocumentsModal, RollCallPanel, ChatPanel, SettingsPanel, ScoreboardPanel, FeedbackLogPanel, CowDelegationBoard, GavelChip, CommitteeIdentityBadge, TutorialOverlay) and fix the two AGENTS.md drifts in the same pass — the sessions rules say every doc claim was verified against code, and these three were not._  `[rule-violation]`

## Whole product — iconography

- **`src/components/Emoji.tsx`** — Only one auditor touched iconography, and only for the Emoji component's own table. The product-wide census is five simultaneous systems: lucide-react in 97 files, the Twemoji `<Emoji>` wrapper 50 times (32-entry codepoint table), Microsoft Fluent 3D emoji fetched from jsDelivr 35 times inside NeuIconDisc, 104 raw platform emoji characters across 45 files (🌐 ×30, ⚠️ ×19, ★ ×15, 🪑 ×9, 🎙 ×7, 📎 ×6, 🎉 ×6, and one each of 🖥 🔨 📝 📘 ✨ ⚙), plus bare text glyphs used as controls (⠿ as the drag handle, ▸ as the drill-in, ✓/✗ as blog table cells, ★ as the current-speaker badge). Adjacent elements routinely mix three of them — the chair header pairs two 22px lucide strokes with a 🎙 in the sidebar and a ⚙ text character on /voting. → _Rule: lucide for every control and affordance; Fluent 3D (self-hosted, not jsDelivr) only inside NeuIconDisc as decorative section marks; Twemoji only for flags and genuine content emoji; zero raw emoji as UI. Replace ⠿ with GripVertical, ▸ with ChevronRight, ★ with Mic, ⚙ with Settings — that alone kills most of the mixing._  `[consistency]`

## Whole product — modal semantics

- **`src/components/CommitteeEditorModal.tsx`** — The modal layer is the most-shared surface in the product and its accessibility is essentially absent: 5 `role="dialog"` and 4 `aria-modal` against roughly 21 hand-rolled overlays. Only ConfirmModal binds Escape (:62); ModalOverlay has none, no focus trap, no focus restoration and no body-scroll lock. The live browser pass confirmed the consequence — with the Review Allocations and Motions modals open, every control on the page behind them was still in the accessibility tree and tab order. Three separate auditors reported the styling of these modals; none reported that the layer has no dialog semantics at all. → _Make ModalOverlay the one true shell: `role="dialog" aria-modal="true"`, `aria-labelledby` wired to the title, Escape, a focus trap, focus restoration, `inert` on the app root while open, and body scroll lock. Then migrate the 21 hand-rolled overlays onto it — that single component fixes the semantics, the scrim colour and the flat-card styling together._  `[missing-state]`

## Whole product — form validation

- **`src/app/create/page.tsx`** — Validation presentation was only reported per-form; the product-wide pattern is that there isn't one. 26 `aria-invalid` and just 2 `aria-describedby` across the entire app, against 62 `required` attributes. Concretely: /create disables START SESSION with the label 'ENTER COMMITTEE NAME & TOPIC ABOUT' and never marks which field; MotionsModal disables its CTA until a proposer is picked while only Topic carries an asterisk; the About ambassador form gates on three fields, marks none required, and (AboutClient.tsx:93) ignores the insert error so a failed write shows the success tick; /conferences/new's CREATE button is a compound of nine conditions with no message; the settings role-config fields save on blur with no saved/failed indicator and never validate Closes-after-Opens. Five different ways of saying 'you can't submit yet', none of which says which field. → _Standardise one field-error pattern: a required marker on the label, `aria-invalid` + `aria-describedby` pointing at an inline 12px `#8B2020` message below the field, and disable-with-reason on the submit (a `title` + a one-line summary naming the first blocking field). Build it into a shared `<Field>` and adopt it in /create, MotionsModal, the wizard and the settings role config first._  `[missing-state]`

## Whole product — projector and room legibility

- **`src/components/FitToScreen.tsx`** — Nobody assessed the real deployment context: the chair console runs on a laptop mirrored to a projector in a room where delegates read it from 5–15 metres. Against that, the session UI has 263 elements at ≤10.5px, all of them rendered through FitToScreen's fractional `scale = innerHeight / 820` transform (1.098× on a MacBook, 1.317× on 1080p), which softens small type most. The elements the room actually needs are the smallest on screen: the session code is a 12px mono chip (while Settings renders the same code at 22px), the queue positions are 10px, the ABSENT tag is 10px at 35% opacity, the 'N speakers until your speech' line is 12px. Meanwhile the 128px unmoderated-caucus clock — the least urgent number in the app — is the largest thing in the product. → _Add a 'Project' toggle to the chair header that switches the session to a room-legible density: session code and current speaker at 3–4×, queue positions at 18px minimum, the timer keeping its size, and every ≤11px label suppressed rather than shrunk. Independently, raise the session floor to 12px and rebalance the four timer sizes (unmoderated 128px vs GSL 80px vs caucus total 18px) around urgency rather than available space._  `[contrast]`

## Whole product — empty states

- **`src/components/Skeleton.tsx`** — Empty states were reported per-surface many times but never as a system problem: there is no `NeuEmptyState` primitive, so every one is hand-rolled and they now disagree on all four variables. Committees uses a `1.5px dashed #C8BEA8` box with a 56px circle; Applications uses a NeuCard with a 48px NeuIconDisc and a Fluent 3D emoji; Live uses a bare NeuCard with 14px text and no icon; Jobs uses unstyled centred text with no CTA; the chair Documents modal separates its message from its CTA by 90px; the delegation portal uses plain 18px bold text. Skeleton.tsx's header comment even states the rule for when an empty state may render, but supplies no component to render it with. → _Export `NeuEmptyState({ icon, title, body, action })` from neu.tsx with a fixed recipe (48px NeuIconDisc, 15px/800 title, 13px NEU-secondary body at max 46ch, one NeuButton) and replace all of them. It also settles the recurring question of whether an empty state gets a CTA: it always does._  `[missing-state]`

## Whole product — first-run vs returning

- **`src/components/TutorialOverlay.tsx`** — Only the 17-step chair tutorial was audited as onboarding, but it is the only first-run affordance in the entire product and it exists on exactly one surface. There is nothing for: a first-time DELEGATE (who gets the densest, most jargon-heavy screen — 'Attendance (P/PV)', 'GSL', 'Moderated Caucus' — with no explanation anywhere), a first-time faculty advisor (a read-only grid of 40 cards with no orientation), a first-time organiser landing on the manage dashboard, or a first-time head delegate on the delegation portal. Conversely there is no returning-user state either — the tutorial gate asks 'HAVE YOU USED GAVELLING BEFORE?' on a per-device basis, so an experienced chair on a new laptop is asked again. → _Rather than four more 17-step tours, add one dismissible first-run hint card per role, anchored in the surface it explains (delegate Session tab, advisor rail, manage dashboard, delegation portal), storing dismissal on the profile rather than the device. And expand the acronyms inline on first render for delegates — GSL in particular appears as a bare three-letter label with no expansion anywhere in the delegate UI._  `[missing-state]`

## /auth/* — sign-in, sign-up, forgot, reset

- **`src/app/auth/authUi.tsx`** — Worth stating as a positive because the next phase should not spend budget here: the auth surfaces are the cleanest in the product and no auditor covered them. signin/signup/forgot/reset have ZERO `focus:outline-none`, zero `transition-all`, no native selects, no alert()/confirm(), and they share one primitive file (authUi.tsx, 768 lines: AuthLayout, FieldLabel, TextField, PasswordField, ErrorBanner, CheckEmailScreen, NoticeScreen, GoogleButton, OrDivider). Signup correctly uses the shared DatePicker for date of birth with a `max` and an `initialView` (signup:38-50), and safeNext() guards open-redirect. The two real gaps: signin surfaces raw `error.message` from Supabase (:50), and /auth/onboarding carries 4 focus:outline-none and 3 transition-all, i.e. it did not get the same care as its siblings. → _Map Supabase auth error codes to human copy in authUi's ErrorBanner rather than passing `error.message` through, and bring /auth/onboarding onto the same primitives. Then use authUi.tsx as the model when building the shared Field/Input primitives the rest of the product lacks — it is already most of the way there._  `[consistency]`

## Whole product — dark seals on forest

- **`src/components/LogoDisc.tsx`** — Also worth stating so it is not re-audited: the dark-logo-on-forest problem IS solved, and solved well. LogoDisc (:26-80) wraps every conference logo in a `#FDFCF9` near-white disc with a 7% inner margin and a hairline rim explicitly so dark seals and transparent PNGs survive on both ivory and forest, and `fallbackTone="plain"` exists precisely because a forest-gradient monogram disc is invisible on the chair sidebar. The gap is adoption, not design: the explore LIST row hand-rolls a raw `<img>` with no `onError` while the grid card uses LogoDisc, the participant AllocationCard uses a raw `<img>` for the committee emblem, and the advisor page uses five different bare `<img>` flag treatments and never touches FlagImg. → _Make LogoDisc and FlagImg mandatory — replace the raw `<img>` in the explore list row, AllocationCard, the secretariat/dais avatars and all five advisor flag treatments. No new design work needed, just routing everything through the component that already got it right._  `[consistency]`

- **`AGENTS.md`** — Roughly 60 of the 582 findings are the same nine issues reported independently by three to five auditors, which will make the fix list look far longer than it is: Outfit-never-loaded (3 auditors), focus:outline-none-with-no-ring (5), transition-all (4), the flat #FAF8F3 modal layer (4), MobileGate's fake button + false rotate promise (2, verbatim), ConfirmModal's Enter-fires-Confirm and its 50/50 button pair (2), Toast's 2200ms error dismissal (2), the DatePicker's native select + number input (2), and the grain overlay's duplicated data URI (2). Two are also literal duplicates of the same sentence. → _Before dispatching fixes, collapse these into nine cross-cutting workstreams (fonts / focus / motion / modal shell / date picker / toast / grain / mobile gate / confirm) each with a single owner, and strip the per-area copies. Doing them as nine sweeps rather than 60 patches also avoids five people touching neu.tsx._  `[consistency]`

## Whole product — motion

- **`src/app/globals.css:238`** — `prefers-reduced-motion` is honoured in exactly 4 places across the codebase (globals.css:238 for the ambassador marquee, :257 for the skeleton shimmer, ConferenceCard.tsx:51, VariantStagefront.tsx:293) plus one hook that is used on one page (ConferenceApplyClient.tsx:305/769). Unguarded: the map's cloud-transition video, the HomeClient hero's 1.9s/2.1s/2.3s staggered reveals (which hide the entire join box until 2.3s with no override), the 3500ms and 3000ms caucus loading screens, the infinite `bulkPulse` on the applications bulk bar, gvRise on the confirmation and public CV pages, the Toast slide-in, NeuProgress's 600ms tween, NeuRing's 700ms stroke, Disclosure's 300ms max-height, the mobile menu, and every `translateY/scale` hover lift in neu.tsx. → _Add a single global `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) { *, *::before, *::after { animation-duration: 0.01ms !important; animation-iteration-count: 1 !important; transition-duration: 0.01ms !important; scroll-behavior: auto !important } }` block to globals.css, then keep the four bespoke rules for the cases that need a designed fallback rather than a kill._  `[polish]`

## Chair session — no exit

- **`src/app/chair/[code]/page.tsx:2623`** — Once inside a running committee there is exactly one navigational element: `<SessionsHeaderLogo />` (:2623), whose link goes to /sessions with `aria-label="Back to Sessions"`. Clicking it silently abandons the session with no confirmation — no 'you are chairing a live committee, suspend it first?' — and there is no visible label saying it is an exit, so it reads as branding. The only other Links in the whole 3,550-line file are on the not-found and ended screens (:2161, :2172). A chair who wants to check something on the conference page has no non-destructive way to do it. → _Make the exit explicit: a small 'Leave session' item next to the gear that confirms when a timer is running, and open the conference/marketing destinations in a new tab from the logo instead of navigating away. Also add the acronym+full-name committee identity there so the header stops carrying it as a truncated 12px concatenation._  `[wasted-effort]`

## Whole product — one-item and very-long-name states

- **`src/components/RollCallPanel.tsx`** — Empty and loading states got broad coverage; the one-item and very-long-value states did not, and they are where the layouts actually break. Confirmed instances that nobody framed as a class: 'Republic of Korea' wraps to two lines in the GSL up-next strip while its neighbours stay on one, leaving a ragged baseline; the Motions modal at one motion leaves ~430px of void because its height is fixed at 88%; the Scoreboard ranks ten delegations 1–10 when nine of them scored identically; the GavelChip prints an untruncated full chair name (~320px) in the trigger and then truncates the SAME name at 150px in the popover; 'FACULTY ADVISOR' must fit a 105px /join card at text-sm font-black; 'Democratic Republic of the Congo' becomes four legible characters in the advisor's 80px tile. → _Establish two fixtures and test every list against them: a one-item committee and a committee of 190 delegations whose names include 'United Kingdom of Great Britain and Northern Ireland', 'Democratic Republic of the Congo' and 'Republic of Korea'. Then set one truncation rule (two lines max, ellipsis, full value in a title) and one tie rule for ranked lists (equal rank number, not sequential)._  `[missing-state]`

## Whole product — copy-to-clipboard feedback

- **`src/components/SettingsPanel.tsx`** — There are at least six copy buttons in the product and four different acknowledgements. The chair header code chip swaps its 6-character label for a bare '✓', collapsing from ~70px to ~22px and reflowing the header for 2 seconds; SettingsPanel's CodeCopyButton shows '✓ Copied' and keeps its width; the delegate header code chip calls `navigator.clipboard.writeText` with NO feedback at all; the participant AllocationCard reserves a `minWidth: 42` spacer for 'Copied' which leaves the code visually off-centre until pressed; account/cv and ShareAchievementModal fall back to `window.prompt`. Each was reported separately in a different area; none as the pattern it is. → _One `<CopyButton value label />` primitive: never changes width, swaps the icon to a Check for 1.5s, writes to the shared aria-live region, and falls back to a selectable inline field rather than prompt(). Replace all six._  `[consistency]`

> **Method / limits:** METHOD — I did not re-audit. I walked src/app and src/components myself with `find`/`ls` (never trusting the summaries), cross-checked every route and component name against the 582 findings, then read or grep-profiled the surfaces nobody opened and ran product-wide greps for the dimensions nobody swept. No dev server, no browser. Every count quoted is reproducible from the repo root and was run at audit time. Nothing was edited.

  • /conferences/map (MapClient.tsx, 1007 lines) — "partial skim" only, no findings filed
