## Participant — delegation credits

- **`src/app/conferences/[slug]/participant/DelegationCreditsCard.tsx:126`** — Quantity is set only by 26×26px − / + buttons capped at MAX_QTY 20. Buying 20 credits for a delegation is 19 taps on a target well under the 44px minimum, and the cap is silent — the + button simply dims at 20 with no explanation. There is no way to type a number. → _Make the count an editable numeric field (inputMode="numeric") between the steppers, bump the buttons to 32×32 inside a 44px hit area, and add quick chips for 5 / 10 / 20. When the cap is hit, say 'Maximum 20 per purchase'._  `[wasted-effort]`
- **`src/app/conferences/[slug]/participant/DelegationCreditsCard.tsx:112`** — The balance renders '—' for both `balanceLoading` and `balance === null`. An advisor cannot tell whether the pool is still loading, is genuinely empty, or the RPC failed — and if the RPC fails the em dash stays forever with no retry. → _Show a shimmer bar while loading, '0' when the pool is genuinely empty, and 'Couldn't load balance · Retry' on failure._  `[missing-state]`
- **`src/app/conferences/[slug]/participant/DelegationCreditsCard.tsx:165`** — The CTA reads 'BUY FOR $30.00' but the click redirects to a Stripe checkout page — nothing is bought yet. Money buttons should never overstate what the press does. → _Label it 'CONTINUE TO CHECKOUT · $30.00' and keep 'STARTING CHECKOUT…' as the busy state._  `[copy]`

## Participant — position paper

- **`src/app/conferences/[slug]/participant/PositionPaperCard.tsx:373`** — The upload target is a `<div onClick>` with a 1.5px dashed border, 28px padding, an inset shadow and the text 'Click to select PDF'. It looks exactly like a drop zone but has no onDragOver/onDrop, so dragging a PDF onto it navigates the browser away from the page. It is also not a button, not focusable and not keyboard-operable. → _Make it a `<button type="button">`, add onDragOver/onDragLeave/onDrop wired to the same handler with a hover-lit border while dragging, and change the copy to 'Drop your PDF here or click to browse'._  `[wasted-effort]`
- **`src/app/conferences/[slug]/participant/PositionPaperCard.tsx:294`** — The 'Replace Position Paper?' confirm is a hand-rolled `fixed inset-0 z-50` overlay with no Portal, no Escape handler, no backdrop-click dismiss (the backdrop div has no onClick) and no role=dialog — while the repo already ships `useConfirmModal` from @/components/ConfirmModal, which QuestionBuilder uses two directories away for exactly this job. → _Replace the whole block with `const { confirm, modal } = useConfirmModal()` and an `await confirm({ title, body, confirmLabel: 'Replace', danger: false })`._  `[rule-violation]`
- **`src/app/conferences/[slug]/participant/PositionPaperCard.tsx:283`** — 'Replace' — the only action available once a paper exists — is a 10.5px, weight-500, NEU.muted text button with zero padding, sitting under the card. Its hit area is roughly 42×14px and its resting colour is the same grey as the file metadata above it, so it reads as a caption rather than the primary next action. → _Render it as an ActionButton (the file already imports one) at 12px with the standard 8px/16px padding, or at minimum give it `padding: 6px 8px; margin: -6px -8px` and forest ink so it looks clickable at rest._  `[polish]`

## Organiser — live committee recap

- **`src/app/manage/[slug]/live/LiveModals.tsx:292`** — A gold chip reading 'Feedback format changes on the production branch (placeholder)' is rendered to organisers inside the Chair-feedback section of the Recap modal. That is a developer merge note shipped as user-visible UI. Line 233 does the same in prose: 'Motions count reflects pending motions only. Total motions raised isn't persisted.' — an implementation detail explained to the customer, pulled up with a `-mt-4` hack. → _Delete the placeholder chip. Rename the tile 'Motions pending' → it already says pending, so drop the caveat line and the negative margin with it._  `[copy]`
- **`src/app/manage/[slug]/live/LiveModals.tsx:216`** — Five StatTiles in a `grid-cols-2 md:grid-cols-3` — at md the last row holds two tiles and leaves a ragged gap; at sm the fifth tile sits alone. The tiles are also NeuInset (pressed-in) while the neu.tsx house stat primitive, NeuStatTile, is extruded, so the recap's stats read as wells rather than as the raised tiles used on the organiser dashboard. → _Add a sixth stat (Delegates absent, or Documents total) to balance the grid, and use NeuStatTile compact so the recap matches the dashboard it opens from._  `[polish]`
- **`src/app/manage/[slug]/live/LiveModals.tsx:183`** — The recap labels the lowest-scoring delegate 'Quietest delegate' and shows it with the same visual weight as 'Top delegate'. An absent delegate scores 0 and is therefore named the 'quietest' in a panel a secretariat member may screenshot. Naming and shaming the bottom of the list is not something an organiser asked for. → _Drop the quietest row, or reframe it as an actionable prompt: 'Yet to speak — 4 delegates' with the list, which is the thing a secretariat can actually act on._  `[copy]`
- **`src/app/manage/[slug]/live/LiveModals.tsx:196`** — The acronym-with-full-name-beneath rule is hand-implemented three times in this file (lines 195–214, 406–415, 531–535) as `data.conf.abbreviation ?? data.conf.name` plus a conditional second line, including a `{(!abbr || abbr === name) && <div className="mb-5" />}` spacer hack to keep the layout from jumping. The shared `committeeDisplayName` helper in @/lib/presetNames is not used. → _Extract a local `<CommitteeTitle conf={data.conf} />` built on committeeDisplayName and use it in all three modals — that also removes the empty-spacer div._  `[rule-violation]`

## Auth — shared primitives

- **`src/app/auth/authUi.tsx:386`** — GoogleButton is styled identically to PrimaryButton — same #1B3828 fill, same #EED98A text, same rounded-xl py-3, same weight and tracking. So every auth screen shows two visually identical full-width forest primaries separated by an 'or' divider, with no indication which one is the recommended path. → _Make the Google button secondary: ivory surface, #DDD4C0 border, #1C1410 label, keeping the coloured Google mark. That also matches Google's own branding guidance and restores a single primary per screen._  `[hierarchy]`
- **`src/app/auth/authUi.tsx:303`** — Focus policy is inconsistent inside one file: PrimaryActionButton (332) and PrimaryLinkButton (365) use `focus-visible:outline focus-visible:outline-2 focus-visible:outline-offset-2` with an explicit outlineColor, while PrimaryButton (303), GoogleButton (386), TextField (428), PasswordField (451) and the back link (135) all use `focus:outline-none` with nothing replacing it. The two most-used controls on the sign-in page are the ones with no ring. → _Apply the PrimaryActionButton focus recipe to all of them, and add it to `inputStyle` as a focus-visible box-shadow so TextField/PasswordField get a ring too._  `[contrast]`
- **`src/app/auth/authUi.tsx:475`** — The password show/hide toggle is an 18px SVG in an absolutely positioned button with no padding — roughly an 18×18px tap target at the right edge of the field, well under the 44px minimum, with no focus ring and no hover state. → _Give the button `padding: 10px; margin: -10px` so the visual size stays but the hit area reaches 38–44px, add a focus-visible ring, and darken to #6E5F4E on hover._  `[polish]`
- **`src/app/auth/authUi.tsx:428`** — TextField has no error/invalid prop — every failure is funnelled into the page-level ErrorBanner (287), a centred red block above the form with no icon, no field association and no recovery instruction. A wrong email format and a wrong password therefore look identical and neither points at the field responsible. → _Add `error?: string` to TextField/PasswordField that sets a #8B2020 border, `aria-invalid`, and an inline message under the field. Keep ErrorBanner only for whole-form failures ('We couldn't reach the server'), and always end those with a next step._  `[missing-state]`

## MUN CV — timeline

- **`src/app/account/cv/CVTimeline.tsx:26`** — The house acronym rule is implemented as a hardcoded list of exactly 8 conference regexes (CONFERENCE_ACRONYMS). Every conference outside that list falls through to `{ primary: name, secondary: null }` — so 'Oxford International Model United Nations 2027' renders in full at 18px/900 with no acronym line, which is precisely the case the rule exists for. It is also unmaintainable: adding a conference means editing a component. → _Fall back to `committeeDisplayName(name)` when no explicit acronym matches, and move the 8 named series into src/lib/presetNames.ts alongside the other lookup tables._  `[rule-violation]`
- **`src/app/account/cv/CVTimeline.tsx:46`** — COMMITTEE_ACRONYMS here is the fourth parallel committee-name lookup table in the codebase, alongside COMMITTEE_SUGGESTIONS (CVEntryModal), CONFERENCE_COMMITTEE_PRESETS (ConferenceRosterPicker) and COMMITTEE_LOGO_PRESETS (accountUi.tsx:503). `committeeLabel` consults three of the four in sequence to resolve one name. → _Consolidate into one table in src/lib/presetNames.ts carrying { name, acronym, logoPath } and have committeeLabel, getCommitteeLogo and the roster picker all read it._  `[consistency]`
- **`src/app/account/cv/CVTimeline.tsx:234`** — A single entry card encodes entry_type four separate times: a 1.5px coloured border (type.border), a 172px faded bleed silhouette behind the text (type.bleedIcon), a 34px emoji badge notched into the top-right corner (type.emoji), and a gradient role chip spelling the label out (type.label). Four signals, one fact, all fighting the conference name that is supposed to be the headline. → _Keep the role chip (it is the only one that is unambiguous) and the coloured border. Drop the corner badge and reduce the bleed silhouette to opacity 0.03 or remove it._  `[hierarchy]`
- **`src/app/account/cv/CVTimeline.tsx:181`** — The timeline rail is a fixed 84px column plus a 16–20px gap, so on a 375px phone 100–104px — over a quarter of the viewport — is spent on a logo and a date, leaving ~270px for the card that carries the conference name, committee, allocation, awards, description and photos. → _Below `md`, drop the rail: move the logo inline into the card header at 40px and render the date as a small line under the role chip. Keep the 84px rail only at md and up._  `[responsive]`
- **`src/app/account/cv/CVTimeline.tsx:160`** — An entry with no event_date shows a bare em dash '—' in 13.5px/700 directly under the 84px conference logo, where every other entry shows 'March 2027'. It reads like a rendering failure rather than 'no date recorded'. → _Render 'Date not set' in NEU.muted at the same size, and — since the card is clickable to edit — that string is itself the prompt to fix it._  `[copy]`

## MUN CV — entry editor

- **`src/components/CVEntryModal.tsx:327`** — The entry date is entered through two native `<select>` elements (Month and Year) rather than the shared @/components/DatePicker, which the rest of the product uses (VouchersSection:401, CustomQuestionsField:125). It is the one remaining date-entry surface that does not use the house picker. Worse, `emit(Number(month), selYear >= 0 ? selYear : currentYear)` silently defaults a missing year to the current year and a missing month to January (`selMonth >= 0 ? selMonth : 0`), so an entry can be saved with a date the user never chose. → _Use DatePicker in a month-precision mode (or add one), and refuse to emit until both parts are chosen instead of defaulting. Also drop the 50/50 grid — the Year column does not need the same width as 'September'._  `[rule-violation]`
- **`src/components/CVEntryModal.tsx:934`** — `transition-all` on the entry-type picker tiles (934) and on the award chips (1168). Both also change padding/border on selection, so the whole tile geometry lerps on every click. → _Narrow to `transition-[background-color,border-color,box-shadow,transform]`._  `[polish]`

## Conference roster picker — paste review

- **`src/components/ConferenceRosterPicker.tsx:700`** — The 'Review pasted list' overlay is a bare `fixed inset-0 z-[60]` div with no Portal — inside a component whose own two typeaheads (354, 548) ARE correctly portaled to fixed viewport coords with the exact comment explaining why. The overlay is mounted inside CommitteeEditorModal, a scrollable `overflow-y:auto` panel, which is the case the rule was written for. → _Wrap the review overlay in `<Portal>` like the typeaheads in the same file already are._  `[rule-violation]`
- **`src/components/ConferenceRosterPicker.tsx:830`** — The confirm button is `transition-all disabled:opacity-30` on a #1B3828 fill with #EED98A text. At 30% opacity the forest fill washes to roughly #D3D0C4 and the gold label to about #F4EDD6 — the text nearly vanishes into the button, which reads as a rendering bug rather than a disabled control. Line 621 has the same `transition-all disabled:opacity-30` pattern. → _Use an explicit disabled palette (`backgroundColor: '#DDD4C0', color: '#9A8A78'`, the recipe QuestionBuilder:268 already uses) instead of an opacity multiplier, and replace transition-all with transition-colors._  `[contrast]`
- **`src/components/ConferenceRosterPicker.tsx:731`** — Each review row's remove control is the literal character ✕ in a `text-sm` button with no padding, no aria-label, no hover state and `focus:outline-none` — roughly a 14×18px target, in a list where mis-clicking removes a country from the roster. → _Use lucide `X size={14}` inside a 28×28 rounded button with a rose hover wash and `aria-label={`Remove ${r.name}`}`._  `[polish]`

## Conference roster picker — country list

- **`src/components/ConferenceRosterPicker.tsx:712`** — The empty state shouts 'NO COUNTRIES' / 'NO CHARACTERS' in bold uppercase forest — the loudest treatment on the panel for the most ordinary state there is. Every other empty state in the product is sentence case and muted ('No questions yet.', 'No chair feedback recorded yet.'). → _Change to sentence case in NEU.muted: 'No countries yet' / 'No characters yet', keeping the helper line beneath._  `[copy]`

## Participant — questions & requests

- **`src/app/conferences/[slug]/participant/RequestsPanel.tsx:19`** — `const MONO = "'DM Mono', monospace"` — and DM Mono is not loaded anywhere (layout.tsx only pulls Playfair Display and Noto Sans Arabic), so line 137's timestamp falls back to the platform monospace (Menlo/Courier New). Both accountUi.tsx:16 and delegationShared.tsx:19 carry explicit comments that monospace is banned on the conferences side and alias MONO to Outfit; this file re-introduces it. → _Change to `const MONO = OUTFIT` (or delete it and use OUTFIT with `fontVariantNumeric: 'tabular-nums'`, which is what the timestamp actually needs)._  `[consistency]`
- **`src/app/conferences/[slug]/participant/RequestsPanel.tsx:686`** — Every chip on this panel is `fontSize: 9` (lines 435, 441, 455, 687, 693) plus a 9px eyebrow at 411 and a 10px unread badge at 671. Thread rows therefore carry two 9px pills (kind + status) next to a 9px date. Nine-pixel text with 0.06em tracking is below any reasonable legibility floor, especially for the swap_notice chip's #6B5F52 on rgba(154,138,120,0.16). → _Raise every status/kind chip in this file to 10.5px and the eyebrow to 10.5px, and drop one of the two chips from the row (kind is inferable from the subject icon)._  `[contrast]`

## Organiser — financials, money inputs

- **`src/app/manage/[slug]/financials/VouchersSection.tsx:363`** — Money and quantity fields use `type="number"` (here at 363 and 388, ApplicationFeeSection:171, AddonsSection:238). That means native spinner arrows on hover, and — the real problem — a focused field silently changes value when the organiser scrolls the page with the cursor over it. On a page where the number is a conference's application fee, that is a bad default. → _Switch to `type="text" inputMode="decimal"` with a numeric pattern and `onWheel={e => e.currentTarget.blur()}` as a belt-and-braces guard, and hide the spinners with `appearance: textfield`._  `[polish]`

## Organiser — financials, application fee

- **`src/app/manage/[slug]/financials/ApplicationFeeSection.tsx:191`** — The currency select separates pinned currencies from the rest with `<option disabled>──────</option>`. A screen reader announces that as six dash characters, dimmed, and it is selectable-looking noise in the list. → _Use two `<optgroup label="Common">` / `<optgroup label="All currencies">` blocks — same visual effect, correct semantics, no fake row._  `[polish]`

## Organiser — financials, vouchers

- **`src/app/manage/[slug]/financials/VouchersSection.tsx:359`** — The amount field labels itself 'Amount (%)' and then repeats the unit as a '%' adornment inside the field 8px away — the same information twice. Meanwhile the optional-field convention differs from the rest of the product: 'Max redemptions · optional' and 'Expiry date · optional' here versus 'Placeholder (optional)' / 'Help text (optional)' in QuestionBuilder. → _Keep the in-field adornment and shorten the label to 'Amount'. Standardise on one optional marker — the parenthesised form is the more common one in the codebase._  `[consistency]`

## Organiser — draft-email notices

- **`src/components/DraftNotice.tsx:76`** — The notice sentence is assembled from five inline fragments — 'No email was sent for ‘X’:' / TURN ON / 'to send our default, or' / DRAFT / 'your own.' — with two uppercase underlined links embedded mid-sentence, all in the same amber #8A6614 as the surrounding prose. The result reads as a run-on and the two actions have identical visual weight despite one being a one-click fix and the other a navigation away. → _Split into a sentence plus an action row: 'No email was sent for ‘X’.' on one line, then a TURN ON button (filled) and a 'Draft your own' text link on the next. Also drop the trailing colon after the event name._  `[copy]`
- **`src/components/DraftNotice.tsx:114`** — The dismiss control is a bare `×` character button with `ml-auto`, no padding, no aria-label and no hover state — roughly a 10×16px target. The notice also auto-dismisses after a hard-coded 8s (line 35) with no pause-on-hover, so a slow reader loses the message and its two actions mid-sentence. → _Use lucide X in a 28×28 button with `aria-label="Dismiss"`, and pause the 8s timer while the notice is hovered or focused._  `[polish]`

## Decorative bleed

- **`src/components/DecorativeBleed.tsx:85`** — `rotate` is written into `style.transform`, so any consumer that also wants to translate or scale a bleed item silently loses the rotation (last write wins). The component also has no `prefers-reduced-motion` concern of its own but is mounted inside animated hero areas — and there is exactly one prefers-reduced-motion guard in the whole components + account + auth + participant + manage tree. → _Compose the transform (`transform: [rotate, item.transform].filter(Boolean).join(' ')`) and add one global `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) { *, *::before, *::after { animation-duration: .001ms !important; animation-iteration-count: 1 !important; transition-duration: .001ms !important } }` block to globals.css._  `[polish]`

## Logo disc

- **`src/components/LogoDisc.tsx:129`** — The monogram font size is computed as a fraction of the disc — `max(9, round(size * 0.24))` for 3+ characters in the default 'disc' tone. At the 22px sizes used in dense lists that clamps to 9px gold-on-forest initials, and at the 18–22px avatar sizes the three letters are effectively a smudge. There is also no letter-count guard: `fallbackText` is whatever the caller slices, so a 4-character acronym overflows the disc. → _Clamp fallbackText to 3 characters inside the component, switch to 2 characters below 32px, and raise the small-size floor to 10px._  `[polish]`
- **`src/components/LogoDisc.tsx:95`** — There is no loading state between mount and image decode — the disc renders its #FDFCF9 backdrop with nothing inside, so a slow conference logo shows as a blank white circle rather than the monogram it would fall back to on error. On a list of 20 cards that is 20 empty circles until the network settles. → _Render the monogram underneath the <img> (absolutely positioned) and let the image paint over it, so the fallback is what shows during load as well as on error._  `[missing-state]`

> **Method / limits:** METHOD — Source read only. No dev server, no browser, no edits, no git operations. I read AGENTS.md and CLAUDE.md in full, then src/components/neu.tsx, then worked the completeness critic's uncovered list file by file: the 8 "never mentioned" components (ResolutionsPanel, ChatAvatar, LogoCropModal, CustomQuestionsField, QuestionBuilder, PositionPaperRoster, PositionPaperButtons, AuthProvider — the last is non-visual and correctly skipped), the 5 "read but zero findings" (DecorativeBleed, DraftNotice, DefaultEmailPreviewModal, PersonalConferenceCard, LogoDisc), the 3 "one passing mention" (CVEntryModal, ConferenceRosterPicker, ShareAchievementModal), all 14 of src/components/ui, and the app-local set (authUi, accountUi, CVTimeline, participant/*, manage/live/LiveModals, manage/financials/*, manage/assignment/delegationShared). Every line number and hex value quoted was read from the working tree at audit time.

COVERAGE LIMITS — I did not open RequestsPanel, DelegationPanel, ChairParticipant, PledgeInvoicingCard, AidRequestModal, DelegationsView, IndependentsView, AddonsSection or AidFormEditor end to end; for those I read the header plus grep-profiled for the sweep dimensions and filed only what I could quote exactly. CVEntryModal (1374 lines) and ConferenceRosterPicker (839) were spot-read at their date-entry, overlay and typeahead surfaces rather than in full, so there is almost certainly more in both. I did not re-report anything the critic listed as already covered or verified-fine.

VERIFIED WHILE SWEEPING, WORTH KNOWING —
• Import counts: `grep -rn "components/ui/"` outside src/components/ui returns 8 lines, all in AboutClient.tsx and ContactClient.tsx. alert, avatar, badge, carousel, field, input-group, progress, separator and spinner have zero importers.
• ResolutionsPanel.tsx, SpeakersListPanel.tsx and CaucusPanel.tsx all have zero importers — confirmed dead, not merely unreferenced by the audit.
• 'DM Mono' is referenced in RequestsPanel.tsx:19 but is not loaded by layout.tsx (only Playfair Display ital@1 and Noto Sans Arabic are), so it resolves to the platform monospace.
• Native date pickers: no `type="date"` in any file I opened. The two date-shaped exceptions are CVEntryModal's Month/Year `<select>` pair (filed) and CustomQuestionsField, which correctly uses DatePicker. VouchersSection also uses DatePicker correctly.
• accountUi's ExperienceInfo is a correct implementation of the hover-'i' rule and of the Portal rule — it is the best small component in the uncovered set and is a reasonable template for the others.

NON-UI / FUNCTIONAL OBSERVATIONS (per the brief, kept out of findings) —
• src/components/Portal.tsx:9 targets `document.getElementById('fit-root') ?? document.body`. On session pages #fit-root carries a CSS transform, which makes it the containing block for `position: fixed` descendants — so anything portaled there with `fixed inset-0` is positioned against the transformed box, not the viewport. None of the components I audited are mounted inside FitToScreen (they are all conference/account/manage surfaces), so no finding was filed, but this is the mechanism behind the critic's "move fitScale() into Portal" recommendation and it is real.
• LiveModals.tsx:189 `joinAsSecretariat` opens `/chair/${code}?chairName=Secretariat` — per AGENTS.md a chair's identity IS that string, so every secretariat member who uses this button becomes literally "Secretariat" in chat and feedback, indistinguishable from each other. There is a `TODO(merge)` on the line acknowledging it.
• CVEntryModal.tsx:327 `emit(selMonth >= 0 ? selMonth : 0, selYear >= 0 ? selYear : currentYear)` writes January and the current year when the user has only picked one half of the date. That is a data-correctness issue as much as a UI one.
• QuestionBuilder.tsx:408 renders 'Untitled question' as a fallback label, but `canSave` requires a non-empty label so the branch is unreachable — dead copy.
• accountUi.tsx:515 the last COMMITTEE_LOGO_PRESETS regex ends in `|\bUN\b`, which will match a very wide range of free-text committee names and flatten them all to the generic UN seal.
• accountUi.tsx exports LEVEL_TONE and PersonalConferenceCard exports ROLE_TONE; neither is consumed by the file that declares it — worth a dead-export check across the account surface.


---

# Gap sweep: uncovered-routes  
_124 findings_


## 404 / not-found — every bad URL

- **`src/app/not-found.tsx`** — The file does not exist. Any mistyped conference slug, dead blog link, expired /manage/[slug] or shared session URL falls through to Next's stock black-on-white "404 | This page could not be found" — no SiteNav, no ivory background, no way back into the product. Given the app ships public marketing pages, a blog, a sitemap and shareable conference slugs, this is the most-hit unstyled screen in the product. → _Add src/app/not-found.tsx reusing the exact card shell from src/app/error.tsx (460px max-width, radius 20, NEU.surface, ⚠️ disc) with a compass/map icon instead, copy "We couldn't find that page", and two pills: "Explore conferences" → /conferences/explore and "Go home" → /. Render SiteNav above it so the user is never stranded._  `[missing-state]`

## Route error boundary

- **`src/app/error.tsx:38`** — The card is built from the legacy paper palette — `backgroundColor: '#FAF8F3', border: '1px solid #DDD4C0', borderRadius: 20` — with no neu shadow. Every other current surface is `NEU.surface #F0EBDD` + `NEU.out` at radius 22. The crash card therefore looks like a different product than the page it replaced. → _Switch to NEU.surface + NEU.out + radius 22, drop the 1px border. Keep it dependency-light by inlining the two token strings rather than importing neu.tsx (global-error.tsx's self-containment reasoning does not apply to error.tsx, which sits under the root layout)._  `[consistency]`
- **`src/app/error.tsx:49`** — The failure glyph is a raw `⚠️` emoji at fontSize 26 inside a red-tinted disc. This is the only place the app expresses an error with a system emoji — every other error surface uses a lucide glyph (TriangleAlert in financials/settings:361, ShieldAlert in profile:1116) or a hand-drawn SVG (auth/error:57). The emoji renders differently on every OS and will not tint with the surrounding red. → _Replace with `<TriangleAlert size={24} strokeWidth={2.2} style={{ color: '#8B2020' }} />`, matching src/app/manage/[slug]/financials/settings/page.tsx:361._  `[consistency]`
- **`src/app/error.tsx:56`** — "The team has been alerted automatically — try again, or head back to safety." The phrase "head back to safety" is melodramatic for what is usually a transient chunk failure, and it does not name where "safety" is (the button says "Go home"). The global boundary uses completely different copy for the same event: "Gavelling hit a snag" / "The page couldn't load" (global-error.tsx:57-62). → _Use one string in both files: title "Something went wrong", body "This page didn't load. We've been notified — try again, or go back to the home page." Extract to a shared const so the two boundaries can never drift again._  `[copy]`
- **`src/app/error.tsx:68`** — Both actions carry `focus:outline-none` with no replacement ring (`className="rounded-full focus:outline-none"` on the TRY AGAIN button and the Go home link). On the one screen a user may reach after their pointer-driven flow already broke, neither control is keyboard-visible. → _Swap to `focus-visible:outline focus-visible:outline-2 focus-visible:outline-offset-2` with `outlineColor: '#1B3828'` — the same pattern authUi.tsx:348 already uses for PrimaryActionButton._  `[contrast]`
- **`src/app/error.tsx:90`** — `Reference: {error.digest}` is rendered as plain text in `ui-monospace` at 10.5px in `#B6A88E` on `#FAF8F3` (~2.1:1 contrast). A support reference code that is illegible and not selectable-by-click defeats its own purpose. → _Raise to 11.5px `#9A8A78`, wrap in a pressed-in NEU.inSm chip, and make it a click-to-copy control with a "Copied" acknowledgement — this is the one string a user will be asked to quote._  `[polish]`

## Root error boundary

- **`src/app/global-error.tsx:64`** — The only action is `reset()`. There is no "Go home" escape and no stale-deploy hard-reload branch — src/app/error.tsx:65 correctly hard-reloads when `isStaleDeployError(error)` is true, but the global boundary calls bare `reset()`, which re-requests the same missing chunk and lands the user in the identical dead card. → _Mirror error.tsx's handler: `if (isStaleDeployError(error)) window.location.reload(); else reset();` and add a plain `<a href="/">Go home</a>` (an anchor, not next/link, since the router may be what broke)._  `[missing-state]`

## Every route-level loading state

- **`src/app/conferences/[slug]/loading.tsx:5`** — All six loading.tsx files are byte-identical: a bare 28px spinner centred on an empty ivory field. For /conferences/[slug] the destination is a banner + logo + committee grid; for /conferences/[slug]/pay it is a two-column invoice layout; for /account it is a tab body. In every case the user gets an unstructured void followed by a full-page pop-in, and on /conferences/[slug] specifically the completeness pass already noted spinner → skeleton → content, i.e. three visual states for one navigation. → _Give each route a skeleton that matches its real geometry — the app already has ConferenceCardSkeleton (src/components/PersonalConferenceCard) and a pulse keyframe (MapClient.tsx:477). For /conferences/[slug] render the same skeleton the client already shows so the two states are indistinguishable and the extra transition disappears._  `[missing-state]`

## Account tab loading

- **`src/app/account/loading.tsx:9`** — This one uses `minHeight: '60vh'` and no background colour while the other five use `min-h-screen` + `backgroundColor: '#EDE7D8'`. Because the account layout also renders the grain overlay and sidebar, the spinner lands 60vh down the content column rather than optically centred against the sidebar card. → _Match the content column: `minHeight: 420` (roughly the height of the shortest account page) rather than a viewport-relative value, so the spinner does not migrate vertically between a 700px laptop and a 1400px monitor._  `[consistency]`

## Chair invite — invalid token

- **`src/app/invites/chair/[token]/page.tsx:133`** — `{invite?.error ?? error ?? "This invite link isn't valid…"}` — `error` is initialised to `''` (line 65), and `??` only falls through on null/undefined. So whenever the RPC returns `{ok:false}` without an `error` field, the body renders as an empty string and the carefully written fallback copy is unreachable. The user sees the heading "Invite not found" over blank space, then a button. → _Use `||` instead of `??`, or `invite?.error || error || "This invite link isn't valid. It may have been mistyped or already removed."`. The identical bug is at src/app/invites/organizer/[token]/page.tsx:187 — fix both._  `[copy]`

## Chair invite — pending

- **`src/app/invites/chair/[token]/page.tsx:181`** — While responding, the DECLINE button's style becomes `boxShadow: responding !== null ? 'none' : NEU.outSm`. Removing the shadow entirely makes an extruded neumorphic button collapse flat into the card — it reads as broken, not busy. There is no opacity change, no spinner, and the ACCEPT button (a NeuButton) uses a completely different disabled treatment (`rgba(27,56,40,0.14)` fill + NEU.muted text, neu.tsx:583). → _Keep NEU.outSm and add `opacity: 0.55` while responding — the same convention the delegation portal already uses for busy controls (DelegationPortalClient.tsx:213, 358)._  `[polish]`

## Chair invite — signed in as the wrong account

- **`src/app/invites/chair/[token]/page.tsx:112`** — `InviteData` carries an `email` field (line 26) that is never rendered, and there is no "sign out and switch account" affordance. The sibling organizer invite has one (organizer/[token]/page.tsx:227-235). A chair who is signed into a personal account but was invited on a school address hits a raw RPC error with no idea why and no way out. → _Render `Invited to {invite.email}` under the heading, and port the `handleSwitchAccount` block from the organizer page verbatim. Two sibling routes solving the same problem two different ways is the underlying issue._  `[missing-state]`

## Chair invite vs organizer invite — signed out

- **`src/app/invites/chair/[token]/page.tsx:69`** — Signed-out chair invitees are silently `router.replace`d to /auth/signin (line 70). Signed-out organizer invitees get a designed choice screen with SIGN IN and CREATE ACCOUNT side by side plus reassuring copy (organizer/[token]/page.tsx:117-159). Signed-out delegation invitees are redirected too (delegation/[token]/page.tsx:84). Three invite routes, two behaviours, and the redirecting ones give a brand-new user no signal that creating an account is the right move. → _Use the organizer page's choice screen for all three. It is already written; extract it to a shared `<InviteAuthGate token pathname icon eyebrow />` and drop the redirects._  `[consistency]`

## All three invite pages

- **`src/app/invites/chair/[token]/page.tsx:40`** — `primaryPillStyle` (a 6-line gradient/gold/shadow object) and the `Spinner()` component are copy-pasted verbatim into all three invite files (chair:40+46, delegation:43+49, organizer:41+47). Three copies of the same CTA recipe that exists to imitate NeuButton's rendered state — any tweak to the button system now needs three edits and will inevitably drift. → _Add a `NeuLinkButton({ href, gradient, children })` to src/components/neu.tsx next to NeuButton (it is the same style block plus `as Link`), and a shared `<FullPageSpinner />`. That also fixes the missing hover/press states — the pill is currently completely static while NeuButton lifts and scales._  `[consistency]`

## Chair invite — resolved states

- **`src/app/invites/chair/[token]/page.tsx:159`** — `<Eyebrow>{invite!.acronym}</Eyebrow>` renders the raw acronym with no fallback. A conference with a null acronym (the type marks it optional, line 27) produces an empty gold eyebrow — a 12px band of whitespace above the heading. The MonogramMedallion two lines up already handles this correctly with `acronym || conference_name || '?'`. → _Use the same chain: `<Eyebrow>{invite.acronym || invite.conference_name}</Eyebrow>`, and skip the eyebrow entirely when both are empty._  `[missing-state]`

## Chair invite — committee name

- **`src/app/invites/chair/[token]/page.tsx:161`** — `Chair {invite!.committee_name}` renders the full spelled-out committee name as the h1 at text-xl. AGENTS.md's UI RULE requires long committee names to render as ACRONYM with the full name small beneath, via `committeeDisplayName()` from src/lib/presetNames.ts. "Chair Disarmament and International Security Committee" will wrap to three lines inside a 460px card. → _Apply the rule: h1 reads `Chair DISEC`, with the full name as a 12px muted second line. The delegation portal's local `committeeDisplay()` (DelegationPortalClient.tsx:114-120) already implements exactly this shape — better still, both should call the shared helper._  `[rule-violation]`

## Delegation invite — failure

- **`src/app/invites/delegation/[token]/page.tsx:118`** — The only action on a dead delegation invite is "BROWSE CONFERENCES" → /conferences, which is a bare redirect to '/'. So a delegate who was sent a link by their head delegate, and whose link expired, is dumped on the marketing homepage with no mention of the delegation, the conference, or how to get a fresh link. → _Point the CTA at /conferences/explore (the real directory), and add a second line: "Ask your head delegate or faculty advisor to send you a new link." The resolve RPC already returns `society_name` and `slug` on partial failures — name them when available._  `[wasted-effort]`

## Delegation portal — seat assignment fails

- **`src/app/delegation/[societyId]/DelegationPortalClient.tsx:544`** — `if (error || !result?.ok) { setSeats(prev); setBusyId(null); return; }` — a failed assignment silently rolls the seat back with no message anywhere. The head delegate sees the delegate's name appear in the seat for a moment and then vanish, and will simply try again. There is no error state at all on the only write this page performs. → _Add a `assignError` state rendered as a red inline note on the affected SeatRow (the page already has the pattern at line 718 for `inviteError`), and keep the optimistic name in place with a red "Couldn't save — retry" affordance rather than reverting silently._  `[missing-state]`

## Delegation portal — whole page

- **`src/app/delegation/[societyId]/DelegationPortalClient.tsx:577`** — `const Shell = ({ children }) => (...)` is declared inside the component body, so React sees a brand-new component type on every render and unmounts/remounts the entire subtree — grain overlay, SiteNav, every SeatRow — whenever `busyId` or `seats` changes. Assigning a seat therefore blows away and rebuilds the whole page instead of updating one row. → _Hoist `Shell` above `DelegationPortalClient` as a module-level component taking `children`. Zero visual change, but seat assignment stops flashing the nav and losing scroll position._  `[polish]`

## Delegation portal — roster summary

- **`src/app/delegation/[societyId]/DelegationPortalClient.tsx:371`** — `SummaryStat` is a hand-built stat chip (32px disc, 20px/900 value, 10.5px muted label, radius 16, NEU.outSm) sitting a few lines away from imports of the real `NeuStatTile` system. NeuStatTile in compact mode is 34px disc / 22px value / 10.5px label / radius 22 — near-identical intent, measurably different output, and this page gets none of NeuStatTile's sparkline, delta or active-filter affordances. → _Replace SummaryStat with `<NeuStatTile compact icon={Users} gradient={NEU_GRADIENTS.forest} value={members.length} label="Delegates" />` and delete the local component. Bonus: the Assigned tile can then become a click-to-filter tile (`onClick` + `active`) so a leader with 40 seats can isolate the unassigned ones._  `[consistency]`

## Delegation portal — seat list

- **`src/app/delegation/[societyId]/DelegationPortalClient.tsx:738`** — Seats render as a flat `flex-col gap-2.5` list with no grouping, no search and no filter. A delegation with 30 block seats across 5 committees gets 30 rows, each repeating the same committee logo and name, and the leader must eyeball the whole list to find the unassigned ones. The sort is already committee-then-country, so the grouping information exists and is being thrown away. → _Group by committee with a sticky 11px gold eyebrow header per group (logo + acronym + "3 of 6 assigned"), and add an "Unassigned only" NeuPill filter above the list. Both come almost free from the existing `assignedFor()` and the sort._  `[density]`

## Delegation portal — seat row

- **`src/app/delegation/[societyId]/DelegationPortalClient.tsx:336`** — The UNASSIGNED chip and the assigned-member chip use the identical treatment — `borderRadius: 999, backgroundColor: NEU.base, boxShadow: NEU.inSm`. The only difference is the 24px avatar and the text. At a glance down a 30-row list the two states are indistinguishable, which is precisely the distinction the leader is scanning for. → _Give UNASSIGNED a dashed 1px `rgba(27,56,40,0.22)` outline on the ivory surface (open/empty), and keep the pressed-in inset for filled seats only. Occupied then reads as "seated", empty reads as "slot"._  `[hierarchy]`

## Delegation portal — unassign

- **`src/app/delegation/[societyId]/DelegationPortalClient.tsx:358`** — The unassign control is a 30×30px circular X — a 30px tap target, well under the 44px minimum, on a page a faculty advisor will realistically use on a phone at a conference. It is also destructive (removes a delegate from their seat) with no confirmation and no undo, sitting 10px from the CHANGE button. → _Raise to 36×36 with a 44px hit area via padding, and either move unassign inside the AssignPicker as an "Unassign this seat" row (where it is already contextual) or gate it behind the portaled confirm-popover pattern used by RemovePledgeAction (pay/page.tsx:609)._  `[polish]`

## Delegation portal — invite link

- **`src/app/delegation/[societyId]/DelegationPortalClient.tsx:690`** — The COPY button is `borderRadius: 12` while every other action on the page — CREATE INVITE LINK, ASSIGN/CHANGE, the not-a-leader CTA, the back link — is `borderRadius: 999`. One square-ish button among five pills, at the visual centre of the card. → _Set borderRadius 999 to match. Also lift the copied→green swap into a NEU.outSmHover press state so the button acknowledges physically, not only by colour._  `[consistency]`
- **`src/app/delegation/[societyId]/DelegationPortalClient.tsx:675`** — Once generated, the invite link is permanent and irrevocable in the UI — there is no expiry shown, no revoke, no "3 delegates joined via this link" count. A leader who shares it in a public Discord has no way to shut it off and no way to know it was used. → _Show "Anyone with this link can join your delegation" as a muted caption under the field, plus a usage count and a quiet "Revoke and generate a new link" text action. Even without a backend change, the caption and count are worth having._  `[missing-state]`

## Delegation portal — committee display

- **`src/app/delegation/[societyId]/DelegationPortalClient.tsx:114`** — `committeeDisplay()` reimplements the AGENTS.md acronym rule locally with its own threshold (`name.length > 16 || words >= 3`) rather than calling `committeeDisplayName()` from src/lib/presetNames.ts, and `committeeAbbr()` (line 122) invents a fourth monogram algorithm (first letters, max 4). The result is that the same committee can render one way here and another way on /manage/[slug]/applications. → _Call the shared `committeeDisplayName(name, abbreviation)` helper the rule names, and use the shared `monogramFor()` from accountUi for the LogoDisc fallback so the disc matches the one on the applications page._  `[rule-violation]`

## Delegation portal — assign picker

- **`src/app/delegation/[societyId]/DelegationPortalClient.tsx:236`** — The search box inside the picker only renders when `members.length > 6`. Below that threshold there is no search, which is correct — but there is also no keyboard support at all: no arrow-key navigation, no Enter-to-select, no autofocus on the search field when it does appear. A leader assigning 30 seats must mouse into the menu every single time. → _Autofocus the query input when the menu opens, and add ArrowUp/ArrowDown/Enter handling over `listed`. This is the highest-repetition interaction on the page — it is worth the twenty lines._  `[wasted-effort]`

## Delegation portal — not a leader

- **`src/app/delegation/[societyId]/DelegationPortalClient.tsx:601`** — `!isLeader` collapses two very different situations into one screen: "you are a delegate in this delegation, not its leader" and "this society id does not exist / RLS returned nothing". Both render "You do not manage this delegation", which is confusing and slightly accusatory in the second case. → _Branch on `society === null` → "We couldn't find that delegation" with the explore CTA; keep the current copy only when the society loaded but the leadership check failed, and in that case name the delegation ("You're a delegate in {society.name}, but only its head delegate or faculty advisor can distribute seats")._  `[missing-state]`

## Sign up

- **`src/app/auth/signup/page.tsx:199`** — `setError(error.message)` surfaces Supabase's raw string verbatim. The overwhelmingly common one is "User already registered", which appears in a red banner with no next step — the "Sign in" link is 200px further down in muted 14px text and is not connected to the error at all. → _Map the known cases: on an already-registered address show "An account already exists for {email}" with an inline `Sign in` link and a `Forgot your password?` link inside the banner. Fall back to a generic "We couldn't create your account. Please try again." rather than raw provider text._  `[copy]`
- **`src/app/auth/signup/page.tsx:263`** — Password and DOB validation errors go to `<ErrorBanner>` at the very top of the card (line 263 — above the Google button and the OrDivider), while the email error is rendered inline under its own field (line 290). Two error presentations in one 5-field form, and the top banner is ~340px away from the password field it is about. → _Move password and DOB errors inline under their fields with `aria-invalid` + `aria-describedby`, exactly as the email error already does. Reserve ErrorBanner for form-level failures (network, provider)._  `[hierarchy]`

## Sign up vs Reset password

- **`src/app/auth/signup/page.tsx:297`** — Sign-up takes a single password field with no confirmation; /auth/reset takes New password + Confirm new password (reset/page.tsx:118-133) and validates a mismatch. The higher-stakes case — a typo at account creation, where the user has no existing password to fall back on — is the unguarded one. → _Either add a confirm field to sign-up, or rely on PasswordField's show/hide eye in both and drop the confirm from reset. Pick one model; the current split is backwards._  `[consistency]`

## Sign up — date of birth

- **`src/app/auth/signup/page.tsx:49`** — DOB is a hard requirement at account creation, justified by an 11px footnote ("Some conferences use this to check age requirements"). Most signups are chairs, organisers and browsers who will never hit an age gate, and requiring a birthday before a stranger has seen anything of the product is a measurable drop-off point. → _Move DOB into /auth/onboarding as a fifth (skippable) step, or collect it lazily at the first conference application that declares an age requirement. Keep only the 13+ self-attest at signup if a legal minimum is required._  `[wasted-effort]`

## Auth — shared layout

- **`src/app/auth/authUi.tsx:316`** — The file is internally split on focus rings: `PrimaryButton` (:316), `GoogleButton` (:390), `TextField` (:438), `PasswordField` (:478) and its eye toggle (:488), the ConferencesWordmark link (:47) and the Back link (:138) all carry `focus:outline-none` with no replacement, while `PrimaryActionButton` (:348), `PrimaryLinkButton` (:369) and `SecondaryButton` (:639) all correctly use `focus-visible:outline … outlineColor: '#1B3828'`. The newer primitives got rings; the older ones did not. → _Apply the `focus-visible:outline focus-visible:outline-2 focus-visible:outline-offset-2` + `outlineColor:'#1B3828'` pattern to all seven. It is the same three tokens already present in the file._  `[contrast]`
- **`src/app/auth/authUi.tsx:386`** — GoogleButton and PrimaryButton are both full-width, radius 12, solid `#1B3828` with `#EED98A` text, and both hover to `#2A5A3C`. On the sign-up card that means two visually identical primaries stacked with only a thin "or" divider between (signup:265 and :306). The user has no signal which path is recommended, and the OAuth button — the faster one — sits above the fold looking like the submit button for the form below it. → _Demote GoogleButton to the SecondaryButton recipe already in this file (transparent fill, 1.5px `#1B3828` border, forest text) and keep the solid forest fill for the one true submit. That instantly gives the card a hierarchy without any layout change._  `[hierarchy]`
- **`src/app/auth/authUi.tsx:488`** — The password show/hide toggle is an 18px SVG in a button with no padding, absolutely positioned at `right-3`. The tap target is roughly 18×18px — under half the 44px minimum — and it is the control users reach for most on a phone keyboard. → _Give the button `padding: 10px` and `margin: -10px` so the hit area grows to 38px without moving the glyph, and add a focus-visible ring._  `[polish]`

## Auth — brand panel

- **`src/app/auth/authUi.tsx:239`** — `<BrandStat value="Conferences" label="Run end-to-end" />` sits directly beside `<BrandStat value="120+" label="Countries" />`. The component's whole design is number-on-top / noun-underneath (bold 14px value, 11px muted label), so the second one reads as a broken stat where the number failed to load. → _Either give it a real figure ("40+ / Conferences run") or drop the second BrandStat and let "120+ Countries" stand alone with the divider removed._  `[copy]`

## Auth — brand panel on tablet/mobile

- **`src/app/auth/authUi.tsx:215`** — The headline block is `hidden lg:block`, so below 1024px the eyebrow, headline and sub that every auth page passes in as props render nowhere. On a tablet the left panel becomes a 230px photo strip containing only a logo square and two stats; on a phone, 190px. Four pages each maintain three strings of copy that are invisible to most mobile signups. → _Show a condensed version below lg — the headline at ~22px overlaid on the photo strip — or drop the props on small screens and move the value proposition into `CardHeading`'s `sub` so it is seen on every viewport._  `[responsive]`

## Auth — notice screens

- **`src/app/auth/authUi.tsx:617`** — `IconBadge` renders a neutral `rgba(27,56,40,0.1)` forest disc regardless of meaning. It holds the green CheckMark on "You're verified" (signup:223) AND the info-circle on "This link has expired" (auth/error:56) AND "This reset link has expired" (reset:98). Success and failure are visually identical. → _Add a `tone` prop: `'success' | 'info' | 'error'` mapping to `rgba(61,122,82,0.12)` / `rgba(27,56,40,0.10)` / `rgba(139,32,32,0.08)`, matching the tint already used by error.tsx:46._  `[consistency]`

## Auth — expired-link icon

- **`src/app/auth/error/page.tsx:57`** — The 24px info-circle SVG is written out inline here and again, character for character, at src/app/auth/reset/page.tsx:100-104. ## Reset password — success

- **`src/app/auth/reset/page.tsx:82`** — The action node is wrapped in `<div style={{ marginTop: 24 }}>` and then dropped into `NoticeScreen`, whose action slot already applies `mt-6` (24px). The button therefore sits 48px below the body copy here, versus 24px on /auth/error and /auth/signup's verified screen — a visible rhythm break between three screens using the same component. → _Delete the wrapper div and pass `<PrimaryLinkButton>` directly, as auth/error:66 does._  `[consistency]`
- **`src/app/auth/reset/page.tsx:83`** — After updating their password the user is sent to `/` with the label "CONTINUE TO GAVELLING". This page never reads `?next=`, so someone who started at an invite link, a conference application or /manage and got bounced through forgot → email → reset ends up on the marketing homepage and has to find their way back manually. → _Thread `next` through: /auth/forgot's `redirectTo` (forgot:28) hardcodes `?next=/auth/reset` — make it `?next=/auth/reset%3Fnext%3D{original}` and have this page honour it via the same `safeNext()` helper the other auth pages use._  `[wasted-effort]`

## Forgot password

- **`src/app/auth/forgot/page.tsx:28`** — `resetPasswordForEmail` hardcodes `redirectTo: .../auth/callback?next=/auth/reset` and the page never reads or forwards its own `?next=`. Combined with the finding above, the entire password-recovery path is a one-way trip that destroys the user's destination — even though `safeNext`/`withNext` exist in authUi and every other auth page uses them. → _Read `safeNext(searchParams, '')` on mount and append it to the reset URL; also use `withNext('/auth/signin', searchParams)` for the two "Back to sign in" / "Sign in" links here (lines 75 and 111) which currently drop it._  `[wasted-effort]`

## Onboarding — every card and button

- **`src/app/auth/onboarding/page.tsx:301`** — `transition: 'all 200ms'` on the add-conference button (:301), `transition: 'all 200ms'` on ConfSummaryRow (:363) and `transition: 'all 220ms'` on StepFooter (:440). `all` animates layout-affecting properties too — the StepFooter one transitions `background`, `color`, `boxShadow`, `textDecoration` and `opacity` together, so switching from the underlined-skip state to the filled-primary state cross-fades the underline through a half-drawn line. → _Enumerate: `box-shadow 220ms ${EASE}, transform 220ms ${EASE}, background-color 220ms ${EASE}, color 220ms ${EASE}`, matching the explicit lists used throughout neu.tsx._  `[polish]`

## Onboarding — step 1

- **`src/app/auth/onboarding/page.tsx:172`** — `pickEducation` auto-advances after a 320ms timeout, but steps 2, 3 and 4 all require pressing the footer button. The user learns "picking advances" on the first screen and then waits on the second one wondering why nothing happened after choosing a country. → _Drop the auto-advance and let step 1 use the same StepFooter as everything else — or make single-select steps 1 and 3 both auto-advance. Consistency matters more than the saved click._  `[consistency]`

## Onboarding — footer

- **`src/app/auth/onboarding/page.tsx:437`** — In the un-answered state StepFooter renders `color: NEU.muted` (#9A8A78) underlined text on the #EDE7D8 page — about 2.6:1, below AA for 14px — and it is the only forward control on the screen. The `→` arrow is identical in both states, so the styling is the only thing distinguishing "Skip this question" from "Continue". → _Darken the skip state to `#6B5F52` (≈4.6:1) and drop the arrow when the label says Skip, so the two intents differ by shape as well as weight._  `[contrast]`

## Onboarding — step 2 (countries)

- **`src/app/auth/onboarding/page.tsx:240`** — All 193 UN countries are pushed into a 3-column `CardSelect` with a search box. There is no pre-selection from the profile's nationality, no "popular" cluster, and no cap — a brand-new user is handed a 65-row grid on the second screen of their first minute in the product, in answer to a question ("Which countries do you usually do MUN in?") most delegates will answer with exactly one value. → _Seed the list with the 12 countries with the most live conferences, show the rest behind the existing search, and pre-check the user's nationality if the profile already has one. Also reword to "Where do you usually attend conferences?" — "do MUN in" is jargon-adjacent and ambiguous._  `[density]`

## Onboarding — step 4

- **`src/app/auth/onboarding/page.tsx:286`** — The add-a-conference button is `border: 2px dashed rgba(27,56,40,0.18)` at radius 18; the ConfSummaryRow immediately above it is `border: 1.5px solid ${type.border}` at radius 18. Two adjacent cards in one stack with different border weights and styles, both carrying NEU.outSm — a dashed card with an extruded shadow is contradictory (dashed reads "empty slot", the shadow reads "raised object"). → _Drop NEU.outSm from the dashed add button and give it the NEU.inSm pressed-in well treatment instead, so it reads as a slot to fill rather than another card._  `[consistency]`

## Onboarding — resilience

- **`src/app/auth/onboarding/page.tsx:54`** — `step`, `education`, `countries` and `level` are all plain useState and are only persisted at finish/skip (`persist()` at :100). A refresh, an accidental back gesture or a tab crash halfway through discards everything and drops the user at step 1 with no explanation, right after they typed a birthday and a password. → _Persist each answer as it is chosen (the writes are tiny and `persist()` is already idempotent), and restore `step` from a `?step=` search param so browser Back moves between questions instead of leaving the flow._  `[missing-state]`

## Onboarding — top bar

- **`src/app/auth/onboarding/page.tsx:195`** — The gavel mark in the top-left is a bare `<img>`, not a link — the one universally expected escape hatch on a wizard is inert. The only exit is "I'll do this later →" on the far right, styled as 14px muted text with `focus:outline-none` and no ring. → _Wrap the mark in `<Link href="/">`, and give the skip control a focus-visible ring. `NEU.base ?? ## Account shell — every /account page

- **`src/app/account/layout.tsx:359`** — `{children}` is rendered twice: once inside `<div className="hidden md:flex">` (line 360) and again inside `<div className="md:hidden">` (line 366). ## Account shell — mobile tab bar

- **`src/app/account/layout.tsx:79`** — Five uppercase 0.05em-tracked labels ("MY PROFILE", "MUN CV", "CONFERENCE CALENDAR", "CREDITS & SUBSCRIPTION", "MY CONFERENCES") in a single `overflow-x-auto` row. ## Account shell — avatar

- **`src/app/account/layout.tsx:146`** — `<img src={profile.avatar_url} …>` with no `onError`. A rotated Supabase URL, a deleted object or a Google avatar that 403s renders the browser's broken-image icon inside a 64px circle with a 2px border. The initials fallback exists on the very next branch but is only reached when the URL is null, not when it fails. The 144px avatar on /account/profile:711 has the identical gap. → _Add `onError={() => setAvatarBroken(true)}` and fall through to the initials block, or route both through the existing `LogoDisc`/`MemberAvatar` components, which already handle load failure._  `[missing-state]`

- **`src/app/account/layout.tsx:284`** — SIGN OUT is a left-aligned 12px text button sitting directly under a divider identical to the one above the nav list, giving it the exact visual grammar of another nav row — and it fires immediately with no confirmation. ## Account shell — card system

- **`src/app/account/layout.tsx:134`** — The sidebar card is glass — `linear-gradient(180deg, rgba(252,250,246,0.95), rgba(245,241,232,0.9))`, `backdrop-filter: blur(16px) saturate(1.5)`, `1px solid #D8CDB6`, a bespoke three-part shadow, radius 22.
