Two labels and two visual weights for the identical concept, in adjacent cards. → _Use one: `<NeuPill active gradient={NEU_GRADIENTS.green}><Check/> CURRENT PLAN</NeuPill>` in every card that is the user's plan._  `[consistency]`

## Credits & Subscription — cancel path

- **`src/app/account/unlimited/page.tsx:726`** — "MANAGE SUBSCRIPTION" — the only route to cancelling or updating a card — is a 12px underlined text link in `NEU.muted`, positioned under the ACTIVE pill and visually below every other element on the card. For a paid subscriber it is the single most likely reason they opened this page. → _Promote it to a bordered pill (SecondaryButton weight) directly under the ACTIVE chip, and rename to "Manage or cancel" so the user does not have to guess that Stripe's portal is behind it._  `[hierarchy]`

## Credits & Subscription — quantity stepper

- **`src/app/account/unlimited/page.tsx:645`** — The − and + buttons are 26×26px — under the 44px tap minimum on a page that ends in a payment. At the 1 and 20 bounds the only feedback is `opacity: 0.5`, with no message explaining the 20 cap. → _Raise to 32×32 with padding-based 44px hit areas, and add a muted "Max 20 at a time" caption once `buyQty === 20`. Also make the quantity a typeable input — buying 12 credits currently takes eleven taps._  `[polish]`

## World map — hover feedback

- **`src/app/conferences/map/MapClient.tsx:544`** — The continent polygons are `fill="transparent" stroke="transparent"` and hovering them sets `hovered` state that nothing consumes — `hoveredDef` and `tooltipPos` are computed at lines 447-448 and never rendered. The result is a desktop map with literally no hover feedback: no highlight, no continent name, no count, only `cursor: pointer`. There is no way to tell where a hot zone is except by moving the mouse and watching the cursor. → _Render the dead code that already exists: a forest-tinted `fill="rgba(238,217,138,0.18)"` + gold stroke on the hovered polygon, and a small pill at `tooltipPos` reading the continent label and its live count. This is the single largest usability gap on the page and 90% of the work is already done._  `[missing-state]`

## World map — desktop entry gesture

- **`src/app/conferences/map/MapClient.tsx:336`** — The on-screen hint reads "TAP A CONTINENT TO EXPLORE" (line 911) but on desktop tapping is only half the story — `handleWheel` fires `enterContinent(hoveredRef.current)` on ANY wheel event with no deltaY check and no threshold (lines 336-340). Idly scrolling with the cursor resting over Europe triggers a 1.7-second cloud transition the user never asked for, and scrolling *up* enters a continent too. → _Either delete the wheel entry entirely (click already works and is what the copy promises), or gate it on `e.deltaY > 30` with a debounce and change the hint to "SCROLL OR CLICK A CONTINENT". As it stands the copy is wrong and the gesture is accidental._  `[copy]`

## World map — transition

- **`src/app/conferences/map/MapClient.tsx:312`** — Both `enterContinent` and `exitContinent` hard-wait `setTimeout(…, 1700)` for the cloud video before doing anything. Comparing two continents costs 3.4 seconds of unskippable animation, and the data fetch does not even start until the timeout fires (line 304), so the skeleton appears *after* the 1.7s wait rather than during it. → _Kick off `fetchActiveConferences`/`fetchContinentDetail` immediately in `enterContinent`, before the timeout, so the card is populated the instant the clouds clear. Cut the transition to ~900ms, and let a click during the clouds skip to the end._  `[wasted-effort]`

## World map — motion

- **`src/app/conferences/map/MapClient.tsx:563`** — Two continuous motion sources run unguarded: the cloud `<video>` autoplays on every transition, and the cursor-trail canvas runs a permanent `requestAnimationFrame` loop (line 428) drawing 28 white trail segments on every mousemove, with no pause on blur and no `prefers-reduced-motion` check anywhere in the file. → _Wrap both in `window.matchMedia('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)')`: skip the video (cross-fade the maps instead) and disable the trail. Also `cancelAnimationFrame` when `document.visibilityState !== 'visible'` — the loop currently burns a core in a background tab._  `[polish]`

## World map — viewport sizing

- **`src/app/conferences/map/MapClient.tsx:454`** — The root is `width: '100vw', height: '100vh'`. `100vw` includes the scrollbar gutter, so on any desktop browser showing a vertical scrollbar the page gains a horizontal scrollbar; `100vh` on mobile is the pre-toolbar height, so the bottom-anchored controls (`bottom: 32`, `bottom: 92`) sit under the browser chrome on iOS Safari. → _`width: '100%'` and `height: '100dvh'` with `100vh` as a fallback. This route is outside FitToScreen so dvh is safe here._  `[responsive]`

## World map — continent card collision

- **`src/app/conferences/map/MapClient.tsx:224`** — For Europe and Africa `getContinentLayout` places the info card at `{bottom: 48, left: 48}` (line 224). The "← BACK TO MAP" button in the same phase is at `{bottom: 32, left: 48}` (line 982). The card is 200px+ tall and starts 48px from the bottom, so it sits directly on top of the back button between y=48 and y=66 — on two of the six continents the primary escape control is covered by the card. → _Move the continent-phase back button to the opposite corner from `cardPosition` (mirror the same switch `getContinentLayout` already performs), or place it top-left under the nav where it cannot collide with anything._  `[responsive]`

## World map — the hero number

- **`src/app/conferences/map/MapClient.tsx:764`** — The card's hero figure is `activeCount`, defined as conferences where `start_date <= today <= end_date` — i.e. literally in session right now. That is 0 essentially always. And the 0-state copy only renders when there is ALSO no highlighted conference, so the common case is a 46px gold **0** with "conferences in session" beside it and a live Spotlight conference chip directly underneath. The card contradicts itself. → _Make the hero figure "conferences listed" or "upcoming this year" (`confs.length` / start_date >= today, both already fetched at line 155), and demote "in session now" to a small green live chip that appears only when the count is non-zero._  `[copy]`

## World map — continent card

- **`src/app/conferences/map/MapClient.tsx:606`** — The card is `#FAF8F3` + `1px solid #DDD4C0` + `borderRadius: 16` + a bespoke `0 8px 40px rgba(27,56,40,0.25)` shadow, and the file never imports neu.tsx at all. Inside that card there are four radii — 16 (card), 12 (spotlight chip), 10 (EXPLORE CTA and the 0-state icon square), 24 (hint pill) — and two eyebrow treatments: "On the board" at 11px/700 and "Spotlight" at 12px/500, both gold uppercase. → _Port the card to NeuCard (NEU.surface, NEU.out, radius 22), unify the inner radii to 12, and use one eyebrow spec (11px/800/0.14em, matching financial-aid:19)._  `[consistency]`

## World map — CTA

- **`src/app/conferences/map/MapClient.tsx:828`** — `EXPLORE {LABEL} →` is a flat `#1B3828` rectangle at `borderRadius: 10` with no shadow, no hover state and no press feedback — the only non-pill, non-lifting primary CTA in the conferences area. Its label also grows unbounded: "EXPLORE SOUTH AMERICA →" in an 11px/0.08em uppercase inside a 340px-max card. → _Use NeuButton with `NEU_GRADIENTS.forest` (pill, gradient, hover lift, scale-on-press), and shorten the label to "EXPLORE →" with the continent name already being the card's h2 directly above it._  `[consistency]`

## World map — skeleton

- **`src/app/conferences/map/MapClient.tsx:700`** — The loading skeleton is two 20px grey bars. What replaces them is a 46px numeral plus a ~62px spotlight chip — roughly 110px of content in place of 48px of skeleton. The card visibly jumps taller the moment data lands, immediately after a 1.7s transition. → _Shape the skeleton to the real content: one 46px block for the numeral and one 60px rounded block for the chip. `pulse-skeleton` is already defined at line 477._  `[missing-state]`

## World map — keyboard and screen readers

- **`src/app/conferences/map/MapClient.tsx:545`** — The `<polygon>` hot zones have no `tabIndex`, no `role`, no `onKeyDown` and no accessible name, and the tappable mobile fallback list is `md:hidden` — so on a desktop viewport a keyboard user has literally zero reachable controls on this page apart from the nav and the back link. The world image also carries `alt="World Map"` on a purely decorative background, which screen readers announce as content. → _Give each polygon `role="button" tabIndex={0} aria-label={cont.label} onKeyDown={Enter/Space}` with a visible focus fill, and change both map images to `alt="" aria-hidden`._  `[contrast]`

## World map — mobile

- **`src/app/conferences/map/MapClient.tsx:946`** — On phones the map itself is inert (hover zones are stretched with `preserveAspectRatio="none"` and do not line up), so the experience is a full-bleed `object-fit: cover` world photo — which on portrait crops to a slice of ocean — with a floating 2-column grid of six text buttons at `bottom: 92`. The buttons are `padding: '12px 10px'` ≈ 41px tall, just under the tap minimum. → _On mobile, drop the cover crop to `object-fit: contain` so the map is at least recognisable behind the picker, raise the buttons to 48px, and add each continent's live count to its button so the list carries information the map cannot._  `[responsive]`

## World map — nav recolouring

- **`src/app/conferences/map/MapClient.tsx:481`** — SiteNav is forced onto the dark forest bar by injecting `.map-nav-wrapper a, .map-nav-wrapper button { color: #EDE7D8 !important; }`. Any nav element that is neither an `<a>` nor a `<button>` — spans, badges, the language chip, an avatar initial — keeps its ink-on-ivory colour and becomes near-invisible on `rgba(27,56,40,0.72)`. → _Give SiteNav an explicit `tone="dark"` prop that sets its own colours, rather than overriding from outside with `!important` and a descendant selector that cannot know what it is hitting._  `[contrast]`

## Pay & Financial Aid — heading

- **`src/app/conferences/[slug]/pay/page.tsx:518`** — The page title is a `<p>` at `fontSize: 22, fontWeight: 900`. There is no `<h1>` anywhere on a route that handles money. Below it, the conference name is a 12.5px muted line — so the identity of what you are paying for is the smallest text in the header. → _Make the title an `<h1>` and add the conference logo + name at 14px semibold beside it (LogoDisc is already used elsewhere in this flow). Someone confirming a payment should see the conference brand, not read it in 12.5px grey._  `[hierarchy]`

## Pay & Financial Aid — right column

- **`src/app/conferences/[slug]/pay/page.tsx:2174`** — The 300px right column always renders four ActionRows. For an ordinary delegate — the overwhelming majority of users on this page — three of them ("Add Delegation Spots", "Buy Advisor Tickets", "Buy Delegation Credits") are permanently dimmed to 0.55 with the subtitle "Delegation leaders only", and clicking any of them opens a full modal overlay to display one sentence saying so. A third of the page is dedicated to teaching a delegate about features they can never use. → _Render the three delegation controls only when `canBuyDelegationStuff` is true, grouped under a "Delegation tools" eyebrow. If they must stay visible for discoverability, replace the modal with a native `title` / inline caption — a modal for one read-only sentence is a heavy price for a misclick._  `[wasted-effort]`

## Pay & Financial Aid — dimmed actions

- **`src/app/conferences/[slug]/pay/page.tsx:221`** — `ActionRow` keeps `hover` on even when `dimmed`, so an unavailable card still lifts and brightens under the cursor while sitting at 0.55 opacity. The card promises interactivity and then delivers an apology. Opacity alone is also how the page signals "busy" elsewhere, so the same visual means two things. → _When `dimmed`, drop the hover lift, swap the icon disc to a muted `NEU_GRADIENTS.sage`-free grey, and add a small `<Lock size={13}>` on the right — `Lock` is already imported at line 32 and currently unused for this purpose._  `[polish]`

## Pay & Financial Aid — tabs

- **`src/app/conferences/[slug]/pay/page.tsx:1845`** — CURRENT INVOICES / PAYMENTS are `text-xs` outline pills whose selected state is only a border-colour change plus a 6% forest tint — one of the weakest selected states in the product, next to the applications page's saturated gradient StatusPills. The count is shown on PAYMENTS but not on CURRENT INVOICES, so the two tabs are also asymmetric. → _Use `NeuPill active gradient={NEU_GRADIENTS.forest}` for the selected tab and the plain extruded NeuPill for the other — the component is one import away and gives a real filled/unfilled distinction. Add a count to both._  `[hierarchy]`

## Pay & Financial Aid — invoice selection

- **`src/app/conferences/[slug]/pay/page.tsx:1891`** — Invoice selection uses a raw `<input type="checkbox">` at 16×16 with `accentColor: NEU.forest` — the only native checkbox on any surface in this sweep, in a design system that hand-builds toggles (PillToggle), pills, steppers and discs. It is also a 16px tap target on a payment screen. → _Build a 22px neu checkbox: NEU.base + NEU.inSm well when unchecked, forest gradient + white Check glyph when checked, with a 44px hit area. That also fixes the fact that the checkbox currently has no visible focus state._  `[consistency]`

## Pay & Financial Aid — total bar

- **`src/app/conferences/[slug]/pay/page.tsx:2128`** — The "Total (n selected)" row and the PAY SELECTED button render *below* the invoice list and only once something is checked. With a role fee plus an application fee plus three add-ons the list is well over a viewport, so the running total the user is building scrolls out of sight exactly as they build it. The button also shows a count — `PAY SELECTED (3)` — while the amount sits in a separate row above it. → _Make the total a sticky footer bar (`position: sticky; bottom: 0`) inside the left column with a NEU.surface backdrop, and put the money on the button: `PAY $85.00 · 3 INVOICES`. Confirming a payment should never require looking in two places._  `[hierarchy]`

## Pay & Financial Aid — buttons

- **`src/app/conferences/[slug]/pay/page.tsx:2064`** — `NeuButton` is never imported on this page (see the import block at lines 27-52) even though NeuCard, NeuIconDisc and the NEU tokens are. Every CTA — PAY, PAY SELECTED, SUBMIT, CANCEL, SIGN IN, GOT IT — is a hand-rolled `rounded-xl` forest rectangle with `transition-colors` and no hover lift, no press scale and no shadow. The most transactional page in the product has the least tactile buttons. → _Import NeuButton and use it for all six. It brings the pill radius, the gradient, the hover lift and the 0.96 scale-on-press for free, and removes ~40 lines of duplicated inline style._  `[consistency]`

## Pay & Financial Aid — disabled buttons

- **`src/app/conferences/[slug]/pay/page.tsx:2066`** — `backgroundColor: paying ? '#DDD4C0' : NEU.forest, color: paying ? '#9A8A78' : NEU.gold` — the disabled fill is #DDD4C0 with #9A8A78 text, about 1.9:1, which reads as broken rather than temporarily unavailable. Worse, the identical treatment is used for two different meanings: "busy, checkout is opening" (line 2066) and "invalid, you haven't chosen a file" (ProofUploadModal line 1478). → _Busy → keep the forest fill, add a spinner and 0.7 opacity. Invalid → keep the forest fill at 0.45 opacity so it still reads as the primary action you have not yet unlocked. Never grey a primary to #DDD4C0._  `[polish]`

## Pay & Financial Aid — modals

- **`src/app/conferences/[slug]/pay/page.tsx:1405`** — `ProofUploadModal` (line 1405) and the stub-message modal (line 2268) are `backgroundColor: '#FAF8F3', border: '1px solid #DDD4C0', rounded-2xl` — the legacy paper card — while every card behind them on the same page is a NeuCard. Opening a modal visibly changes the design system. → _Convert both to NEU.surface + NEU.out + radius 22. Since the overlay dims the page anyway, no border is needed._  `[consistency]`

## Pay & Financial Aid — proof upload

- **`src/app/conferences/[slug]/pay/page.tsx:1426`** — The proof uploader accepts `image/*` only, and the helper text says "JPG or PNG, up to 10MB". Bank transfer confirmations, UPI receipts and PayPal statements are very often PDFs, and the modal's own copy invites "a receipt or a transfer confirmation". A user with a PDF receipt has to screenshot it before they can pay. → _Add `application/pdf` to the accept list and to the copy, and render a file-name chip instead of an image preview when a PDF is chosen. The 10MB rule should also be stated before the picker opens, not only in the caption below the drop zone._  `[wasted-effort]`

## Pay & Financial Aid — voucher input

- **`src/app/conferences/[slug]/pay/page.tsx:1996`** — The voucher field carries the Tailwind `uppercase` class, which transforms only the rendering — `voucherCode` still holds exactly what was typed. A user typing "earlybird10" sees EARLYBIRD10 on screen and, if the code is rejected, has no way to tell that a lowercase string was submitted. The APPLY button's busy label is also just `'...'`, which reads as truncated text rather than progress. → _Normalise in the handler — `applyVoucher(voucherCode.trim().toUpperCase())` — so what is shown is what is sent. Change the busy label to `APPLYING…` to match every other busy label on the page._  `[polish]`

## Pay & Financial Aid — empty states

- **`src/app/conferences/[slug]/pay/page.tsx:2121`** — Both empty states are a bare NeuCard with one 12.5px muted sentence: "Nothing to pay right now." (line 2121) and "No payments recorded yet." (line 1530). No icon, no explanation of what would put something there, no next step — and the Payments one is what a user sees on their *first* visit to that tab, which is most visits. → _Give both an icon disc and a second line: "Nothing to pay right now — invoices appear here once your application is accepted"; "No payments yet — anything you pay will be recorded here with its receipt." The map card's designed 0-state (MapClient.tsx:723) is the right shape to copy._  `[missing-state]`

## Pay & Financial Aid — status pills

- **`src/app/conferences/[slug]/pay/page.tsx:151`** — `BadgePill` (line 151: 10px, weight 700, letterSpacing 0.08em) and `BatchStatusPill` (line 1508: 10px, weight 800, letterSpacing 0.06em) are two nearly-identical tinted pills in the same file, already drifted on two axes. Both differ again from the applications page's `StatusPill`, which is a saturated gradient with a white glyph. Three pill languages for "status" across the payment/application surface. → _Collapse BadgePill and BatchStatusPill into one component with a status→tone map, and align its spec with the applications StatusPill so a delegate's PAID chip looks the same wherever they see it._  `[consistency]`

## Pay & Financial Aid — amber note

- **`src/app/conferences/[slug]/pay/page.tsx:187`** — The same amber advisory box is written three times in this file: `NOTE_TONES.amber` via `<Note>` (line 163), re-implemented inline in `PaymentsNotSetUp` (line 187), and again inline in `ManualPayAction`'s awaiting-review chip (line 565). All three use `rgba(184,132,74,0.1)` / `rgba(184,132,74,0.24)` but with different padding, radius and text colours (#B8844A vs #8A6614). → _Route all three through `<Note tone="amber">`, adding an optional `icon` prop for the Clock and the heading line. One box, one spec._  `[consistency]`

## Position paper — review thread

- **`src/app/conferences/[slug]/papers/[paperId]/page.tsx:445`** — `const alignRight = m.is_reviewer;` — bubbles align by *role*, not by author. For a chair that happens to look normal (own messages right). For the delegate who wrote the paper, their own messages sit on the LEFT in white while the chair's sit on the RIGHT in forest — the inverse of every chat convention and of Gavelling's own ChatPanel. A delegate reading feedback will read the thread as if someone else wrote their words. → _Use `const alignRight = mine;` (the variable is already computed on the line above). Distinguish reviewer messages by a small "Chair" chip or a gold left border instead of by side._  `[consistency]`
- **`src/app/conferences/[slug]/papers/[paperId]/page.tsx:452`** — Incoming bubbles are `backgroundColor: '#FFFFFF'` with a `1px solid rgba(221,212,192,0.9)` border. neu.tsx's own header states the system is "never white cards" — pure white punches a hole in the warm ivory surface, and the bubble sits inside a NeuCard whose surface is #F0EBDD. → _Use `NEU.surface` with `NEU.outSm` and no border, matching every other raised element in the system._  `[rule-violation]`

## Position paper — header meta

- **`src/app/conferences/[slug]/papers/[paperId]/page.tsx:370`** — Four facts are concatenated into one 12.5px muted line with `·` separators: committee · submitters · "Submitted {date}" · "Due {date}". The deadline — the thing that determines whether the paper is late and the reason the LATE chip exists — is last and least legible, and with two co-submitters the line will wrap awkwardly under a 30px flag. → _Keep committee + delegates on the meta line, and move the dates into two small labelled chips beside the status pill: "Submitted 3 Mar" and "Due 1 Mar" (the latter in amber when late). The chips then explain the LATE badge instead of competing with it._  `[density]`

## Position paper — status controls

- **`src/app/conferences/[slug]/papers/[paperId]/page.tsx:389`** — APPROVE and REJECT are conditionally unmounted based on the current status (`paper.status !== 'approved'` / `!== 'rejected'`), so acting on a paper makes a button disappear rather than become selected. Combined with `savingStatus` disabling both with no spinner or label change, the reviewer gets no confirmation that anything happened beyond the status chip quietly changing. → _Keep both buttons mounted and render the current one as selected (filled) and the other as outline, so the control reads as a two-state switch. Add "SAVING…" to the pressed button while `savingStatus`._  `[polish]`

## Position paper — PDF pane

- **`src/app/conferences/[slug]/papers/[paperId]/page.tsx:504`** — The paper renders in a bare `<iframe src={paper.file_url}>` with no fallback. Mobile Safari and most Android browsers refuse to render PDFs inline, so on a phone the right-hand 480px card is simply blank — and this is a page chairs will open on a phone between committee sessions. There is no loading state either; the iframe is white until the file arrives. → _Detect the failure (or just always, on small viewports) and render a placeholder card with a document icon, the file name and size, and the existing DOWNLOAD button enlarged — so the pane is never an empty white rectangle._  `[missing-state]`

## Position paper — composer

- **`src/app/conferences/[slug]/papers/[paperId]/page.tsx:471`** — The key handler is `if (e.key === 'Enter' && !e.shiftKey)` — i.e. it deliberately reserves Shift+Enter for a newline — but the control is a single-line `<input>`, so Shift+Enter does nothing. Chairs writing multi-paragraph feedback on a position paper get one line with no wrapping and no way to break it. → _Switch to an auto-growing `<textarea>` capped at ~5 rows, keeping the same Enter/Shift+Enter contract. Also add a hint ("Shift + Enter for a new line") beside the send button._  `[copy]`

## Position paper — button shapes

- **`src/app/conferences/[slug]/papers/[paperId]/page.tsx:107`** — `SendButton` is `width: 38, height: 38, borderRadius: 12` while `SignInLink` twenty lines away is `rounded-full`, and the header's ActionButtons are pills. Three shapes for three buttons on one screen, all built with the same hover/press physics. → _Make SendButton `borderRadius: 999` — a circular send disc is also the stronger affordance next to a pill-shaped input well._  `[consistency]`

## Financials → Settings — country picker

- **`src/app/manage/[slug]/financials/settings/page.tsx:414`** — The payout country is chosen with a native `<select>` containing all 193 UN countries, twice (wizard step 1 at line 414, steady-state row at line 611) — no flags, no search, no typeahead. Yet the *result* is displayed with a `<FlagImg>` chip (line 632), and the rest of the app picks countries with flag typeaheads (profile's nationality field, ConferenceRosterPicker, onboarding's CardSelect). The picker has no flags but the answer does. → _Reuse the profile page's portaled flag typeahead (profile/page.tsx:846-898) for both instances. Organisers pick a country once, but they pick it while nervous about payouts — a scrolling native list of 193 options is the wrong first impression._  `[consistency]`
- **`src/app/manage/[slug]/financials/settings/page.tsx:419`** — Both selects set `border: 'none', outline: 'none'` with an inset shadow and no replacement focus style. A native `<select>` stripped of both border and outline has *no* keyboard focus indicator whatsoever — the organiser tabbing through the wizard cannot see where they are. → _Replace `outline: 'none'` with `outlineColor: NEU.forest` on a `:focus-visible` rule, or swap in the typeahead above, which manages its own focus ring._  `[contrast]`

## Financials → Settings — manual payment form

- **`src/app/manage/[slug]/financials/settings/page.tsx:309`** — Both fields are labelled "· optional": "Payment page link · optional" (line 309) and "Payment instructions · optional" (line 326). But `savePaymentPage` rejects the form when both are blank (line 258) with "Add a payment link or written payment instructions so delegates have somewhere to pay." The labels are individually true and collectively a lie, and the organiser only discovers it after pressing COMPLETE SETUP. → _Label them "Payment page link" and "Payment instructions", and put one caption above the pair: "Fill in at least one — delegates need somewhere to pay." Then the constraint is stated before the failure, not after._  `[copy]`

## Financials → Settings — method rows

- **`src/app/manage/[slug]/financials/settings/page.tsx:69`** — `MethodRow` uses `PillToggle` for a mutually-exclusive choice: `onChange={() => { if (!active && !disabled) onActivate(); }}`. Clicking the ON toggle does nothing at all — no animation, no message, no state change. A switch that ignores you is the canonical "this UI is broken" experience. When Stripe is unsupported, the Manual row is simultaneously `active` and `disabled`, so it renders as a permanently-on switch you can never touch, with the explanation sitting in a separate 11.5px line above the card. → _Use a radio treatment: a filled forest dot on the active row, an empty ring on the other, and make the whole row clickable to switch. Put the "Stripe isn't available for {country}" explanation *inside* the disabled row where the user is trying to click._  `[polish]`

## Financials → Settings — wizard

- **`src/app/manage/[slug]/financials/settings/page.tsx:439`** — Steps 1, 3 and 4 render inside a `NeuCard`; step 2 does not — its "Step 2 of 3" label and helper text float bare on the page background above two loose cards (line 439). The card frame appears, disappears and reappears as the organiser advances. The step label is also plain 10px text with no progress indicator, while the app's other wizard (src/components/wizard.tsx:57) has animated gold progress dots. → _Wrap step 2 in the same NeuCard, and replace the text label with `WizardShell`'s progress dots — they already exist, are already themed, and would make the two wizards feel like one product._  `[consistency]`

## Financials → Settings — method cards

- **`src/app/manage/[slug]/financials/settings/page.tsx:447`** — The Stripe and Manual choice cards in step 2 are `NeuCard hover onClick` — clickable `<div>`s with no `role`, no `tabIndex`, no keyboard handler (neu.tsx:169-177), and no visible "choose" affordance: no chevron, no radio, no button. The organiser has to infer that a whole paragraph of body copy is a button, and a keyboard user cannot select a payment method at all. → _Add an explicit "CHOOSE" NeuButton or a right-aligned ChevronRight to each card, and give NeuCard a `role="button" tabIndex={0}` + Enter/Space path when `onClick` is set — that fixes this and every other clickable NeuCard in the product at once._  `[polish]`

## Financials → Settings — free-conference guidance

- **`src/app/manage/[slug]/financials/settings/page.tsx:499`** — The forest callout "Running a free conference?" appears twice with different wording — wizard step 3 (line 499: "Write 'This conference is free' in the instructions") and the steady-state Manual row (line 752: "Choose Manual and write 'This conference is free'"). Both are `#EED98A` on `#1B3828` at radius 10, the only dark inline callout in the app, and one of them tells the organiser to choose the method they have already chosen. → _Extract to one constant with the step-3 wording. Better still: add a "This conference is free" checkbox that writes the note for them — the current instruction asks the organiser to type a magic string._  `[consistency]`

## Financials → Settings — alert palette

- **`src/app/manage/[slug]/financials/settings/page.tsx:356`** — The readiness banner is `rgba(238,217,138,0.22)` background, `rgba(182,135,31,0.35)` border, `#6B4F12` text, `#8A6614` icon — a fourth distinct alert palette across the routes in this sweep, alongside the pay page's `rgba(184,132,74,0.1)`/#B8844A amber, the invite pages' `rgba(139,32,32,...)`/#8B2020 red, and error.tsx's `rgba(139,32,32,0.08)`/no-text-colour. → _Define `NEU_ALERT = { warn, error, success, info }` in neu.tsx with a bg/border/ink triple each, and convert these four call sites. Warnings about money should look the same everywhere in the product._  `[consistency]`

## Financials → Settings — page shell

- **`src/app/manage/[slug]/financials/settings/page.tsx:296`** — `if (!conference) return null;` — a blank content area with no skeleton or spinner while the manage layout resolves — and the page has no heading of its own. The organiser lands on "Financials → Settings" and the first thing on screen is either an amber warning banner or a dark gradient card. The sibling /manage/[slug]/financial-aid does have an eyebrow + h1 (financial-aid/page.tsx:19-22). → _Add the same header block (`NeuIconDisc` + "{ACRONYM} · FINANCIALS" eyebrow + h1 "Payment Settings"), and replace `return null` with a two-card skeleton._  `[hierarchy]`

## Financial Aid

- **`src/app/manage/[slug]/financial-aid/page.tsx:19`** — The eyebrow reads "{acronym} · Financial Aid" and the h1 immediately below reads "Financial Aid" — the same two words twice within a 40px stack. The eyebrow's job is to give context the heading doesn't. → _Eyebrow: "{acronym} · APPLICATIONS" or just the acronym; keep the h1. Compare the calendar page, which correctly pairs "Where You're Headed" with "Conference Calendar"._  `[copy]`
- **`src/app/manage/[slug]/financial-aid/page.tsx:34`** — The "Requests" section label uses exactly the same spec as the page-level eyebrow above it — 11px, weight 800, 0.14em, `NEU.deepGold`, uppercase. A section heading and a page breadcrumb are typographically identical, so the page reads as two equal-rank labels with content in between. It also carries no count, so the organiser cannot tell whether there are 0 or 40 requests without scrolling. → _Give the section a heading treatment (14px/800 ink with a small icon disc, like the Basic Information block at profile/page.tsx:702) and append a count chip: "Requests · 12"._  `[hierarchy]`
- **`src/app/manage/[slug]/financial-aid/page.tsx:11`** — `if (!conference) return null;` renders a completely blank page while the manage layout loads — no spinner, no skeleton, no shell. Combined with the identical `return null` at financials/settings:296, two manage sections flash empty on every cold navigation. → _Return the header block plus a pulsing skeleton for the form and the requests list. The header can render from the slug alone._  `[missing-state]`

## Financial Aid — header row

- **`src/app/manage/[slug]/financial-aid/page.tsx:15`** — `className="flex items-start justify-between gap-4 flex-wrap mb-5"` wraps a single child. The `justify-between` reserves the right-hand slot for an action that does not exist, so the header is visually lopsided against sibling manage pages that do put controls there. → _Either drop `justify-between`, or fill the slot with the actions this page actually wants — an "Export requests (CSV)" button and an aid-budget summary chip are the obvious candidates for a screen about allocating money._  `[polish]`

## Landing lab

- **`src/app/conferences/landing-lab/shared.tsx:160`** — `VariantSwitcher` is a permanently-fixed glass pill at `bottom: 20, right: 20, z-[90]` reading "LAB V1 V2 V3". The route is publicly reachable with no auth gate — robots.ts:27 disallows crawling, but a disallow is not a noindex and does not stop anyone who has the URL. An internal A/B lab with a debug switcher is shipped at a production URL. → _Gate the route on an env flag or a signed-in admin check, and add `export const metadata = { robots: { index: false, follow: false } }`. Label the buttons with the variants' real names — Stagefront / Record / First Gavel — since "V2" tells a reviewer nothing._  `[polish]`
- **`src/app/conferences/landing-lab/LandingLabClient.tsx:19`** — `conferences` and `reviews` both start as `[]` and the fetch has no loading flag, so all three variants first render their zero-conference layout and then reflow when the data lands. The conferences query has no `.limit()` either — it pulls every public conference row with banner and logo URLs for what is a landing page showing at most six. → _Add a `loading` state and render each variant's skeleton, and `.limit(24)` the query. The variants already slice to 3/5/6 client-side (VariantStagefront:186, VariantRecord:272, VariantStagefront:908)._  `[missing-state]`

## Landing lab / map / delegation portal — logo fallbacks

- **`src/app/conferences/landing-lab/VariantRecord.tsx:417`** — Four different monogram algorithms feed LogoDisc across the routes in this sweep: `acronym.slice(0, 2)` (VariantRecord:417, VariantFirstGavel:186, MapClient:803), `(acronym || full_name).slice(0, 3).toUpperCase()` (VariantStagefront:1016), `monogramFor(name)` (onboarding:368, accountUi:539), and `committeeAbbr()` — first letters, max 4 (DelegationPortalClient:122). The same conference therefore shows a different fallback monogram depending on which screen you are on. → _Route every LogoDisc fallback through the shared `monogramFor()` and delete the three local variants. The `.slice(0, 2)` calls are also unguarded against a null acronym and will throw._  `[consistency]`

## Pay & Financial Aid / papers / map — page structure

- **`src/app/conferences/[slug]/pay/page.tsx:467`** — None of the routes in this sweep wrap their content in a `<main>` landmark, and none provide a skip link — the pay page, the papers page, the map, the delegation portal and both invite pages all render `<SiteNav />` followed by a bare `<div>`. A keyboard or screen-reader user must tab through the entire nav on every one of these pages before reaching content. → _Add `<main id="content">` around the content div on each, and a single visually-hidden "Skip to content" link in the root layout that targets `#content`. One layout edit plus six one-word changes._  `[contrast]`

> **Method / limits:** METHOD — Source read only. No dev server, no browser tools, no edits, no git operations. I read AGENTS.md and src/components/neu.tsx in full first, then read every route the completeness critic listed as uncovered: src/app/error.tsx, src/app/global-error.tsx, all six loading.tsx, the three /invites/* pages, /delegation/[societyId]/DelegationPortalClient.tsx, /auth/{signup,error,forgot,reset,onboarding} plus authUi.tsx and src/components/wizard.tsx, /account/{layout,calendar,profile,unlimited,conferences}, /conferences/map/MapClient.tsx (all 1007 lines), /conferences/[slug]/pay/page.tsx (read in four passes covering the page shell, the shared primitives, ProofUploadModal, PaymentsPanel and the full PayInvoiceAndActions render), /conferences/[slug]/papers/[paperId]/page.tsx, /conferences/[slug]/role/[role], /conferences/landing-lab (page + LandingLabClient + VariantSwitcher in shared.tsx), /manage/[slug]/financials/settings/page.tsx and /manage/[slug]/financial-aid/page.tsx. I also read the PaymentMenu reference in the applications page to check the portal rule before filing anything against AssignPicker.

COVERAGE LIMITS — I did not open the three landing-lab variant bodies in full (VariantStagefront 1344 / VariantRecord 507 / VariantFirstGavel 363 lines); I grep-profiled them for vh sizing, transition:all, focus rings, native date inputs and monogram derivation, and filed only what that surfaced. /account/cv and /account/cv/CVTimeline.tsx were grep-profiled, not read line by line. The pay page's AddonsModal, AdvisorTicketsModal, AddSpotsPanel, GenericInvoiceCard and RemovePledgeAction internals were skimmed via their signatures and call sites rather than read whole — there is more in those five components than I filed.

RULE CHECKS THAT CAME BACK CLEAN — No `<input type="date">`, `type="time"` or `datetime-local` on any route in this sweep; /auth/signup correctly uses the shared DatePicker (signup:42). Every portaled layer I opened (DelegationPortalClient AssignPicker, profile's nationality typeahead, the pay page's RemovePledgeAction) follows the AGENTS.md portal pattern with edge-flipping, and closes on scroll exactly as the PaymentMenu reference does — so that is a match, not a deviation. I found no click-to-open informational "i" popups on these routes.

NON-UI OBSERVATIONS (per the brief, kept out of findings):
• src/app/delegation/[societyId]/DelegationPortalClient.tsx:544 — `delegation_assign_seat` failures roll back state and return with no error surfaced; a leader cannot tell an RLS rejection from a network blip. Also `ChevronLeft` is imported at :27 and never used.
• src/app/account/layout.tsx:359+365 — `{children}` mounts twice at every viewport. Beyond the UI cost I filed, this doubles every account page's Supabase traffic (CalendarPage issues 8 queries instead of 4) and duplicates DOM ids.
• src/app/conferences/[slug]/pay/page.tsx:1819 — `selectedTotalCents` sums `invoiceDueCents(inv)` across invoices whose `inv.currency` may differ, then formats the total with the single `currency` derived from `roleConfig` at :1627. A mixed-currency selection would display a wrong total and hand a wrong figure to Stripe checkout. Worth a correctness pass.
• src/app/conferences/[slug]/papers/[paperId]/page.tsx — the review thread has no realtime subscription; messages only refresh after the local user sends. A chair's reply is invisible to the delegate until reload.
• src/app/conferences/landing-lab/VariantRecord.tsx:417/487 and VariantFirstGavel.tsx:186 — `c.acronym.slice(0, 2)` is unguarded; a public conference with a null acronym throws and takes the whole variant down.
• src/app/conferences/map/MapClient.tsx:448 — `tooltipPos` and `hoveredDef` are computed and never consumed (this is the dead code behind the missing-hover finding). The 1700ms timeouts at :312 and :322 also fire regardless of whether cloud_transition.mp4 loaded, so a slow connection gets a blank 1.7s pause.
• src/app/auth/onboarding/page.tsx:191 — `NEU.base ?? '#EDE7D8'`; NEU.base is a non-nullable const, so the fallback is dead.
• src/app/account/layout.tsx:217-247 and :84 — the entire `link.highlight` nav branch is unreachable; no NAV_LINKS entry sets the flag.
• src/app/account/accountUi.tsx:16 — `export const MONO = OUTFIT`, so every "mono" figure in the account area (calendar counts, etc.) renders in the proportional brand face. shared.tsx:29 does the same with SANS.


---

# Gap sweep: thin-dimensions (404/not-found, loading & skeletons, error states, focus rings & keyboard, dialog semantics, landmarks/aria-live, RTL/Arabic, motion & reduced-motion, iconography, contrast, form-validation presentation, empty states, responsive/mobile, image & asset loading, print) — swept across the whole app, not one area  
_94 findings_


## Every route — 404

- **`src/app/not-found.tsx`** — The file does not exist and no page calls notFound() (verified: `grep -rn "notFound()" src/` returns nothing). Every mistyped or dead URL — a deleted conference slug, an expired /invites/... token path, an old /cv/[id] someone shared — falls through to Next.js's stock 404: white background, system Times/Arial, "This page could not be found", no nav, no logo, no link home. This is a product that exists to hand people links (6-char session codes, invite tokens, public CV URLs, conference slugs), so 404 is a routine state, not an edge case. → _Add src/app/not-found.tsx modelled on src/app/error.tsx: NEU.base page, NeuCard at maxWidth 460, a NeuIconDisc, "That link didn't lead anywhere", and three concrete exits — Go home, Join a session with a code field, Browse conferences. Also call notFound() from the conference/committee/CV loaders so a bad slug 404s instead of rendering an empty live shell._  `[missing-state]`

## Every route — crash boundary

- **`src/app/error.tsx:38`** — The crash card is `backgroundColor:'#FAF8F3', border:'1px solid #DDD4C0', borderRadius:20` — a flat bordered card. Nothing else in the current design system looks like that: NeuCard is NEU.surface #F0EBDD, borderRadius 22, boxShadow NEU.out, no border. So the one screen a user sees when something breaks is the one screen that looks like a different product. → _Rebuild the card with NEU.surface + borderRadius 22 + NEU.out, replace the ⚠️ emoji at :49 with `<NeuIconDisc gradient={NEU_GRADIENTS.amber} icon={AlertTriangle} size={56}/>`, and make TRY AGAIN a `<NeuButton>`. Keep global-error.tsx self-contained (correct call) but match its geometry to the same radius/padding so the two crash screens are siblings._  `[consistency]`
- **`src/app/error.tsx:53`** — Both crash boundaries are hardcoded English ("Something went wrong", "Gavelling hit a snag", "TRY AGAIN", "Go home") in an app that ships en/es/fr/ar. An Arabic delegate whose session crashes gets an English card in an LTR box. → _error.tsx runs under LanguageProvider — wire it to useLanguage() with four new keys. global-error.tsx cannot (providers may not have mounted), so read `document.documentElement.lang` and pick from a tiny inline 4-locale map, and set dir=rtl on its own <html> when lang is ar (it currently hardcodes `<html lang="en">` at :31)._  `[copy]`
- **`src/app/error.tsx:90`** — The crash reference code renders `color:'#B6A88E'` on `#FAF8F3` at fontSize 10.5 — a measured 2.20:1. That is the one string on the screen a user may need to read aloud or type into a support message, and it is the least legible thing on the page. → _Take it to NEU.ink at 60% or #6A5A4A (5.4:1 on #FAF8F3), keep the mono face, and bump to 12px. Add a click-to-copy on it — a reference code you cannot select cleanly is a reference code nobody reports._  `[contrast]`

## All six route-level loading.tsx

- **`src/app/conferences/[slug]/loading.tsx:4`** — All six loading.tsx files (my-conferences, account, conferences/explore, conferences/[slug], .../apply, .../pay) are byte-identical 28px two-colour spinners. Three of them front pages that already render their own skeleton, so one navigation shows the user three distinct states in a row: route spinner → in-page skeleton → content. The skeleton primitive that exists (src/components/Skeleton.tsx) is used by exactly two files in the entire app. → _Replace each loading.tsx with a SkeletonBlock composition that traces the real layout it is about to reveal (SectionCardSkeleton already exists for exactly this). One state instead of three, and the page stops jumping when content lands._  `[missing-state]`

## Account tabs — loading

- **`src/app/account/loading.tsx:7`** — This spinner is centred in `minHeight:'60vh'` while the other five use `min-h-screen`. Switching between /account/profile, /account/cv and /account/unlimited therefore makes the spinner jump vertically between tabs, and it sits noticeably high compared to every other route spinner in the app. → _Either give all six the same centring box, or (better) replace this one with a skeleton of the account content column so nothing moves at all between loading and loaded._  `[consistency]`

## App-wide — skeletons

- **`src/app/globals.css:248`** — Four parallel shimmer implementations coexist: `.neu-shimmer` here (reduced-motion guarded, 2 consumers), Tailwind `animate-pulse` (~35 sites, unguarded), `@keyframes pulse-skeleton` in MapClient.tsx:477 whose own comment says "Keyframes, unconditional", and `@keyframes cal-shimmer` in PersonalConferenceCard.tsx:400. They pulse at 1.8s, 2s, 2s and 1.4s respectively, so two loading blocks on adjacent cards visibly beat against each other. → _Delete the three local keyframes and route everything through SkeletonBlock/.neu-shimmer. That also inherits the prefers-reduced-motion guard the other three lack._  `[consistency]`
- **`src/app/manage/[slug]/financials/invoices/page.tsx:907`** — Skeleton fills use seven different greys across the app: rgba(154,138,120,0.16) (Skeleton.tsx:26), #E4DCCB and #EDE7D8 and #F0EBDD (ConferencesRolesClient:1161-1175), #DDD4C0 (ConferencesExploreClient:1087), #F0EDE6 (here), and NEU.surface (manage/[slug]/page.tsx:912). Loading blocks on sibling screens read as different materials. → _One token: `NEU.skeleton = 'rgba(154,138,120,0.16)'` for light surfaces, the existing `tone='dark'` value for forest cards. Everything else deleted._  `[consistency]`

## Tutorial overlay

- **`src/components/TutorialOverlay.tsx:653`** — `animation: 'tutorial-pulse 1.6s ease-in-out infinite'` runs an unbounded pulsing ring around the spotlit element for the entire duration of the tutorial, with no prefers-reduced-motion guard. A continuously scaling ring in the user's focal point is a textbook vestibular trigger, and this is the first thing a new chair sees. → _Wrap the keyframe injection at :372 in `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference)`, and fall back to a static 2px NEU.gold ring. Better still: add one global block to globals.css that neutralises every `animation`/`transition` under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` — the app currently has exactly two guards for ~30 keyframes._  `[missing-state]`

## Session — gavel chip

- **`src/components/GavelChip.tsx:224`** — `animation: 'gavelchip-ring 1.2s cubic-bezier(0.22,1,0.36,1) 2'` fires a double scale-and-fade ring whenever the gavel changes hands, unguarded by prefers-reduced-motion, and the chip is fixed at the top of the chair's screen so it is unavoidable. → _Gate the keyframe on prefers-reduced-motion and substitute a 400ms background-colour flash, which conveys the same "this just changed" without motion._  `[missing-state]`

## /conferences/map

- **`src/app/conferences/map/MapClient.tsx:477`** — The comment on the <style> block literally reads "Keyframes, unconditional". Combined with the rAF cursor-trail canvas (:366) that repaints on every mousemove for the life of the page, the map is the most motion-heavy screen in the product and has zero reduced-motion handling and no way to turn the trail off. → _Guard pulse-skeleton behind `prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference`, and in the rAF effect early-return when `window.matchMedia('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)').matches` so the trail never starts. The map still works perfectly without it._  `[missing-state]`
- **`src/app/conferences/map/MapClient.tsx:516`** — `<img src="/map/world_map.png">` is 5.8MB and has no placeholder — the page shows a bare #1B3828 field until it lands. `<video src="/map/cloud_transition.mp4">` (5.2MB) is mounted eagerly at :563 with no `preload="none"` and no poster, so it downloads on page load even though it only plays on a continent transition. And each continent PNG (4.8–6.4MB; asia_map.png is 6.4MB) is only requested at click time, so clicking a continent plays a 200ms cloud wipe and then sits on empty forest for seconds — the `transition: opacity 400ms` at :594 starts fading in an image that has not decoded. Worst case this page is ~25MB. → _Convert the seven map PNGs to WebP/AVIF (likely 10× smaller), add `preload="none"` to the video, add a poster frame, and prefetch a continent's PNG on hover so the cloud wipe covers the download instead of preceding it. Show a SkeletonBlock or a low-res blur of the world map during the first paint._  `[missing-state]`
- **`src/app/conferences/map/MapClient.tsx:533`** — The six continents are `<polygon>` elements with `onMouseEnter`/`onClick` and nothing else — no tabIndex, no role, no keyboard handler, no <title>. The entire navigation of this page is mouse-only and completely invisible to a screen reader. Worse, `hoveredDef` and `tooltipPos` are computed at :447-448 and never rendered anywhere, so hovering a continent produces no visual feedback at all beyond the cursor trail — the user cannot tell what they are about to click. → _Render the tooltip that is already being computed (continent name + active-conference count at tooltipPos), and give each polygon `role="button"`, `tabIndex={0}`, an `onKeyDown` for Enter/Space and a `<title>` child. Add a visible focus outline via `stroke` on `:focus-visible`. Also add a plain list of continents below the fold as the non-visual route._  `[missing-state]`
- **`src/app/conferences/map/MapClient.tsx:454`** — The root is `width: '100vw'`. On desktop browsers that reserve a classic scrollbar, 100vw exceeds the content box and produces horizontal overflow — caught only by globals.css:71's `overflow-x: clip` backstop, which its own comment says is not meant to be load-bearing. → _`width: '100%'` on a `position:fixed; inset:0` root, which is what this layout actually wants._  `[responsive]`

## App-wide — secondary text

- **`src/components/neu.tsx:33`** — NEU.muted #9A8A78 measures 2.81:1 on NEU.surface #F0EBDD and 2.71:1 on NEU.base #EDE7D8 — WCAG AA for body text needs 4.5:1. It is the app's universal secondary colour: 494 `NEU.muted` references plus 915 hardcoded `#9A8A78` literals, and it is almost always applied at 10–13px (e.g. NeuStatTile labels at fontSize 11, neu.tsx:370). Every stat label, every helper line, every timestamp in the product is below AA. → _Darken the token to roughly #6F6255 (≈4.6:1 on both surfaces) and keep #9A8A78 only for genuinely decorative rules and disabled glyphs. Then sweep the 915 hardcoded literals onto the token so one edit fixes the whole app — that sweep is the only reason this is a one-line fix rather than a thousand._  `[contrast]`

## App-wide — gold accents

- **`src/components/neu.tsx:30`** — NEU.deepGold #B6871F is 2.72:1 on NEU.surface and NEU.amber #B8844A is 2.74:1 — both fail AA badly, and both are used for *text*, not just borders: the chair badge in chat (ChatMessageGroup.tsx:41 uses NEU.deepGold on the ivory thread), warning copy, "Take the gavel" affordances. NEU.green #3D7A52 is 4.30:1, marginally under. → _Add darkened text-safe variants — deepGoldText ≈ #8A6412 (4.6:1), amberText ≈ #8A5A2E (already exists as the amber gradient's dark stop, 5.1:1), greenText ≈ #356846 — and reserve the current three for fills, gradients and rules where contrast rules don't apply._  `[contrast]`

## Session sidebar — committee masthead

- **`src/components/CommitteeIdentityBadge.tsx:140`** — The full committee name renders `rgba(237,231,216,0.52)` on #1B3828 at fontSize 10.5 — 3.99:1 — and the topic line at :155 is `rgba(238,217,138,0.55)` — 3.88:1. Both fail AA for text this small, and both are then multiplied down again by FitToScreen's scale (0.94 on a 1366×768 laptop → an effective 9.8px). → _Raise the alphas to 0.72 (the value already used for topicLabel at :160, which measures 5.51:1) and lift both sizes to 11.5px. The visual hierarchy is preserved by weight (500 vs 900) and by the gold-vs-ivory hue split, not by opacity._  `[contrast]`

## /conferences/map — nav

- **`src/app/conferences/map/MapClient.tsx:481`** — `.map-nav-wrapper a, .map-nav-wrapper button { color: #EDE7D8 !important; }` forces every button inside SiteNav to ivory. The language dropdown (SiteNav.tsx:242) is rendered inline, NOT through Portal, so it is a descendant of .map-nav-wrapper — its four buttons become #EDE7D8 on the menu's own #FAF8F3 background. Measured contrast 1.16:1. The language menu is effectively invisible on this page. ProfileDropdown escapes because it IS portaled (ProfileDropdown.tsx:224). → _Portal the language dropdown the same way ProfileDropdown is (this is also the AGENTS.md popover rule), and scope the !important override to `.map-nav-wrapper > * a, .map-nav-wrapper > * button` or better to explicit nav-link classes so it can never leak into a floating layer again._  `[contrast]`

## App-wide — keyboard focus

- **`src/components/neu.tsx:534`** — `focus:outline-none` appears 843 times across src/ against 19 `focus-visible` occurrences, and most of those 19 are inside shadcn ui/ primitives that nothing imports. globals.css's `@layer base { * { @apply border-border outline-ring/50 } }` sets only outline-COLOR — no width, no style — so there is no default ring to fall back on. Result: keyboard focus is invisible in essentially the entire product, including NeuPill (:534) and NeuButton (:578), the two shared primitives every screen is built from. → _Define one token — `--neu-ring: 0 0 0 2px #EDE7D8, 0 0 0 4px #1B3828` — expose it as a `.neu-focus` utility applying it on `:focus-visible` only, and replace every `focus:outline-none` with `focus:outline-none neu-focus`. Doing it in neu.tsx's NeuButton/NeuPill/NeuCard alone covers a large fraction of the 843 for free._  `[missing-state]`

## Session — gavel chip explainer

- **`src/components/GavelChip.tsx:296`** — The "i" hint badge is explicitly given `tabIndex={0}` and `onFocus/onBlur` handlers so keyboard users can reach it — and then `focus:outline-none` with no replacement means they cannot see that they have. A control deliberately made keyboard-reachable with an invisible focus state is worse than one that is not reachable at all. → _Give this specific badge a visible focus ring (`:focus-visible { box-shadow: 0 0 0 2px ${NEU.forest} }`). The hover behaviour itself correctly follows the AGENTS.md 'i' rule — only the focus affordance is missing._  `[missing-state]`

## App-wide — modals

- **`src/components/CommitteeEditorModal.tsx:100`** — ModalOverlay is the shared modal shell for most of the conferences side, and it provides none of the four things a modal needs: no role="dialog"/aria-modal, no Escape handler, no focus trap or initial-focus, no restore-focus-on-close, and no body scroll lock (the page scrolls behind the dim layer). Each caller has to reinvent it, and most don't: CommitteeEditorModal, CVEntryModal (1374 lines), LogoCropModal, CreditsWelcomeModal, EmailComposer and LiveModals all have zero 'Escape' occurrences and zero dialog roles. → _Fix it once in ModalOverlay: add role/aria-modal/aria-labelledby props, an Escape listener, `document.body.style.overflow='hidden'` on mount, focus the first focusable child (or the card itself) on open, and restore `document.activeElement` on close. Every consumer inherits correct behaviour with no call-site changes._  `[missing-state]`

## Manage — confirm dialogs

- **`src/components/ConfirmModal.tsx:65`** — `if (e.key === 'Enter') { ...onConfirm() }` is bound to `window` for every variant including `danger`. A user who has just pressed Enter to submit the form behind the modal, or who taps Enter while toggling the optional checkbox at :98, irreversibly confirms a destructive action. There is also no initial focus, so pressing Tab moves focus into the page *behind* the dialog. → _Drop the global Enter binding for `danger={true}` (keep Escape), focus the Cancel button on mount for danger and the Confirm button otherwise, and trap Tab inside the card. Both buttons also carry `focus:outline-none` at :120/:131 with no replacement — add the ring._  `[polish]`

## App-wide — modal scrims

- **`src/components/CommitteeEditorModal.tsx:109`** — Nine different backdrop treatments ship: rgba(0,0,0,0.4) ×12 plus bg-black/40 ×2 (pure black, here), rgba(28,20,16,0.45) ×4, rgba(28,20,16,0.5), rgba(28,20,16,0.42), rgba(27,20,16,0.42) (LiveModals.tsx:125 — a one-digit typo of the ink token), rgba(20,14,10,0.55) (ShareAchievementModal.tsx:188), rgba(5,4,3,0.92) (voting/[code]/page.tsx:114), bg-black/10 (ui/dialog.tsx:42). Opening two modals in one session shows two different-temperature dimmings. → _Export `NEU.scrim = 'rgba(28,20,16,0.45)'` and `NEU.scrimHeavy = 'rgba(28,20,16,0.75)'` (for the voting takeover, which legitimately needs to kill the room) and replace all nine. Pure black is wrong in a warm ivory system — it turns the page grey rather than dim._  `[consistency]`

## App-wide — page structure

- **`src/app/layout.tsx:141`** — Zero skip links exist in src/, and there are 10 `<main>` elements against 117 `<h1>`s — so on most routes a keyboard or screen-reader user has no way to jump past the SiteNav (which contains 6+ nav links, a language menu, a credit chip and a profile menu) to the content, and no landmark to jump to even if they wanted. → _Add a visually-hidden-until-focused skip link as the first child of <body> targeting #main, and wrap the content of each top-level client shell in `<main id="main">`. Cheapest accessibility win available in this codebase._  `[missing-state]`

## App-wide — live regions

- **`src/components/Toast.tsx:109`** — The whole product is built on optimistic updates that change state with no page navigation — a delegate added to the GSL, a motion accepted, a document approved, a vote result — and there are exactly 4 aria-live/role=status regions in src/ (Toast, authUi, ui/spinner, ui/field). A screen-reader user chairing a committee is told nothing when anything happens. → _Add one polite live region per session surface, fed by the same strings the UI already shows: current speaker changed, phase changed, motion outcome. Toast already does this correctly — it just isn't mounted anywhere except manage/[slug]/assignment._  `[missing-state]`

## App-wide — notifications

- **`src/components/Toast.tsx:52`** — Four notification systems ship simultaneously with four durations: this Toast (2200ms, matte-glass, portaled, top-right) mounted in exactly ONE file (manage/[slug]/assignment/page.tsx:4419); authUi.tsx:563 useToast (3500ms, its own auth-toast-in keyframe); an inline layout-shifting flash banner (manage/[slug]/committees/page.tsx:611-632, 4500ms) — the very pattern Toast.tsx's header comment says it replaced; and native alert() (blocking). The same class of confirmation looks and behaves differently on adjacent screens. → _Mount <ToastHost/> in the root layout and migrate the flash banner and useToast onto `toast(kind, msg)`. Delete the two rivals._  `[consistency]`
- **`src/components/Toast.tsx:23`** — `DISMISS_MS = 2200` applies to `kind:'err'` identically to `kind:'ok'`. An error the user must act on ("Could not transfer this spot.") vanishes in 2.2 seconds, often before they have finished reading it — and there is no history, so it is simply gone. → _Keep 2200ms for 'ok'; make 'err' persist until dismissed (the X button already exists) or at minimum 8s. Errors are the one thing that should outlast the user's attention span, not the other way round._  `[missing-state]`
- **`src/components/Toast.tsx:56`** — `if (last && last.kind === kind && last.msg === msg) return;` silently swallows a repeat of the same message. Copy a code, copy it again — no toast the second time, so the second action looks like it failed. Deduplication is right for a double-fire, wrong for a deliberate repeat. → _Instead of suppressing, restart the existing toast's dismiss timer and pulse it (a 120ms scale to 1.02). The user gets confirmation without a stacked duplicate._  `[missing-state]`
- **`src/components/Toast.tsx:163`** — ToastHost is pinned `top: 20, right: 20`, the card enters with `translateX(18px)` and carries `borderLeft: 3px solid ${accent}`. All three are physical. Under dir="rtl" the toast should originate from the top-left and its accent bar should sit on the inline-start edge; instead it slides in from the wrong side and the accent rule lands on the trailing edge, away from the icon it belongs to. → _`insetInlineEnd: 20`, `borderInlineStart`, and drive the enter transform off `document.dir` (or use a `rtl:translate-x-[-18px]` variant). This is a 3-line fix in a shared component that fixes every future toast._  `[rule-violation]`

Please try again.')` — a blocking OS dialog in Chrome's system font, on the create flow, in a page that otherwise uses useLanguage() for every other string. Eight more native holdouts exist: manage/[slug]/settings/page.tsx:1364, :1425, :2609 (alert), manage/[slug]/committees/page.tsx:982 (window.confirm) and :994 (window.alert), account/cv/page.tsx:95, :98 and ShareAchievementModal.tsx:119, :124 (window.prompt). → _Route all of them through the existing ConfirmModal (already used 43 times) or Toast. committees/page.tsx is the sharpest case — it uses the styled `await confirm({...})` at :824, :866 and :1066 and then a raw window.confirm at :982 for a destructive 'Remove from dais', in the same file._  `[consistency]`

## App-wide — copy to clipboard

- **`src/app/delegate/[code]/page.tsx:1149`** — The delegate's session-code button calls `navigator.clipboard.writeText(committee.code)` and does nothing else — no label swap, no toast, no state. The user taps it and the screen is identical, so the only sensible conclusion is that it didn't work. grain-dev/page.tsx:192 has the same problem. → _Mirror the chair page's copied-state, or better, fire the shared Toast. Across the app there are 13 clipboard call sites and five behaviours: ✓-swap at 2000ms (chair:2680, SettingsPanel:23/:43, ChairParticipant:112, AllocationCard:141, committees:938), 1600ms (live/page.tsx:46), 2200ms (account/cv:92), toast (manage/[slug]/page.tsx:137), and nothing (here). Pick one._  `[missing-state]`

## Session header — copy code

- **`src/app/chair/[code]/page.tsx:2682`** — `{copied ? '✓' : committee.code}` replaces the six-character session code with a bare checkmark for two seconds. The chair is usually copying the code *because they are about to read it out to the room* — and for those two seconds it is not on screen. The button also shrinks from ~7ch to 1ch, so the header jumps. → _Keep the code visible and add the tick beside it (`{code} ✓`) with a fixed min-width, or flash the button's background NEU.green for 600ms and leave the label alone._  `[polish]`

## Manage — live page copy

- **`src/app/manage/[slug]/live/page.tsx:46`** — `navigator.clipboard.writeText(value).catch(() => {}); setCopied(true);` — the failure is swallowed and the success state is set unconditionally, so on an insecure origin or a denied clipboard permission the user is shown "Copied" for something that was never copied. Only account/cv/page.tsx:93 does this correctly (`if (navigator.clipboard?.writeText)` with a window.prompt fallback). → _Adopt the account/cv pattern everywhere: feature-detect, set `copied` only in `.then()`, and on rejection surface a selectable text field rather than a lie._  `[missing-state]`

## App-wide — error copy

- **`src/app/invites/chair/[token]/page.tsx:81`** — `setError(rpcErr.message || 'Could not load this invite.')` puts a raw Supabase/Postgres string in front of the user — things like `JWT expired` or `permission denied for function get_chair_invite`. Eighteen sites do this: auth/signin:51, auth/signup:199, auth/reset:65, manage/communications:1337 and :1751, conferences/new:519, invites/chair:81 and :97, invites/delegation:71, invites/organizer:88 and :104, CommitteeEditorModal:499, CVEntryModal:865, plus the apply flow's `err.message` passthroughs. None of them say what to do next. → _Add a small `friendlyError(err)` mapper that translates the handful of codes that actually reach users (expired JWT → "Your sign-in expired — sign in again", unique violation → "That already exists", network → "Check your connection and retry") and falls back to a written sentence plus a Retry control. Keep the raw string behind the existing digest/reference pattern for support._  `[copy]`

## Chair invite

- **`src/app/invites/chair/[token]/page.tsx:130`** — The `failed` branch renders the heading "Invite not found" whenever `!invite || !invite.ok` — which is also what a transient network failure or an expired session produces. - **`src/app/invites/chair/[token]/page.tsx:181`** — The Decline button's disabled treatment is `boxShadow: responding !== null ? 'none' : NEU.outSm` — the shadow is removed but the ink colour, background and label stay at full strength, so a pending Decline reads as a broken flat rectangle rather than a temporarily unavailable control. Accept and Decline are also both `flex:1` with equal weight, giving a one-way action the same prominence as the reversible one. → _On disabled, drop colour to NEU.muted and set opacity 0.55 alongside removing the shadow. And demote Decline to a text button so ACCEPT is unambiguously the primary._  `[polish]`

## Delegate — Stats tab

- **`src/app/delegate/[code]/page.tsx:484`** — StatisticsTab has no loading state. Before the committee's messages/ledger resolve it renders a 60px `0`, `0` speeches, `0:00` total time and `—` rank — which is indistinguishable from a real, bad result. A delegate who opens Stats early is told they have zero points. `getDelegateFeedback` at :443 likewise has no loading or error branch, so a failed fetch is identical to "no recap yet". → _Gate the tab on a `ready` flag and render SkeletonBlock rows for the score, the three stat tiles and the leaderboard until the first fetch resolves. ## App-wide — RTL

- **`src/app/layout.tsx:98`** — `<html lang="en" className="h-full">` is hardcoded, and direction is only applied client-side by LanguageContext.tsx:32-33 in a useEffect. So every page an Arabic user loads paints once in LTR English-metrics layout and then flips to RTL after hydration — a full-page mirror flash on every navigation. It also means the server-rendered HTML always claims lang=en, which is wrong for SEO and for screen readers that pick a voice from it. → _Read the persisted locale in a Server Component (cookie rather than localStorage — that is the change that unlocks it) and set lang/dir on <html> at render time. Short of that, write the dir attribute in a tiny blocking inline script in <head> before first paint._  `[rule-violation]`
- **`src/components/SiteNav.tsx:445`** — The RTL migration is half-done and it shows most in the nav. Physical properties still in use across src/: 65 `left-*` and 67 `right-*` Tailwind position utilities (against ~0 `start-*`/`end-*`), 52 `text-left` + 11 `text-right` (against 24 `text-start` + 18 `text-end`), 48 `ml-/mr-/pl-/pr-` (against 84 `ms-/me-/ps-/pe-`), plus inline `marginLeft` ×29, `paddingLeft` ×48, `borderLeft` ×38 against `insetInlineStart` ×7 and `borderInline` ×1. Only three `rtl:` variants exist in the whole codebase. In this file alone: `borderLeft: '3px solid #B6871F'` marks the active mobile nav item on the wrong edge in Arabic (:445), `marginLeft:'auto'` (:511), `text-left` on the language menu items (:254), and `ml-1` on the selected-language tick (:268). → _Run one mechanical pass: `ml-→ms-`, `mr-→me-`, `pl-→ps-`, `pr-→pe-`, `text-left→text-start`, `text-right→text-end`, `left-N→start-N`, `right-N→end-N`, and inline `marginLeft→marginInlineStart`, `borderLeft→borderInlineStart`, `left:/right:→insetInlineStart:/insetInlineEnd:`. Then keep physical only where the thing genuinely is physical (FitToScreen.tsx:66's `left:0`, which is correctly commented as deliberate)._  `[rule-violation]`

## Session — back buttons, Arabic

- **`src/lib/translations.ts:2887`** — U+2190/U+2192 are not Bidi_Mirrored (verified with unicodedata.mirrored — both return 0), so an arrow baked into a string keeps its literal direction. The Arabic locale correctly flipped forward CTAs to `←` (e.g. :2702 'التالي ←') but ALSO left the back buttons as `←`: motions_back (:2887), documents_back (:2947), documents_back_to_documents (:2948), voting_back_docs (:3002), voting_back_to_session (:3010). In Arabic, Next and Back therefore both point the same way. → _Flip the five Arabic back strings to `→`. Better long-term: strip arrows out of translation strings entirely and render `<ArrowRight className="rtl:rotate-180"/>` beside the label — create/page.tsx:634 already does exactly this and is the only place that gets it right._  `[rule-violation]`

## Voting page — tally bars, Arabic

- **`src/lib/translations.ts:3042`** — `voting_against_bar: '← {n} معارِض'` and `voting_for_bar: '{n} مؤيِّد ←'`. In English the two arrows point outward in opposite directions — that opposition IS the tug-of-war metaphor of the For/Against bar. In Arabic both are `←`, so under RTL reordering the Against arrow ends up on the visual right pointing inward and the For arrow on the visual left pointing outward. Both bars appear to push the same way. → _Set voting_against_bar to `'{n} معارِض →'` (arrow at the logical end, which reorders to the visual left, pointing away). Or drop the glyphs and render two lucide arrows that are mirrored by a `rtl:rotate-180` class._  `[rule-violation]`

## Session — gavel chip, Arabic

- **`src/components/GavelChip.tsx:216`** — `<div className="fixed z-50" style={{ top: '3.75rem', right: '0.85rem' }}>` pins the chip to the physical right. Under dir=rtl the session header's flex children mirror to the right, so the chip lands on top of the content that just moved there. TutorialOverlay has the same problem three times over — the Gavin mascot at `right: -20` (:527 and :679) and a control at `top: 52, right: 16` (:734). → _`insetInlineEnd: 14` (and drop the rem/px mixing — everything else in this scaled tree is px). Same change for the three TutorialOverlay layers._  `[rule-violation]`

## Delegate — header tabs, Arabic

- **`src/app/delegate/[code]/page.tsx:1141`** — The chat unread badge is `absolute top-1 right-1`, and the active-tab underline at :1145 is `left:'12px', right:'12px'` with `transformOrigin:'left'`. Under RTL the badge stays on the physical right of a mirrored tab and the underline wipes in from the wrong edge — a small animation that visibly runs backwards. → _`top-1 end-1` for the badge; `insetInline: 12` and `transformOrigin: 'inline-start'` (or a `rtl:origin-right` variant) for the underline._  `[rule-violation]`

## Join / Settings — copy that names a position

- **`src/lib/translations.ts:93`** — `join_chair_role_co_note` says the gavel chip is "at the top-right of the session", and `settings_head_chair_note` (:574) repeats it. Because GavelChip is pinned with a physical `right`, the Arabic text happens to be correct today — but it is correct by accident, and it will be wrong the moment the chip is fixed to use insetInlineEnd. → _Stop naming a position. Rewrite as "…later, from the gavel chip in the session toolbar" in all four locales. Positional copy is the one kind of copy an RTL build can never keep true._  `[copy]`

- **`src/components/Emoji.tsx:54`** — Five parallel icon systems ship: lucide-react (97 files), this Twemoji SVG wrapper (8 files, the session cockpit), Emoji3D/Fluent 3D PNGs (86 references, the organiser dashboard), raw Unicode glyphs used as controls (✓ ×96, ✗ ×35, ✕ ×29, → ×476, ← ×118), and hand-inlined <svg> paths (ChatDisabledNotice:7, GavelChip:258, SettingsPanel:193). The glyph controls are the priority: ✕ and ✗ have inconsistent optical weight per platform and never sit on the same baseline as the label beside them._  `[consistency]`

## App-wide — icon delivery

- **`src/lib/countries.ts:33`** — Every country flag in the product — roll call, GSL, leaderboard, voting page, delegate cards — is a runtime request to `cdn.jsdelivr.net/gh/twitter/twemoji@14.0.2`. MUN conferences run on locked-down school and venue wifi where CDNs are routinely blocked; on such a network every flag and icon in the cockpit is simply absent. → _Self-host the ~200 flag SVGs used by UN_COUNTRIES from /public — it is a few hundred KB total and removes a hard third-party dependency from the one screen that must work in the room. ## Chair session on a phone

- **`src/components/MobileGate.tsx:81`** — "(or just rotate your phone, we're not judging)" — detection is user-agent based (:13), not size based, so rotating the phone changes absolutely nothing. That is the job this screen is failing to do._  `[copy]`
- **`src/components/MobileGate.tsx:65`** — "OPEN ON DESKTOP" is a `<div>` styled exactly like the app's primary CTA — forest #1B3828 fill, ivory text, 10px/20px padding, letter-spaced uppercase — but it is inert. Every user will tap it. It also uses `borderRadius: 8`, a value that appears nowhere else in the design system (pills are 999, cards 16–22), and the whole component sets `fontFamily: 'system-ui, sans-serif'` at :30 while its heading uses Playfair, so nothing here is in the house Outfit face. → _Make it a real control (copy the session URL, or a mailto/share sheet) or restyle it as an obviously-inert badge — NEU.base fill, NEU.inSm, NEU.muted text. ## Delegate session on a phone

- **`src/app/delegate/[code]/page.tsx:1116`** — MobileGate wraps only /chair (chair/[code]/page.tsx:2618). The delegate cockpit — by far the surface most likely to be opened on a phone — is wrapped in FitToScreen instead, which computes `scale = window.innerHeight / 820`. On a 375×812 phone that is scale 0.99, giving a layout width of ~379px for a cockpit designed at ~1456px. The file has 1613 lines and 2 responsive breakpoint utilities in total (`grep -coE "md:|sm:|lg:"` = 2). → _Either give the delegate view a real narrow-viewport layout (single column, stacked floor card, tabs as a bottom bar) below ~700px, or opt it out of FitToScreen on touch devices so normal responsive CSS can do its job. This is the single largest responsive gap in the product._  `[responsive]`

## Session pages — breakpoints inside FitToScreen

- **`src/app/delegate/[code]/page.tsx:1136`** — `text-sm sm:text-[18px] px-1.5 sm:px-3` — `sm:` is a media query against the REAL viewport, but the element lives inside a transform-scaled fit-root whose layout width is `window.innerWidth / scale`. The two are unrelated: on a 640px-wide phone `sm:` fires while the fit-root is ~646px, and on a 1200×900 desktop `sm:` fires while the fit-root is ~1092px. Breakpoints in this tree are measuring the wrong box, so they either never fire when needed or fire when irrelevant. → _Inside FitToScreen use container queries (`@container`) on the fit-root, or size purely off the fit-root's own computed width. As written these utilities give a false sense that the cockpit is responsive._  `[responsive]`

## Site nav — mobile menu

- **`src/components/SiteNav.tsx:420`** — `maxHeight: menuOpen ? '480px' : '0px'` with `overflow-hidden`. The menu contains navLinks (each ~41px: 12px padding + 13px text), an 8px-margin divider, a 4-button language row and the auth actions. Once the link set grows past ~8 items the last entries are silently clipped with no scroll and no indication anything is missing. The `duration-300` also animates 0→480 regardless of real content height, so a short menu finishes its reveal early and then sits still for the remainder. → _Animate a measured height (ResizeObserver or a grid-template-rows 0fr→1fr transition, which handles auto height natively) and add `overflow-y:auto` with a max-height derived from the viewport._  `[responsive]`

## Site nav — mobile

- **`src/components/SiteNav.tsx:436`** — Mobile nav links are `padding: '12px 16px'` at 13px → ~37px tall, and the four language buttons at :462 are `py-2.5` at 14px → ~34px tall. Both are under the 44×44 minimum for reliable thumb targets, and the language buttons sit in a tight 4-across row where a mis-tap silently changes the whole app's language. → _Take nav links to `padding: '14px 16px'` (min-height 44) and the language buttons to `py-3` with a `min-height: 44px`. Add `aria-pressed` to the language buttons — right now the only selected-state cue is a colour swap with no semantic marker._  `[polish]`

## Manage — import review tables

- **`src/app/manage/[slug]/import/page.tsx:908`** — RowTable and ResultTable scroll a 9-column body inside `maxHeight: 520, overflowY: 'auto'` but the `<thead>` is not sticky. On a 300-row roster import — the exact scenario this screen exists for — the organiser scrolls past row 12 and can no longer tell which column is Payment, which is Committee/Country and which is Notes. The horizontal wrapper is correct (`overflow-x-auto`); only the header pinning is missing. → _`position: sticky; top: 0; z-index: 1` on the `<tr>` inside `<thead>`, with its existing `#F0EDE6` background kept opaque so rows pass underneath cleanly. Same for the third table at :1111._  `[polish]`

## Manage / roster editors — hover-only controls

- **`src/components/ConferenceRosterPicker.tsx:769`** — Fourteen `opacity-0 group-hover:opacity-100` affordances exist, and several are the ONLY way to perform an action: rename a roster entry (:769), remove one (:773), remove a delegate on create/page.tsx:839, remove a GSL speaker on chair/[code]/page.tsx:435, and the two committee-card controls at manage/[slug]/committees/page.tsx:1477 and :1691. On a touch device there is no hover, so these functions do not exist. None of them add `focus-within:opacity-100` either, so a keyboard user tabs onto an invisible focused button. → _Add `focus-within:opacity-100` to every one (one-word fix, restores keyboard access), and on coarse pointers reveal them permanently: `@media (pointer: coarse) { opacity: 1 }`. Organisers do use iPads._  `[polish]`

## Session — GSL reorder

- **`src/components/RollCallPanel.tsx:637`** — Two drag idioms ship. The manage side shows an explicit `<GripVertical size={13}>` handle (assignment/page.tsx:4056, delegationShared.tsx:549, QuestionBuilder.tsx:403), while the session side — the GSL queue here and the speaker cards at chair/[code]/page.tsx:336 and :421 — has `cursor-grab` and nothing else, so reordering the speakers list is discoverable only by hovering and noticing the cursor. There is also no `cursor: 'grabbing'` anywhere in src/, so the cursor never changes during the drag itself. → _Add a GripVertical handle to the session drag rows to match, and set `cursor:'grabbing'` on the dragging element. Also add a keyboard path (focus a row, Alt+↑/↓ to move) — HTML5 draggable is mouse-only and reordering the queue is a core chair action._  `[consistency]`

## Session — motion/GSL reorder

- **`src/components/SettingsPanel.tsx:1`** — 25 `draggable` usages across the app (motion priority ordering in SettingsPanel, GSL reorder, assignment allocation, question builder, apply-flow preference ranking at ConferenceApplyClient.tsx:585) and not one has a keyboard alternative or an aria-describedby explaining the interaction. Ranking committee preferences during an application — a required step — is entirely mouse-dependent. → _Give every sortable list a pair of ↑/↓ buttons alongside the drag handle. They are trivially small, they double as the touch affordance, and they make the whole feature keyboard-operable._  `[missing-state]`

## App-wide — form labelling
