- **`src/app/conferences/[slug]/apply/ConferenceApplyClient.tsx:2293`** — 21 `htmlFor` attributes exist against 247 `<input>/<textarea>/<select>` elements, 9 `aria-invalid` (7 of them in unused shadcn primitives) and 2 `aria-describedby`. Concretely in this file: `societyError` (:2293), `invoicingError` (:2430), `dobError` (:3652), `voucherError` (:2103) and `submitError` (:3389) all render as free-floating red text with no aria-describedby linking them to a field, no aria-invalid on the field, and no red border — so on a long wizard step the only signal that something failed is a sentence the user has to find. → _Extract the Field/Input/ErrorText trio that auth/signup/page.tsx:288-291 already gets right (aria-invalid + aria-describedby + role="alert" + an id) into a shared primitive and adopt it. authUi.tsx is the model — it is the cleanest form code in the repo._  `[missing-state]`

## Apply wizard — error text

- **`src/app/conferences/[slug]/apply/ConferenceApplyClient.tsx:2430`** — Four different error presentations inside one file: `mt-1.5 text-xs` left-aligned (society :2294, dob :3653), `text-xs` with `marginBottom:10` (voucher :2104), `mt-3 text-xs text-center` (invoicing :2431), `mb-4 text-sm text-center` (submit :3390). Same colour #8B2020, four different sizes, alignments and spacings, none with an icon or a background. Whether an error is centred or left-aligned currently depends on which step you are on. → _One `<FieldError>`: 12px, #8B2020, inline-start aligned, with a 12px AlertCircle, `mt-1.5`, `role="alert"`. Reserve the centred treatment for the single form-level submit error and give that one a tinted background so it reads as a different tier._  `[consistency]`

## Create committee — errors

- **`src/app/create/page.tsx:534`** — `setPasteError(language === 'ar' ? 'لا جديد لإضافته' : language === 'fr' ? ... : 'Nothing new to add')` — a four-way inline ternary instead of a translation key, in a file that imports useLanguage and uses t() everywhere else. When a fifth locale is added this silently falls through to English, and the string is invisible to anyone auditing translations.ts. → _Move it to a `create_paste_nothing_new` key. It is the only inline-ternary translation in the file._  `[copy]`

## App-wide — dead session panels

- **`src/components/ResolutionsPanel.tsx:96`** — ResolutionsPanel, SpeakersListPanel and CaucusPanel are imported by nothing (verified — zero matches for `from '@/components/<name>'`). They still carry a pre-redesign dark-blue palette that exists nowhere else: #0d1120, #141929, #1e2540, #4a5580, #8892aa, `bg-blue-600`. Worse, they have been half-migrated: `text-[#1C1410]` (near-black ink) now sits on `bg-[#141929]` (near-black) at :101/:107 and SpeakersListPanel:55 puts `text-[#1C1410]` on `bg-[#1B3828]` — unreadable if any of this ever rendered. They are a trap for anyone grepping for a pattern to copy. → _Delete all three. Also fix CLAUDE.md:63, which lists MotionsPanel — a file that has never existed — alongside them._  `[rule-violation]`

## /grain-dev

- **`src/app/grain-dev/page.tsx:182`** — The dev tool ships to production (robots-disallowed, but reachable) with the same half-migrated palette problem: `bg-[#222] border border-[#444] ... text-[#1C1410]` at :182 puts near-black ink on a near-black field, and `text-[#1B3828]` (forest) on the `#111` toolbar at :127 and :194 is roughly 1.4:1. The tool's own chrome is unreadable. → _Either guard the route behind `process.env.NODE_ENV !== 'production'` and stop shipping it, or fix the three ink colours to #EDE7D8. It is a one-minute fix that removes a broken page from the deployment._  `[contrast]`

## App-wide — print

- **`src/app/globals.css:281`** — Zero `@media print` and zero `@page` rules exist in src/. The product generates things people demonstrably print or PDF: invoices (manage/[slug]/financials/invoices), delegation placards (DelegationPlacard.tsx), the public MUN CV (/cv/[id]), and the committee scoreboard. Printing any of them today emits the full SiteNav, the fixed grain overlay, every shadow, and — on session pages — a transform-scaled fit-root that prints at the wrong size. → _Add one print block: hide nav/toasts/grain/floating layers, force NEU shadows off, set `color-scheme: light`, `@page { margin: 16mm }`, and un-transform #fit-root. Then give the placard, the invoice and the CV a `.printable` class that expands to full width._  `[missing-state]`

## App-wide — images

- **`src/components/FlagImg.tsx:13`** — 155 raw `<img>` elements against 2 uses of next/image. Of those: 140 have no `loading` attribute (so everything below the fold is eager), 83 have no onError at all, and 54 have no width+height attributes — including chair/[code]/page.tsx:213, :227, :297, :426, :499, :513 and join/page.tsx:333, :379 — so each one reserves no space and shifts the layout when it lands. → _Add `loading="lazy"` + `decoding="async"` + explicit width/height to every non-hero <img>, and route the handful of large decorative images (hero, conference banners, onboarding cards) through next/image so they get AVIF/WebP and srcset. The nav logo at SiteNav.tsx:165 is correctly `loading="eager" fetchPriority="high"` — that pattern just needs to stay the exception._  `[polish]`

## Delegation portal

- **`src/app/delegation/[societyId]/DelegationPortalClient.tsx:589`** — The page has a loading branch and a not-a-leader branch but no error branch. If the society fetch fails, `society` stays null and the page renders the header as `society?.name ?? 'Delegation'` with 0 delegates / 0 seats / 0 assigned — a fully-populated-looking portal for a delegation that failed to load. A head delegate cannot distinguish "your roster is empty" from "we couldn't reach the server". → _Add an explicit failed state with a Retry, and separately give the genuinely-empty case a first-run empty state ("No seats allocated yet — your organiser assigns these") instead of three zeros._  `[missing-state]`
- **`src/app/delegation/[societyId]/DelegationPortalClient.tsx:583`** — `<SiteNav hideLanguage />` silently removes the language toggle on this route with no explanation, so a user who was reading in French loses the control here and has to navigate away to change it. The prop is used on this page and nowhere else. → _Either keep the toggle (the page is English-only, but so is the rest of the conferences layer, and the toggle still governs the session pages) or move it into the profile dropdown so it is never route-dependent._  `[consistency]`

## Delegation portal — invite link

- **`src/app/delegation/[societyId]/DelegationPortalClient.tsx:686`** — The invite URL renders inside a `truncate` pill with no `title`, no expand and no select-all — the head delegate can see roughly the first third of the link and has no way to read the rest except copying it and pasting it somewhere. On a narrow window the visible portion may be nothing but the domain. → _Add `title={inviteUrl}` at minimum, and make the pill a read-only input so the text is selectable and the browser shows the tail on focus._  `[polish]`

## App-wide — disabled controls

- **`src/components/neu.tsx:589`** — NeuButton's disabled state is `background: 'rgba(27,56,40,0.14)'`, `color: NEU.muted`, `boxShadow: 'none'`, `cursor: 'default'` — a flat grey-green slab with 2.7:1 label text and no shadow, which reads as "this is broken" rather than "this is not available yet". Across src/ there are only 2 `cursor:'not-allowed'` against 12 `cursor:'default'`, so disabled controls do not even agree on what the cursor should say. → _Keep the extruded NEU.outSm shadow on disabled but flatten it to 40% and set the label to the darkened muted at 60% opacity — the control still looks like a button, just dimmed. Standardise on `cursor: not-allowed` and add a `title` naming the precondition ("Pick at least one preference first")._  `[polish]`

## App-wide — first run

- **`src/components/TutorialOverlay.tsx:1`** — A first-run tutorial exists for the chair and for nobody else. A delegate opening /delegate/[code] for the first time, a faculty advisor on /advisor/[code], a head delegate on /delegation/[societyId], and an organiser landing on /manage/[slug] all get the full interface with no orientation — and three of those four roles are typically first-timers (students), unlike the chair, who is usually the experienced one. → _Reuse TutorialOverlay's spotlight machinery with a 3-step script per role. The delegate script is the highest value: floor card, request to speak, submit a document._  `[missing-state]`

## App-wide — z-index

- **`src/components/TutorialOverlay.tsx:611`** — Stacking values are picked ad hoc with no scale: 9990/9993/9998/9999 here, `z-[60]` in GavelChip:265, `zIndex: 9999` in Toast:165, `z-50` in ModalOverlay:108, `zIndex: 50` in SiteNav's language menu :247, `zIndex: 15/20/30/40/50` in MapClient. Two portaled layers opening at once currently have no defined winner. → _Export a `Z = { nav: 30, dropdown: 40, modal: 50, overlay: 60, toast: 70, tutorial: 80 }` map from neu.tsx and replace the magic numbers._  `[consistency]`

## Chair session — Right of Reply overlay

- **`src/app/chair/[code]/page.tsx:3161`** — `fontFamily: "'Poppins', sans-serif"` on the 'GSL' label. Poppins is never loaded (see layout.tsx:129) and there is no Outfit fallback, so this one label in the RTR overlay renders in the OS system sans — a visibly different face sitting next to Outfit labels in the same overlay. → _Change to OUTFIT._  `[consistency]`

## App-wide — animation timing

- **`src/app/manage/[slug]/applications/page.tsx:527`** — The same enter animation is redefined under six different names with near-identical bodies: `neuFadeIn` (here, and again at :662 and :1064 in the same file), `neuPopIn` (financials/invoices:227 and pay/page.tsx:687), `dpFadeIn` (DelegationPortalClient:227), `gvRise` (PublicCVClient:50, apply/confirmation:57), `sbFade` (ScoreboardPanel:147), `gvRuleFade`/`gvRulePop` (VotingRulesPanel:332, :784), `profileMenuIn` (ProfileDropdown:244), `accFade` (accountUi:340). They differ only in whether the slide is 4px, 6px, 8px or 10px and whether scale is involved — so popovers on adjacent screens enter at subtly different distances. → _Define `@keyframes neu-pop-in` once in globals.css (opacity 0→1, translateY(-6px)→0, 160ms with EASE) and delete the nine local copies. That also gives one place to add the prefers-reduced-motion guard none of them currently have._  `[consistency]`

## App-wide — 105 transition:all

- **`src/app/create/page.tsx:1`** — 105 `transition-all` / `transition: 'all ...'` declarations remain, concentrated in create/page.tsx (12), chair/[code]/page.tsx (11), manage/[slug]/jobs (9), RollCallPanel (8) and HomeClient (8). `transition: all` animates layout-affecting properties (width, height, padding, top/left) whenever they change for unrelated reasons, which is why hovering a GSL row in a live session can produce a subtle reflow shudder. It is also the standard cause of the entrance-animation-plays-on-every-rerender bug. → _Replace each with the explicit property list. SiteNav.tsx:446 already shows the right form: `transition: 'color 150ms ease, background-color 150ms ease, border-left-color 150ms ease'`._  `[polish]`

## Chat — failed message retry

- **`src/components/chat/ChatMessageGroup.tsx:83`** — The retry control for a failed message is `<button className="focus:outline-none">` with `background:none; border:none; padding:0` and a 10px CHAT.danger label. It is the recovery path for lost data, it has no focus ring, no hover state, and a ~12px tap target. On touch it is very hard to hit accurately, and hitting the bubble instead does nothing. → _Give it `padding: 4px 8px`, a `min-height: 32px`, an underline-on-hover and a focus ring. The rest of ChatMessageGroup is genuinely good — it uses borderEndEndRadius/borderEndStartRadius and flex logical alignment, which is the only fully RTL-correct component in the app — this one control is the outlier._  `[missing-state]`

## App-wide — dialog roles

- **`src/components/ui/dialog.tsx:42`** — There is exactly one semantically-correct dialog primitive in the repo (this shadcn Radix wrapper, with focus trap, Escape, aria-modal and scroll lock built in) and roughly 43 files that hand-roll `fixed inset-0` overlays instead. Only 7 of those carry role=dialog and only 4 carry aria-modal. Meanwhile this file's scrim is `bg-black/10` — a 10% dim, far lighter than every hand-rolled overlay's 40–55% — so on the rare screen it IS used the background is barely dimmed. → _Either adopt this primitive for new modals and fix its scrim to NEU.scrim, or delete it and put the same behaviours into ModalOverlay (see the ModalOverlay finding). Shipping both, with the correct one unused, is the worst of the two options._  `[consistency]`

## App-wide — Poppins/Playfair webfont

- **`src/app/layout.tsx:129`** — The css2 request loads Playfair Display `ital,wght@0,700;1,400`. Grepping usage, the upright 700 cut appears in exactly one place — MobileGate.tsx:42, a screen only phone users see — so most visitors download a font cut that never paints. Separately, Noto Sans Arabic's five-weight variable file is requested on every route while ~2400 inline `fontFamily` literals hardcode `'Outfit', sans-serif` and never fall through to it, so the `:lang(ar)` rule in globals.css:57 is overridden almost everywhere it matters. → _Keep Playfair ital 400 (the gold accent words) and drop the 0,700 cut, restyling MobileGate's headline in Outfit 900. For Arabic, change the ~2400 literals to `var(--font-ui)` and define that variable once per locale — otherwise the Arabic face is being paid for and not used._  `[polish]`

## Session — chair copy code

- **`src/app/chair/[code]/page.tsx:2680`** — The session-code button has no `aria-label` and no visible affordance saying it is copyable — it looks like a static code chip (`bg-[#DDD4C0] rounded-lg font-mono`). Discovery depends on the user speculatively clicking a thing that looks like a label. The delegate equivalent (delegate/[code]/page.tsx:1149) is identical and gives no feedback either. → _Add a 12px lucide Copy icon beside the code and `aria-label="Copy session code"`. Small, but this is the single most-used control on the chair header at the start of every session._  `[polish]`

## Manage — bulk pulse

- **`src/app/manage/[slug]/applications/page.tsx:2929`** — `@keyframes bulkPulse { 0%,100% { transform: scale(1) ... } 50% { transform: scale(1.06) ... } }` runs an infinite scale-and-shadow pulse on the bulk-action button, unguarded by prefers-reduced-motion, on the app's most-used organiser screen. It also competes for attention with the selection count next to it. → _Guard it, and cap it at two iterations after a selection change rather than running forever. A control that pulses indefinitely stops reading as urgent and starts reading as noise._  `[missing-state]`

## App-wide — arrow glyphs in buttons

- **`src/lib/translations.ts:12`** — 476 `→` and 118 `←` glyphs are baked into translation strings ('START YOUR COMMITTEE →', 'NEXT →', 'Advance →', 'BEGIN SESSION →'). Because they are inside the label they inherit the button's `letterSpacing: '0.05em'` (neu.tsx:588), which puts an extra tracking gap before the arrow; they cannot be sized, coloured or animated independently; they cannot slide on hover the way NeuChecklistRow's real ChevronRight does at neu.tsx:689; and they render in whatever glyph the font supplies rather than matching the lucide stroke weight used elsewhere. → _Strip the arrows from the strings and pass `icon={ArrowRight}` (NeuButton already supports it) with `className="rtl:rotate-180"`. That fixes the tracking gap, the RTL problem and the hover-nudge in one change._  `[polish]`

## App-wide — image alt text

- **`src/components/Emoji.tsx:86`** — `<img alt={children} aria-hidden="true">` — the alt text is set and then suppressed by aria-hidden, which is contradictory. For decorative emoji aria-hidden is right and alt should be `""`; for meaningful ones (🎙 marking the current speaker, 🪑 marking a chair in the chat list) both are wrong and the meaning is lost entirely. FlagImg.tsx:15 has the mirror problem: `alt={code}` announces a bare two-letter code rather than the country name. → _Add a `label?: string` prop to Emoji — when present, `alt={label}` with no aria-hidden; when absent, `alt=""` + aria-hidden. In FlagImg pass the resolved country name as alt._  `[polish]`

## Session — voting page overlay

- **`src/app/voting/[code]/page.tsx:114`** — `background: 'rgba(5,4,3,0.92)', backdropFilter: 'blur(4px)'` — a 92% near-black scrim, the heaviest in the app by a wide margin (next heaviest is 0.55). Against the warm ivory system it reads as a hard cut to black rather than a dim, and the 4px blur is doing almost nothing under 92% opacity. → _If the intent is to kill the room for a formal vote — which is defensible — make it `rgba(27,56,40,0.94)` so it darkens toward forest rather than to a neutral black, and drop the blur (invisible at that opacity, and it costs a compositing layer on the projector machine)._  `[consistency]`

## Session cockpit — type scale on projectors

- **`src/app/chair/[code]/page.tsx:1`** — 523 inline `fontSize` declarations at ≤11px plus 236 Tailwind `text-[≤11px]` utilities ship across src/. In the session tree these are then multiplied by FitToScreen's scale, which is `window.innerHeight / 820` — on a 1024×768 projector that is 0.937, so a 9px label paints at 8.4 device px, and on a 1366×768 conference-room laptop the same. This is the one product context where the screen is being read from across a room. → _Set a floor of 11px for anything that carries information (labels, counts, timers) inside the session tree, and reserve 9–10px strictly for decorative eyebrows. Consider a chair-side 'Projector' toggle that bumps the FitToScreen BASE_H down so everything scales up._  `[contrast]`

## Settings — Points tab

- **`src/components/SettingsPanel.tsx:1198`** — 'Ranking blend' (:1198) with endpoints 'Objective' (:1200) and its counterpart is presented as a bare slider with no explanation of what blending does to a delegate's headline number, and — like the rest of the Points tab — it is hardcoded English inside a translated panel. A chair setting up awards has to guess whether 0 or 100 favours their subjective ratings. → _Translate the tab, and add a live preview under the slider: 'Ada Lovelace would score 47 (32 objective + 15 quality)' updating as the slider moves. A blend control without a worked example is a control nobody trusts._  `[copy]`

## App-wide — one-item and long-name states

- **`src/app/delegation/[societyId]/DelegationPortalClient.tsx:657`** — Pluralisation is handled correctly in exactly one place — `members.length === 1 ? 'Delegate' : 'Delegates'` here — and nowhere else systematically. Elsewhere: ScoreboardPanel and the session panels emit bare counts with fixed plural nouns, and long-name truncation is ad hoc (`truncate` in some rows, `line-clamp-2` in CommitteeIdentityBadge:149, nothing at all in others). translations.ts even leaks the English plural rule into other locales via `session_hours_until_delete` taking an `{s}` variable that callers fill with 's' or '' (chair/[code]/page.tsx:2092). → _Add a tiny `plural(n, key)` helper to the translation layer with per-locale rules — Arabic has six plural forms and the `{s}` hack cannot express any of them — and apply `truncate` + `title` consistently to every user-supplied name (committee names, delegation names, chair names, document titles)._  `[consistency]`

## App-wide — hardcoded ink on dark surfaces

- **`src/components/SpeakersListPanel.tsx:55`** — A find-and-replace during the forest/ivory redesign substituted `text-white` with `text-[#1C1410]` everywhere including on dark backgrounds, leaving near-black ink on forest and near-black fields. SpeakersListPanel:55 (`text-[#1C1410]` on `bg-[#1B3828]`), :74, :81, :94, :161; ResolutionsPanel:42, :107; grain-dev:182. All three files are dead or dev-only today, but the same pattern is one careless copy-paste away from a live screen. → _Delete the dead files, and add a lint rule or a code-review checklist item: `#1C1410` may never appear on `#1B3828`, `#141929`, `#222` or `#111`._  `[contrast]`

> **Method / limits:** METHOD — Static source read only. No dev server, no browser tools, nothing edited, no git operations. Everything is from reading files in the working tree on feature/conferences-auth plus reproducible greps and small Python scripts run from the repo root. Contrast ratios are computed WCAG 2.1 relative-luminance values from the literal hex/rgba pairs in the source (alpha composited against the stated background), not eyeballed. The bidi claim about arrow glyphs was verified with Python `unicodedata.mirrored`, which returns 0 for U+2190/U+2192 — i.e. the glyph direction is preserved under RTL, which is what makes the Arabic back-button finding real.

SCOPE — I swept the dimensions the completeness critic flagged as thin or zero, across the whole app rather than one area: 404/not-found, route-level loading, skeletons, error states and error copy, focus rings and keyboard operability, dialog semantics, landmarks and aria-live, RTL/Arabic, motion and reduced-motion, iconography, contrast, form-validation presentation, empty states, responsive/mobile, image and asset loading, and print. I deliberately did NOT re-report the things the critic listed as already covered (portal/fitScale, the two native date-picker violations, the dead-code deletions as such — I only mention the dead panels for their palette hazard, which is a new angle).

COVERAGE LIMITS — I sampled rather than exhaustively read the three largest uncovered surfaces (/conferences/[slug]/pay at 2341 lines, /delegation/[societyId] at 755, /account/profile at 1211); my findings on those are from targeted greps plus reading the state-handling regions, so a full read of them will surface more. I did not read manage/[slug]/communications, manage/[slug]/jobs, or the blog routes beyond greps. The /conferences/landing-lab variants I skipped entirely as they look experimental.

NON-UI OBSERVATIONS (per the brief, not filed as findings):
• src/app/manage/[slug]/live/page.tsx:46 — `navigator.clipboard.writeText(value).catch(() => {})` swallows the rejection but `setCopied(true)` runs unconditionally on the next line, so a denied clipboard permission shows a false success. Filed as a UI finding because the visible lie is the problem, but the root cause is the empty catch.
• AGENTS.md's "Document limits — LEGACY, NO UI" section is now stale: the Settings control DOES exist at src/components/SettingsPanel.tsx:514-530 (`wpSubmissionLimit`/`drSubmissionLimit` with a per-committee semantic documented in the comment). The doc says "the Settings UI ... no longer exists anywhere in src/". Worth correcting.
• src/app/grain-dev/page.tsx ships to production with no `NODE_ENV` guard. It is robots-disallowed, so this is hygiene rather than exposure, but it is a broken-looking page on a public domain.
• src/app/layout.tsx:140 preloads `/Conferences.webp` with `fetchPriority="high"` on every route including /join, /create, /chair, /delegate and /voting, which never paint it. Confirms the critic's note.
• src/components/Toast.tsx is a well-built, portaled, aria-live-correct component mounted in exactly one file. The gap is adoption, not design — same shape as the LogoDisc situation the critic verified.
• src/components/chat/ChatMessageGroup.tsx is the single most RTL-correct component in the repo (borderEndEndRadius/borderEndStartRadius, flex logical alignment, no physical margins). If a house pattern for RTL is ever documented, this file is the reference.
• src/app/auth/authUi.tsx remains the best form code in the product (role=status, aria-live, aria-describedby, aria-invalid, real focus-visible). Every form-validation finding above resolves to "do what authUi already does".


---

# Gap sweep: cross-product  
_62 findings_


## Global — page chrome

- **`src/components/SiteNav.tsx:159`** — Five different page headers for one product: SiteNav is a 72px transparent bar PLUS a separate fixed floating pill (SiteNav.tsx:99, :159); /manage is a 56px solid-forest #1B3828 bar (manage/[slug]/layout.tsx:813); chair/delegate/advisor/voting are a 44px ivory h-11 bar with a 1px #DDD4C0 rule (chair/[code]/page.tsx:2622); /create is a bare 72px nav (create/page.tsx:580); /join is a bare 64px h-16 nav (join/page.tsx:331). Nothing about the top of the screen is stable as a user moves through the product. → _Reduce to two archetypes: the 72px site header (marketing, account, public conferences) and ONE app header component with a height prop used by /manage (56px) and the session cockpits (44px), sharing a left-mark / centre-context / right-actions grid. Delete the bespoke navs in /create and /join and give them the app header._  `[consistency]`

## Global — brand mark

- **`src/components/SessionsHeaderLogo.tsx:27`** — Three logo assets, four render paths. Sessions cockpits use /GavellingLogo.png via shared SessionsHeaderLogo at 32px; SiteNav paints /Conferences.webp at 36px on conferences paths and /GavellingSessionsApp.webp at 32–40px elsewhere (SiteNav.tsx:75, :166, :196); /manage paints /Conferences.webp through filter: grayscale(1) brightness(2.1) (manage/[slug]/layout.tsx:828). Homepage → conference → session shows three different marks in three minutes. → _Keep one wordmark and render the context word ('Conferences' / 'Sessions') as adjacent text, so the mark itself never changes. If two lockups must stay, make them the same optical height and cap-height across all five headers._  `[consistency]`

## Advisor + Create + Join headers

- **`src/app/advisor/[code]/page.tsx:489`** — Three of the six session routes bypass the shared SessionsHeaderLogo and hand-roll a raw <img src="/GavellingLogo.png">, each at a different size: advisor w-[150px] max-h-9 (advisor:489), create h-10 (create/page.tsx:581), join w-[150px] h-auto with no max height at all (join/page.tsx:333). chair (:2623), delegate (:1121) and voting (:198) use the shared component at 32px. The advisor and join marks are ~50% larger than the chair's for the same product. → _Replace all three raw <img> tags with <SessionsHeaderLogo height={…} />. The component already derives width from the 4:1 natural ratio and handles the error fallback; three call sites currently reimplement it wrongly._  `[consistency]`

## Global nav — active state

- **`src/components/SiteNav.tsx:112`** — `const active = pathname === link.href` is an exact match, so the nav pill shows NO active item on /conferences/explore, /conferences/[slug], /conferences/map, /my-conferences, /account/*, /manage/* and /conferences/roles — i.e. on almost every real conferences page. The CONFERENCES tab only lights up on the bare homepage. → _Compute active with a prefix match plus an ownership map, e.g. CONFERENCES owns ['/', '/conferences', '/manage', '/my-conferences', '/account'] and SESSIONS owns ['/sessions', '/create', '/join']. Keep '/' exact-only inside that map so it does not swallow everything._  `[missing-state]`

## Session cockpits — global nav

- **`src/app/chair/[code]/page.tsx:2622`** — None of /chair, /delegate, /advisor, /voting, /create or /join renders SiteNav, so there is no route back into conferences, no account menu, no sign-out and no language control. The single exit is the logo, which hard-links to /sessions (SessionsHeaderLogo.tsx:41) — the wrong half of the product for a conference chair who arrived from /conferences/[slug]. → _Add a compact 'context chip' to the left of the session toolbar: for a conference session show the conference logo + acronym linking back to /conferences/[slug]; for a standalone session keep the Gavelling mark linking to /sessions. The data is already fetched (chair/[code]/page.tsx:1280 reads conference_committees)._  `[wasted-effort]`

## Session cockpits — conference identity

- **`src/app/chair/[code]/page.tsx:2160`** — `session_origin='conference'` is used only as an access gate (lib/conferenceAccess.ts:29) and never surfaces in the UI. A chair running WorldMUN's DISEC sees only DISEC — no conference name, logo or link. The access-denied screen even says 'contact your conference organisers' (chair:2160) without naming the conference or offering a link. → _Show the conference acronym as a small eyebrow above the committee name in CommitteeIdentityBadge, and make the denied screen name the conference and link to /conferences/[slug]._  `[missing-state]`

## Account menu

- **`src/components/ProfileDropdown.tsx:276`** — The shared account dropdown has five rows (Profile, MUN CV, Conference Calendar, Credits, Your conferences) and not one of them touches the sessions half. A signed-in user has no menu route to /create, /join or a past session. Sessions are reachable only from the SESSIONS nav item, which is itself absent on every session page. → _Add a 'SESSIONS' group to the dropdown with 'Start a session' → /create and 'Join with a code' → /join, mirroring the 'YOUR CONFERENCES' block._  `[wasted-effort]`

## Global — legal footer

- **`src/components/FooterLegal.tsx:46`** — FooterLegal (Terms / Privacy / GDPR rights) is rendered on 9 public marketing and conference pages but on ZERO signed-in or session surfaces: no /manage/*, no /account/*, no /my-conferences, and none of chair/delegate/advisor/voting/create/join. The screens that actually process names, countries and chat messages are the ones with no route to the privacy policy. → _Render <FooterLegal tone="ivory" /> at the bottom of the /manage and /account content wrappers, and put a single 'Terms · Privacy' line in the /join footer (the delegate's only pre-session screen)._  `[consistency]`

## Global — head

- **`src/app/layout.tsx:139`** — `<link rel="preload" as="image" href="/Conferences.webp" fetchPriority="high">` fires on EVERY route including /chair, /delegate, /join, /create and /voting, which never paint that file — they paint /GavellingLogo.png, which is not preloaded. Sessions users pay a high-priority download for an asset they never see while their own logo waits. → _Move the preload into the conferences-side layout (or drop it and let SiteNav's fetchPriority="high" img do the work), and add a matching preload for /GavellingLogo.png in a sessions-side layout._  `[polish]`

## Design system — adoption

- **`src/components/neu.tsx:24`** — The neumorphic primitives (NeuCard, NeuButton, NeuStatTile, NeuInset, NeuPill, NeuProgress) are imported by 41 conferences-half files and by ZERO session pages or session panels. chair, delegate, advisor, voting, create, join, RollCallPanel, SettingsPanel, MotionsModal, DocumentsModal, ScoreboardPanel and TutorialOverlay together carry 1,966 raw hex literals. Only the new chat/, GavelChip and CommitteeIdentityBadge import NEU. → _Do not port the whole sessions half at once. Start by replacing the three highest-frequency literals with tokens (#EDE7D8→NEU.base, #1B3828→NEU.forest, #9A8A78→NEU.muted) and adopt NeuButton for the ~12 primary CTAs. That alone makes the halves recognisably one system._  `[rule-violation]`

## Cards — both halves

- **`src/components/neu.tsx:149`** — Two incompatible card languages on the same #EDE7D8 page. Sessions: #FAF8F3 fill + `1px solid #DDD4C0` + Tailwind rounded-xl, 185 occurrences of the border alone. Conferences: NEU.surface #F0EBDD + the dual NEU.out shadow + borderRadius 22, no border, 224 shadow uses. Side by side the sessions cards read as flat outlined boxes and the conferences cards as extruded — the single biggest visual divergence in the product. → _Adopt the neumorphic card as the product card. Concretely: a shared <AppCard> that renders NEU.surface + NEU.out + radius 22, and sweep the 50 `border: '1px solid #DDD4C0'` + 135 `border-[#DDD4C0]` sessions call sites onto it. The conferences treatment is the better one and should be the target._  `[consistency]`

## Global — radii

- **`src/components/neu.tsx:149`** — Two radius vocabularies. Sessions uses Tailwind's scale (rounded-lg 8 ×83, rounded-xl 12 ×185, rounded-2xl 16 ×43, rounded-3xl 24 ×10, rounded-sm ×14, rounded-md ×9). Conferences uses arbitrary numeric px: 12 ×27, 14 ×23, 16 ×19, 8 ×13, 10 ×12, 20 ×9, 24 ×8, 18 ×7, 11 ×7, 22 ×5, plus 6/9/7/4/2/1/26/28 one-offs. Even the pill radius is split: 999 ×80 vs 9999 ×40. → _Publish a 5-step radius scale in neu.tsx (RADIUS = { sm: 10, md: 14, lg: 18, xl: 22, pill: 999 }) and map both halves onto it. Ban raw radius numbers in review; the 11/9/7/4/2/1 one-offs are all rounding noise nobody chose._  `[consistency]`

## Global — surface palette

- **`src/app/voting/[code]/page.tsx:545`** — Four ivories in circulation with no rule for which is which: NEU.base #EDE7D8 (page), NEU.surface #F0EBDD (conferences card), #FAF8F3 (sessions card, ProfileDropdown panel, landing CREAM, 110 files) and #F6F1E9 (voting/[code]:545, :857; advisor:423; DocumentsModal:960, :1009; MotionsModal:1358 — full-screen takeover backgrounds only). #F6F1E9 and #FAF8F3 appear nowhere in neu.tsx. → _Add the two missing values as tokens with named roles (NEU.raised = '#FAF8F3', NEU.stage = '#F6F1E9') or collapse #F6F1E9 into NEU.base. Right now a reviewer cannot tell whether a given ivory is deliberate._  `[consistency]`

## Global — token naming

- **`src/app/conferences/landing-lab/shared.tsx:20`** — The identifier GOLD means two different colours depending on the file. `#B6871F` in landing-lab/shared.tsx:20 and admin/AdminClient.tsx:24; `#EED98A` in manage/[slug]/jobs/page.tsx:39, EmailComposer.tsx:30 and ui/carousel.tsx:19. In neu.tsx those are NEU.deepGold and NEU.gold respectively. Anyone copying a `color: GOLD` line between files silently changes the colour. → _Delete every local GOLD/INK/FOREST/MUTED/BORDER/CREAM const (6 files) and import NEU. Where a name must survive for diff hygiene, alias it: `const GOLD = NEU.deepGold`._  `[rule-violation]`

## Global — hairline

- **`src/components/neu.tsx:33`** — #DDD4C0 is the product's hairline/border colour — 135 Tailwind uses in sessions, plus HAIRLINE in landing-lab/shared.tsx:23 and BORDER in jobs/page.tsx:43, communications/page.tsx:209, ConferencesRolesClient.tsx:43, EmailComposer.tsx:32, DefaultEmailPreviewModal.tsx:21 — but neu.tsx has no border token at all, which is exactly why it keeps being re-declared. → _Add `NEU.hairline = '#DDD4C0'` (and `NEU.hairlineSoft = 'rgba(221,212,192,0.65)'`, already used in manage/[slug]/layout.tsx:216) and delete the five local BORDER/HAIRLINE consts._  `[consistency]`

## Global — error/danger colour

- **`src/components/chat/chatTokens.ts:20`** — neu.tsx has no red, so nine reds have grown independently: #8B2020 (345 uses, the de-facto product red), #FCA5A5 ×11, #A03030 ×5, #7A2020 ×2, #6E1E1E, #3C1414, #f87171, #FFD0D0, #8B3030 — plus the brand-new chat inventing a tenth, `CHAT.danger = '#B4432F'`, with a comment at chatTokens.ts:19 explicitly saying it exists because neu.tsx has none. chair/[code]/page.tsx:1048 also uses a raw Tailwind `bg-red-500`. → _Add NEU.danger = '#8B2020' plus NEU.dangerSoft = 'rgba(139,32,32,0.10)' (already the de facto pair) and point chat's CHAT.danger at it. Replace the bg-red-500 at chair:1048._  `[consistency]`

## Global — secondary text contrast

- **`src/components/neu.tsx:33`** — NEU.muted #9A8A78 on NEU.base #EDE7D8 measures 2.71:1 (2.81:1 on #F0EBDD, 3.15:1 on #FAF8F3) — well under the 4.5:1 AA floor, and it is the secondary-label colour on BOTH halves, usually at 10–12px. #6A5A4A, already used 18× on the voting page, measures 5.36:1 on the same background and passes. → _Darken NEU.muted to ~#6A5A4A (or introduce NEU.mutedText = '#6A5A4A' for anything under 14px and keep #9A8A78 for decorative rules and icon strokes only). One token change fixes hundreds of labels across both halves simultaneously — the highest-leverage single edit in this audit._  `[contrast]`

## Manage — section eyebrows

- **`src/app/manage/[slug]/layout.tsx:280`** — NEU.deepGold #B6871F on NEU.surface #F0EBDD is 2.72:1, and it is used for the smallest type in the conferences half: the rail's section headers at 9px/800 (manage/[slug]/layout.tsx:280), the applications eyebrow at 11px (applications/page.tsx:2354), the financials eyebrow (financials/layout.tsx:69), the dashboard eyebrow at 9px (manage/[slug]/page.tsx:1145). Similarly NEU.amber #B8844A on #EDE7D8 is 2.64:1 and carries the 10px 'ACCESS DENIED' / 'SECTION RESTRICTED' labels (layout.tsx:744, :781). → _Darken the eyebrow gold to ~#8A6614 (already used for the nav badge text at layout.tsx:358) which clears 4.5:1, or raise the eyebrow to 12px/700 with normal letter-spacing. Do not fix by adding a background chip — the eyebrow's job is to be quiet, not boxed._  `[contrast]`

## Chair sidebar — CommitteeIdentityBadge

- **`src/components/CommitteeIdentityBadge.tsx:140`** — In the newest sessions component, the full-name line is rgba(237,231,216,0.52) at 10.5px on forest = 3.99:1 (:140) and the topic line is rgba(238,217,138,0.55) at 10.5px = 3.88:1 (:157). Both sit under a 17px/900 gold headline at 9.09:1, so the contrast falls off a cliff inside a single 60px block, and this is the ONE place the committee's full name is stated. → _Raise both to 0.70 alpha (5.3:1 for the gold, comparable for the ivory) and bump to 11px. The hierarchy still reads — the 17px/900 weight is doing that work, not the opacity._  `[contrast]`

## Global — paper texture

- **`src/app/account/layout.tsx:10`** — The same 300×300 feTurbulence data-URI is inlined verbatim in 24 files, and it is not applied consistently: mixBlendMode 'multiply' ×32 vs 'overlay' ×10, opacity 0.18 (sessions, HomeClient, account, create, join) vs 0.07 (conferences hero, ConferenceDetailClient:1154, create:372) vs 0.08 vs 0.12 (CommitteeEditorModal:90). landing-lab/shared.tsx:32 already exports GRAIN but only lab files import it. The brand's most distinctive surface treatment is ~2.5× stronger on session screens than on conference screens. → _Export GRAIN plus a ready-made <GrainLayer tone="page"|"media" /> from neu.tsx, standardise on multiply/0.14 over ivory and overlay/0.08 over photography, and replace all 24 inline copies._  `[consistency]`

## Global — type scale

- **`src/app/manage/[slug]/applications/page.tsx:2357`** — Two unrelated type scales. Conferences sets numeric px with half-pixel steps — 26 distinct values from 8.5 to 34, of which 599 declarations are ≤13px (fontSize 11 ×127, 10 ×115, 13 ×77, 12.5 ×72, 12 ×64, 9 ×48, 11.5 ×45, 10.5 ×45, 9.5 ×17). Sessions uses Tailwind's scale (text-xs ×220, text-sm ×150, text-2xl ×32) plus four arbitrary brackets. Shared components carry both at once. → _Define a 9-step scale in neu.tsx (e.g. 10/11.5/13/15/17/20/24/28/34) and forbid half-pixel steps; the 10.5/11.5/12.5/13.5/14.5 tier is 179 declarations of unintentional variance nobody will ever perceive as deliberate._  `[consistency]`

## Global — monospace

- **`src/app/globals.css:51`** — globals.css declares no --font-mono, so every `font-mono` class resolves to Tailwind's default system stack (ui-monospace/SFMono/Menlo), NOT the DM Mono the app loads at layout.tsx:130. `font-mono` appears 72× in the sessions half and 48× in shared components but only 1× in the whole conferences half; the explicit "'DM Mono', monospace" literal appears 22× in sessions and 3× in conferences. Meanwhile landing-lab/shared.tsx:29 deliberately aliases MONO → Outfit because "monospace is eliminated on the conferences side". → _Decide once. Either set --font-mono: 'DM Mono' in the @theme block and keep mono for numerals/codes only, or follow the conferences decision and strip font-mono from the sessions half. Today the product pays to download DM Mono and then renders 120 elements in Menlo._  `[consistency]`

## Manage — page titles

- **`src/app/manage/[slug]/applications/page.tsx:2354`** — Four generations of the manage page header coexist. New: gold uppercase eyebrow with a ' · ' separator, 11px/800/0.14em, 26px h1, 46px NeuIconDisc (applications:2354, financial-aid/page.tsx:19, financials/layout.tsx:69). Old: muted #9A8A78 eyebrow with a ' / ' separator, 12px/700/0.12em, text-2xl h1, no disc (committees:1146, assignment:3797, settings:1655, jobs:891, communications:1844, import:576). Documents uses 11px + 24px h1 (documents:709). Live has no acronym at all and a 28px h1 (live:690). Dashboard uses a 9px eyebrow and an 18px h1 (page.tsx:1145). → _Extract <PageHeader eyebrow icon title actions /> and use it on all eleven manage routes; the applications version is the right target. This is a ~2-hour change that makes the whole organiser dashboard read as one product._  `[consistency]`

## Session cockpits — page titles

- **`src/app/chair/[code]/page.tsx:2676`** — The sessions half has no page-title pattern at all. The chair's only committee identifier outside the sidebar is a truncated 12px grey inline string, `{name}: {topic}` in text-[#9A8A78] text-xs, and it is shown ONLY during pre-session (chair:2676) — during live debate it is replaced by the tab row and disappears entirely. Compare with /manage, where every page states acronym + section + title. → _Keep a persistent context line in the session toolbar (acronym · phase) so the chair, a co-chair joining mid-session and a projector audience can always see which committee and which phase this screen is._  `[hierarchy]`

## Nav type scale

- **`src/app/chair/[code]/page.tsx:2628`** — The session tab labels are `text-[18px]` with font-weight 900 when active (chair:2628, delegate:1134) — the largest navigation type anywhere in the product. SiteNav's links are 13px (SiteNav.tsx:125), the manage rail's are 13px (layout.tsx:341), the account nav's are 12px. Four tabs at 18px/900 inside a 44px bar also crowd the badges, which sit at `top-1 right-1` in a 16px circle (chair:2643). → _Bring the session tabs down to 14–15px/800 and give the row back some breathing room; that also stops the four flex-1 tabs from truncating on a translated label (delegate:1138 already needs a `truncate`)._  `[hierarchy]`

## MobileGate

- **`src/components/MobileGate.tsx:30`** — The only screen in the app that opts out of Outfit: `fontFamily: 'system-ui, sans-serif'` on the wrapper (:30), with a Playfair Display h1 (:42). Its 'OPEN ON DESKTOP' block (:65-78) is a <div> styled exactly like a forest CTA button — it looks pressable and does nothing. And the closing line '(or just rotate your phone, we're not judging)' (:81) is wrong: the gate is user-agent based (:12-14), so rotating never dismisses it. → _Switch to Outfit, make the CTA either a real action (copy the URL to clipboard, or email the link to yourself) or demote it to plain text, and delete the rotate line — it tells the user to do something that provably cannot work._  `[consistency]`

## MobileGate coverage

- **`src/components/MobileGate.tsx:17`** — MobileGate is applied to /chair only (chair/[code]/page.tsx:2618). /delegate, /advisor, /voting, /create and /join are unguarded, so a phone gets the full desktop layout squeezed through FitToScreen's fixed 820px frame (FitToScreen.tsx:9). Delegates are the highest-volume mobile users in the product; the conferences half is responsive throughout. → _Either make /delegate genuinely responsive (it is the one session view that should be phone-first) or extend MobileGate to /voting and /create and give /delegate an explicit mobile layout. The current state — one route gated, five silently degraded — is the worst of both._  `[missing-state]`

## Committee naming rule

- **`src/app/manage/[slug]/assignment/page.tsx:664`** — The AGENTS.md acronym rule has three implementations. The shared one (lib/presetNames.ts:237 deriveCommitteeAcronym + :262 committeeDisplayName, 11-word stop list, initials capped at 2–6 chars) is honoured in exactly ONE place, chair/[code]/page.tsx:2947-2952. assignment/page.tsx:655-673 duplicates it as local `committeeLabels`/`autoAcronym` with a DIFFERENT 14-word stop list (adds French de/du/des/la/le) and no length cap. Everywhere else it is raw `abbreviation ?? name` (committees:1318, :1432; documents:344; ChairParticipant:66; AllocationCard:73; CommitteesSessionsCard:98). → _Delete committeeLabels/autoAcronym from assignment and import deriveCommitteeAcronym + committeeDisplayName. Then apply the same helper to committees/page.tsx and the three participant cards. Today one committee can be labelled differently on /manage/assignment than in its own chair sidebar._  `[rule-violation]`

## Session views — long committee names

- **`src/components/RollCallPanel.tsx:543`** — Outside CommitteeIdentityBadge, every session surface renders the raw name with `truncate` and no acronym or second line: RollCallPanel:543 (text-lg font-black truncate), advisor:503 (same), delegate:1066, :1198, chair:2676. 'Disarmament and International Security Committee' becomes 'Disarmament and Internatio…' rather than DISEC with the full name beneath — the exact case the UI RULE names. → _Route these five through committeeDisplayName + deriveCommitteeAcronym, or reuse CommitteeIdentityBadge's primary/secondary pair, which already implements the rule correctly._  `[rule-violation]`

## Native date/time inputs

- **`src/app/manage/[slug]/jobs/page.tsx:599`** — Five native pickers survive against the DatePicker rule, all on the conferences half: `datetime-local` at settings/page.tsx:1871, :1885 and jobs/page.tsx:599; `type="time"` at committees/page.tsx:274 and documents/page.tsx:281. The sessions half has none. jobs:599 in particular is a public-facing job posting deadline. → _Split each datetime-local into <DatePicker> + a house time control, and build the missing TimePicker once (a 15-minute-step listbox portaled like DatePicker) so committees:274 and documents:281 can use it. Five call sites, one new component._  `[rule-violation]`

## Popovers inside FitToScreen

- **`src/components/Portal.tsx:9`** — Portal mounts into #fit-root when present (Portal.tsx:9), and FitToScreen gives that element a CSS transform (FitToScreen.tsx:66), so raw getBoundingClientRect() pixels land in the wrong coordinate space. The correction is written FOUR separate times — chat/chatTokens.ts:portalFrame(), GavelChip.tsx:38 fitScale(), VotingRulesPanel.tsx:222-234, TutorialOverlay.tsx:245-262 toDesign() — and is MISSING in SettingsPanel.tsx:74, MotionsModal.tsx:151 and :260, and DocumentsModal.tsx:169, all of which portal at raw viewport coordinates. → _Move the transform correction into Portal.tsx itself (expose a usePortalFrame() alongside it) and delete the four duplicates. The conferences half has no #fit-root, so the mapping is the identity there and the change is safe for Christian's code. This single fix corrects nine popovers and finally makes the AGENTS.md popover rule true on session pages._  `[rule-violation]`

## Settings drawer — language menu

- **`src/components/SettingsPanel.tsx:822`** — The globe dropdown in the chair Settings drawer is `absolute right-0 top-full` inside a `overflow-hidden` drawer (SettingsPanel.tsx:805, :824) rather than portaled, and its outside-click catcher is a `fixed inset-0` div (:824) which, inside the transformed #fit-root, is sized to the scaled root rather than the viewport — so clicks in the uncovered strip do not close it. /create's copy of the same menu (create/page.tsx:596-598) has the identical structure. → _Extract ONE <LanguageMenu> that portals through Portal with the fit-root correction, and use it in SiteNav (desktop + mobile), /create and SettingsPanel. Three hand-rolled copies exist today with different fonts, anchors and z-indices._  `[rule-violation]`

## Informational hints

- **`src/components/MotionsModal.tsx:141`** — Three separate hover-hint components in the sessions half, all correct per the rule but none shared: MotionsModal.tsx:141 InfoHint (288px wide, 160ms close delay, raw getBoundingClientRect with no fit-root correction), VotingRulesPanel.tsx:263 InfoDot (230×116, 220ms, correctly transform-mapped), chat/ChatThread.tsx:35 InfoHint (16px badge, 180ms, not portaled at all so the chat scroller can clip it). The conferences half instead uses static inline <Info> icons with no popover (assignment:833, apply:275, accountUi:315, ConferenceRosterPicker:133, QuestionBuilder:99). → _Ship one <InfoHint> in src/components (portaled, transform-corrected, 200ms delay, focus-accessible) and delete the three. Then decide whether the conferences half should adopt it for its five static Info rows, or keep those deliberately always-visible._  `[consistency]`

## Identity marks

- **`src/components/CommitteeEditorModal.tsx:77`** — Two competing emblem primitives with overlapping jobs. LogoDisc (used by the manage rail, dashboard, conference hero, CommitteeIdentityBadge, LiveModals) and MonogramMedallion (used by manage/committees:1426, :1600, manage/documents:344, ConferenceDetailClient:2828, all three participant cards, both invite pages). Their fallbacks share the gradient but differ in border (1px rgba(238,217,138,0.35) vs none), shadow (0 4px 12px .15 vs 0 10px 24px .26), grain overlay (only MonogramMedallion), monogram length (3 vs 6 chars) and glyph ratio (0.24–0.3 vs 0.135–0.167). The same committee at 46px looks different on two adjacent screens. → _Fold MonogramMedallion into LogoDisc as a `variant="medallion"` (grain + heavier shadow + crisis gradient) so there is one component and one fallback language._  `[consistency]`

## Iconography

- **`src/components/Emoji.tsx:11`** — The two halves use two different emoji vendors from two CDNs. Emoji.tsx (flat Twemoji SVG) is imported by exactly the sessions half — chair, delegate, advisor, voting, join, DocumentsModal, MotionsModal, FlagImg. Emoji3D (Microsoft Fluent 3D PNG, neu.tsx:79) is used by 19 conferences files. So 🎙 or 🏁 renders flat on a chair's screen and glossy-3D on the organiser's. On top of that the sessions half has 44 raw system emoji that bypass both, and lucide-react is imported by 63 conferences files but only 1 of the 6 session routes — the sessions half hand-draws 23 inline <svg> paths instead (chair:2688 trophy, chair:2698 gear). → _Pick one icon language per context and write it down: lucide for controls everywhere, and ONE emoji vendor for decorative glyphs. Replace the 23 inline SVGs with lucide (Trophy, Settings) — they are already in the bundle._  `[consistency]`

## Loading states

- **`src/components/Skeleton.tsx:16`** — Three loading languages. (1) SkeletonBlock/.neu-shimmer — a well-documented shared primitive used ONLY by conferences (my-conferences:970, ConferenceDetailClient:1880, ParticipantView:117, account/calendar:213). (2) animate-pulse grey blocks, hand-rolled in ProfileDropdown:366 and manage/live/page.tsx:781 — the shared component bypassed inside shared components. (3) GavelLoader, a 72px animated gavel + 'LOADING…', copy-pasted FOUR times: chair:76, delegate:65, voting:288 and inlined again at advisor:399. The sessions half has zero skeletons; the conferences half never shows the gavel. → _Extract GavelLoader to src/components once, use SkeletonBlock in ProfileDropdown and manage/live, and add skeletons to the session cockpits (the speaker card and queue are perfectly skeleton-shaped) so a slow join does not stare at a spinning gavel._  `[missing-state]`

## Confirmation + error dialogs

- **`src/app/create/page.tsx:495`** — A shared useConfirmModal/ConfirmModal exists and is used 8× — all on the conferences half. The sessions half uses it zero times, and the single most important failure in that half falls back to a browser `alert()`: create/page.tsx:495 'Failed to create committee. Please try again.' and :500 'Something went wrong. Please try again.' Neither says what went wrong or what to try. The conferences half is not clean either (settings:1364, :1425, :2609 alert(); committees:982 window.confirm, :994 window.alert). → _Replace all seven native dialogs with ConfirmModal / an inline error card, and make the create errors actionable ('Could not reach the server — check your connection and press Create again', with the button re-enabled)._  `[consistency]`

## Toasts

- **`src/components/Toast.tsx:67`** — Two useToast implementations: src/components/Toast.tsx:67 (used by exactly one file, manage/assignment:2976) and app/auth/authUi.tsx:563 (used by authUi:716). Neither is used anywhere in the sessions half, which instead hand-rolls a fixed toast for the gavel handover at chair/[code]/page.tsx:2720-2734 and another for the deleted-account notice at HomeClient.tsx:598-617, each with its own position, radius and colours. → _Keep components/Toast.tsx as the one host, delete the authUi duplicate, and move the chair gavel toast and HomeClient notice onto it. Four notification treatments for one concept is three too many._  `[consistency]`

## Create flows

- **`src/components/wizard.tsx:34`** — wizard.tsx (WizardShell with progress dots, TwoTabPick, CardSelect) is a shared step-questionnaire kit built on NEU — used by /conferences/new and /auth/onboarding. The sessions half's two equivalent flows use none of it and show no progress indicator at all: /create is select-preset → build (create/page.tsx:566-640) and /join is role → code → details (join/page.tsx:331+). A user creating a conference sees exactly where they are; a chair creating a committee does not. → _Wrap /create's two steps and /join's three in WizardShell, or at minimum add its progress dots. The kit already handles the back arrow, title and sub-line._  `[consistency]`

## /create — first screen

- **`src/app/create/page.tsx:259`** — The first screen of the sessions product presents three large video cards of which two are `active: false` — 'Regular Debate' badged Coming Soon (:238-252) and 'Crisis Committee' badged Coming H2 (:277-291). Every user must click the middle card, every time, to reach the only working path. The hover default is even pre-set to 'mun' (:242-244), acknowledging there is one answer. → _Skip straight to the build step and surface the two future modes as a small 'More formats coming' line, or keep the card wall only for first-time visitors and remember the choice. This is one guaranteed wasted click on the primary funnel._  `[wasted-effort]`

## Modal shells — both halves

- **`src/components/CommitteeEditorModal.tsx:96`** — Roughly 21 hand-rolled overlays with seven different scrims — bg-black/40 (HomeClient:870, SettingsPanel:802), rgba(0,0,0,0.4) (ModalOverlay:107, manage/layout:911), rgba(28,20,16,0.5)+blur(4px) (PositionPaperCard:296), rgba(28,20,16,0.45) no blur (ScoreboardPanel:148), rgba(0,0,0,0.52)+blur(3px) (TutorialOverlay:516), bg-black/10+backdrop-blur-xs (ui/dialog.tsx:42), rgba(5,4,3,0.92)+blur(4px) (voting:114) — and eight padding conventions (px-4 py-10, px-4, p-4, px-6, px-4 py-8, px-4 py-6, px-6 pb-6, none). The shared ModalOverlay (CommitteeEditorModal.tsx:96) is used by only 4 files. → _Promote ModalOverlay to src/components/ModalShell.tsx, fix one scrim (rgba(28,20,16,0.45) + blur(4px) reads best on ivory) and one padding, and migrate both halves. ui/dialog.tsx's bg-black/10 is far too light for an ivory page and should not be the outlier that survives._  `[consistency]`

## Entering a session from a conference

- **`src/app/conferences/[slug]/participant/AllocationCard.tsx:167`** — The four crossings from conferences into sessions are all drawn and behave differently. Delegate: <Link href="/join?code=…"> 'Join Session →', radius 12, padding 13/18, 13px (AllocationCard:167). Chair: <a href={absolute}/chair/…> 'JOIN SESSION' with a Radio icon, radius 12, py-3, 14px — and it skips /join entirely (ChairParticipant:168). Advisor: <Link href="/advisor/…"> 'View Session', radius 8, padding 7/14, 11px (CommitteesSessionsCard:110). Organiser: window.open('/chair/…?chairName=Secretariat', '_blank') — the only one that opens a new tab (LiveModals:188). → _One <EnterSessionButton role=… code=…> with one radius, one label convention ('Open session') and one target policy (same tab for all, or new tab for all). Today the same action has three sizes, three labels and two tab behaviours._  `[consistency]`

## Session code chip

- **`src/app/advisor/[code]/page.tsx:493`** — The six-character session code is presented five ways. chair:2680 — a button that swaps to '✓' for 2s. delegate:1149 — a visually IDENTICAL button that copies with zero acknowledgement, so the user cannot tell it worked. advisor:493 — a visually identical <span>, not clickable at all. ChairParticipant:139-166 — a ticket-stub split button with a Check + 'COPIED'. AllocationCard:145-165 — a DM Mono inset pill with a Check + 'Copied'. Same object, five affordances, one silent, one dead. → _Ship one <SessionCodeChip code copyable> with a single copy acknowledgement and use it in all five places. At minimum, give delegate:1149 the chair's ✓ feedback and make advisor:493 either a real button or visually distinct from the two that are._  `[consistency]`

## i18n — coverage

- **`src/contexts/LanguageContext.tsx:40`** — The sessions half is fully translated into en/es/fr/ar (6 route files use useT). The conferences half has ZERO useT calls — /manage, /conferences, /my-conferences, /account, /auth, /delegation, /invites are all English-only, including the homepage VariantStagefront. Picking Arabic still flips document.dir to rtl for the whole document (LanguageContext.tsx:33), so a user gets right-to-left layout over English copy on every conferences page. → _Until the conferences half is translated, gate the RTL flip on whether the current route is translated, and make the language menu say what it covers ('Session views: English/Español/Français/العربية')._  `[consistency]`

## Language toggle placement

- **`src/components/SiteNav.tsx:226`** — `hideLanguage` is applied to only 4 of the 11 untranslated conferences surfaces — my-conferences:1031, explore:887, conferences/new:603, delegation portal:583 — while the globe still shows on the homepage (VariantStagefront:346), the conference detail page (ConferenceDetailClient:1117), /conferences/roles (:771, :1045), the apply confirmation (:56), the paper page (:326) and every /account page (account/layout.tsx:73), none of which are translated. The patch that exists to hide a dead control is applied inconsistently. → _Derive hideLanguage from a route list in SiteNav rather than passing it per call site, so it can never drift again._  `[consistency]`

## Delegate / advisor / voting / join — language

- **`src/app/join/page.tsx:331`** — There are four places to change the language — SiteNav desktop (:226), SiteNav mobile drawer (:459), /create's nav (:585) and the chair Settings drawer (:816) — and none of them exist on /join, /delegate, /advisor or /voting. Delegates are the largest user group and the only role with no way to switch language; /join, the first screen they see, is the one place it would matter most. → _Put the shared LanguageMenu in the /join nav (join/page.tsx:331) and in the delegate header (delegate:1148, next to the code chip). One control on /join would cover the whole delegate journey since the choice persists to localStorage._  `[wasted-effort]`

## Phase labels

- **`src/app/advisor/[code]/page.tsx:459`** — The same session state is named four different ways. delegate:104 PHASE_LABELS (English, 'adjourned' → 'Debate Closed', 'pre-session' → 'Pre-Session'); delegate:631 PHASE_LABEL (translated but covers only 3 of 7 phases, so a Spanish delegate reads 'Pre-Sesión' then 'Voting Procedure'); LiveModals:70 PHASE_LABELS ('Pre-session' lowercase s, 'adjourned' → 'Adjourned'); advisor:459 has no map at all — `committee.phase.replace(/-/g,' ')` plus CSS capitalize, so the advisor sees 'Speakers List' and 'Adjourned'. An ended committee is 'Debate Closed' to the delegate and 'Adjourned' to the organiser. → _Move one PHASE_LABELS map into src/lib (keyed to translation keys, all 7 phases) and import it in delegate, advisor and manage/live. Delete the three local copies._  `[copy]`

## Dates and times

- **`src/app/conferences/[slug]/page.tsx:42`** — Date formatting is 'en-GB' day-first in 27 places (manage/layout:146 renders '7 Aug 2026') but US month-first 'en' in conferences/[slug]/page.tsx:42, :45 and account/cv/CVTimeline:164 ('Aug 7, 2026'), browser-default `undefined` in ConferenceDetailClient:2177 and settings:2926, and the app's actual locale in exactly one place — chat/chatTokens.ts:55. Times are 'en-GB' 24-hour on organiser surfaces (manage/page.tsx:305, communications:220, RequestsPanel:71) but browser-default on sessions (ScoreboardPanel:219, chatTokens.ts:33), so a US chair reads '2:05 PM' in the scoreboard and '14:05' in the organiser's log for the same event. → _Add fmtDate/fmtTime helpers in src/lib that take the app language, and replace all 33 call sites. The same conference must not print two date orders on two of its own pages._  `[consistency]`

## Wayfinding — back links

- **`src/app/manage/[slug]/layout.tsx:857`** — Eight different back-affordance conventions: '← BACK' with no destination (manage/layout:857), '← BACK TO HOME' (manage/layout:760, goes to '/'), '← BACK TO DASHBOARD' (manage/layout:797), 'Back to conferences' sentence case with a lucide arrow (explore:899), '← BACK TO CONFERENCES' uppercase (map:937), '← BACK TO MAP' (map:1001), '← Back to {acronym}' (apply:3614, :3679), 'START OVER' with an ArrowLeft (import:588), and a bare ChevronLeft in /create (create:634). voting/[code]:537 labels its button 'BACK TO HOME' but sends the user to /sessions, not home. → _Standardise on '← {Destination}' in sentence case with the destination always named, and fix voting:537 to either say 'Back to Sessions' or actually go to '/'._  `[copy]`

## Wayfinding — deep pages

- **`src/app/conferences/[slug]/ConferenceDetailClient.tsx:1117`** — /conferences/[slug] — the deepest and most-linked public page — has no back affordance and no breadcrumb. Its only chrome is `<SiteNav overlay />` (:1117), whose logo goes to '/', not to /conferences/explore or /conferences/map where the user almost certainly came from. /manage/* has a breadcrumb (logo / acronym, layout:836-845) and the apply flow has '← Back to {acronym}', so the conference page is the odd one out. → _Add the same 'logo / acronym' breadcrumb strip the manage header uses, with a browser-history-aware back link to explore/map._  `[missing-state]`

## My Conferences — duplicate destination

- **`src/app/account/conferences/page.tsx:9`** — The same view lives at two URLs with two different chromes: /my-conferences renders MyConferencesClient standalone with SiteNav (my-conferences/MyConferencesClient.tsx:1031) while /account/conferences renders `<MyConferencesClient embedded />` inside the account shell. ProfileDropdown links to /my-conferences (:428) but the account layout links to /account/conferences (account/layout.tsx:23). A user who reaches 'My Conferences' two ways gets two different-looking pages. → _Pick one canonical URL and permanentRedirect the other, the same way /conferences already redirects to '/'._  `[consistency]`

## Account area — nav

- **`src/app/account/layout.tsx:15`** — A fourth navigation system: /account renders SiteNav AND its own vertical link list (NAV_LINKS with lucide icons, UPPERCASE labels, :15-20) AND a horizontal mobile scroller (:78). Label casing across the four navs is inconsistent — SiteNav uses UPPERCASE ('SESSIONS'), the manage rail uses Title Case ('Applications', layout:116), account uses UPPERCASE ('MY PROFILE'), the session tabs use translated sentence case. → _Settle on Title Case for in-app navigation (the manage rail is right — it reads faster at 13px) and reserve UPPERCASE for eyebrows and small buttons only._  `[consistency]`

## Empty states

- **`src/components/MotionsModal.tsx:761`** — The two halves treat 'nothing here' completely differently. Conferences: a full NeuCard with a 48px icon disc, an 'X yet' headline, an explanatory subline and a next-step link (applications:2451-2464, live:784-794 'Create them in Committees →'). Sessions: a single centred grey <p> with no icon, no card and no next step — MotionsModal:761 `motions_no_vote`, :1446 `motions_no_raised`, DocumentsModal:1062-1064 (headline + subline but still no card or icon). A chair opening Motions on a quiet floor sees one grey line. → _Extract <EmptyState icon title body action> from the applications version and use it for all sessions empties, each with a real next step ('Delegates can raise motions from their device — share the code ABC123')._  `[missing-state]`

## Manage — Live Status freshness

- **`src/app/manage/[slug]/live/page.tsx:695`** — Sessions are realtime everywhere — chairs and delegates never refresh — but the organiser's window into those same sessions is manual: a 'Refresh' button plus a 'Refreshed {n}s ago' counter (live/page.tsx:695-716). The freshness model changes identity between the two halves for the same underlying data. → _Subscribe /manage/live to the same Supabase Realtime channel the chair page uses and demote the button to a quiet retry affordance, or at least auto-poll on an interval so the counter never exceeds ~15s._  `[consistency]`

## /sessions — credit chip

- **`src/components/SiteNav.tsx:281`** — SiteNav renders a persistent forest credit chip with a permanent '✨ NEW' flag (:281-321) on every page it appears, including /sessions (HomeClient:597) — but credits are a conferences-only concept (useCredits is consumed only by the apply flow, /account/unlimited and this chip), and neither /create nor /join mentions them. A chair on the sessions landing sees a balance and a NEW badge for a currency their next action does not use. The badge itself is 7px/900 text (:311) with a 13px line-height, which is below readable size. → _Hide the credit chip on sessions routes, and give the NEW badge an expiry date in code (or drop it to 9px) — a permanent 'NEW' stops meaning anything after a month._  `[hierarchy]`

## Chair identity across halves

- **`src/app/join/page.tsx:250`** — A conference chair's session identity is `profile?.display_name ?? user.email ?? 'Chair'` (join:250). With no display name set, the user's EMAIL ADDRESS becomes the chairName — pushed into the URL (:252), shown in the join confirmation copy ('Joining as {email}, chair of this committee', :569), rendered in the GavelChip and used as the chat sender name visible to every delegate. The anonymous path (:279) uses a typed name instead, so the same role produces two very different display outputs. → _Fall back to the email LOCAL PART, or better, require a display name at this step (prefilled, one field) before routing. Never let a full address become a public in-session label._  `[copy]`

## Session views — signed-in identity

- **`src/app/chair/[code]/page.tsx:1236`** — The session cockpits call useAuth (chair:1236, delegate:641, advisor:264, voting:327) purely for the access gate and never render the user. A signed-in conference chair sees no avatar, no name and no sign-out anywhere in /chair — the account exists but is invisible, which makes the session feel like a different product they are not logged into. → _Show the ProfileDropdown avatar in the session toolbar when a user is signed in (it is already portaled and works anywhere), and keep the toolbar unchanged for anonymous sessions._  `[missing-state]`

## Tabular numerals

- **`src/app/manage/[slug]/live/page.tsx:770`** — `tabular-nums` / fontVariantNumeric appears 192 times on the conferences half and 12 times on the sessions half — yet the sessions half is the one with live ticking counters. The main chair timer is covered (chair:3198), but the delegate's caucus clock (delegate:1225-1227, text-3xl font-mono, no tabular), the advisor's 5xl unmoderated countdown (advisor:552 has tabular-nums, good) and the vote tallies on /voting are inconsistent, so digits jitter as they count down. → _Audit every element whose text changes every second or every vote and add fontVariantNumeric: 'tabular-nums'. Start with delegate:1226 — that clock is on a projector in front of a room._  `[polish]`

## Press feedback

- **`src/components/neu.tsx:592`** — NeuButton has the full press model — translateY(-2px) on hover, scale(0.96) on press, a coloured glow (neu.tsx:590-593) — and it is used only on the conferences half. The sessions half has exactly ONE scale-on-press in six route files, and its primary buttons are flat colour swaps (`bg-[#1B3828] hover:bg-[#2A5A3C]`, 86 occurrences) with no press state at all. `transition-all` also appears 37× in sessions vs 19× on conferences. → _Adopt NeuButton for the sessions half's primary CTAs (Start, Next, Begin Session, Create Committee, Join). Replace `transition-all` with explicit property lists — on a 1-second-tick timer page, transitioning `all` is the most expensive class in the file._  `[polish]`

## Chair — caucus progress bar

- **`src/app/chair/[code]/page.tsx:1048`** — `caucusProgress > 50 ? 'bg-[#B6871F]' : caucusProgress > 20 ? 'bg-[#B6871F]' : 'bg-red-500'` — the first two branches are identical, so the >50 test does nothing, and the low-time state uses raw Tailwind `bg-red-500` (#EF4444), a colour that appears nowhere else in the product. The equivalent GSL bar four lines of code away uses NEU.amber #B8844A for the same state (chair:3202). → _Collapse to a two-branch ternary and use #B8844A so the caucus and GSL bars warn identically. Also unify the track: this bar is h-2 bg-[#DDD4C0] while the conferences half uses NeuProgress (inset track + gradient fill)._  `[polish]`

## Search inputs

- **`src/app/manage/[slug]/applications/page.tsx:2361`** — The applications search is the benchmark — a pressed NEU.inSm pill, radius 999, Search icon, aria-label, and a clear ✕ that appears only when non-empty (:2360-2388). No other search in the product has all four. Placeholder punctuation is also split: nine use '...' (three periods: DelegationPortalClient:242, DelegationsView:135, :1042, IndependentsView:62, applications:690, communications:2360, ConferencesClient:163, RequestsPanel:582, EmailComposer:531) and seven use the '…' character. → _Extract <SearchField> from the applications implementation (pill + icon + aria-label + clear button) and use it for all 19 search inputs. Normalise every placeholder to the '…' character._  `[consistency]`

## Dead code — orphaned landing

- **`src/app/conferences/ConferencesClient.tsx:560`** — ConferencesClient.tsx is 585 lines of a previous conferences landing page imported by nothing — /conferences/page.tsx permanentRedirects to '/'. It still carries its own GRAIN copy (:275, :396), its own 'Search conferences...' input (:163) and its own FooterLegal tone="forest" (:552), so anyone grepping for conferences-half patterns finds a design that is no longer shipped. → _Delete it. Same treatment for /conferences/landing-lab if the lab has served its purpose — VariantRecord (507 lines) and VariantFirstGavel (363) are two more unshipped landing designs that a future audit will keep re-reading as if they were live._  `[consistency]`

> **Method / limits:** METHOD — Source read only, no dev server and no browser tools, per the brief. Every count and file:line quoted is reproducible from the repo root with grep/find at audit time on branch feature/conferences-auth with the working tree as-is (including the uncommitted chat/, GavelChip, CommitteeIdentityBadge, VotingRulesPanel, TutorialOverlay work). Contrast ratios were computed with the WCAG 2.1 relative-luminance formula in a throwaway python3 one-liner; the numbers cited (NEU.muted 2.71:1 on NEU.base, NEU.deepGold 2.72:1 on NEU.surface, NEU.amber 2.64:1, CommitteeIdentityBadge secondary 3.99:1 and topic 3.88:1, #6A5A4A 5.36:1) are exact, not estimates. Nothing was edited.

COVERAGE — I swept the coherence surface between the two halves: SiteNav / SessionsHeaderLogo / manage layout / account layout / the five session headers; neu.tsx adoption; card, radius, colour, red, ivory, type, mono, grain and icon systems; empty / loading / error / confirm / toast / modal / popover / wizard patterns; the four conferences→sessions crossings; wayfinding and back-label conventions; i18n and date/time formatting; and the four AGENTS.md UI RULES read cross-product. I deliberately did NOT re-report per-screen issues inside a single half that the other auditors already own.

WHAT I DID NOT COVER — I did not audit /conferences/[slug]/pay, /delegation/[societyId], /conferences/map, the /auth and /invites flows or /account/* as screens; I only checked their chrome, tokens and nav. Those remain the largest unexamined surfaces and should get their own passes.

IF ONLY FIVE THINGS HAPPEN, in order of leverage:
1. Darken NEU.muted (neu.tsx:33). One token, hundreds of sub-4.5:1 labels fixed on both halves at once.
2. Move the fit-root transform correction into Portal.tsx:9 and delete the four duplicates. Nine popovers corrected, three currently-broken ones fixed, and the AGENTS.md popover rule becomes true on session pages for the first time.
3. Extract <PageHeader> from manage/[slug]/applications/page.tsx:2354 and put it on all eleven manage routes. Cheapest change that makes the organiser dashboard read as one product.
4. Unify the card treatment (NEU.surface + NEU.out) across the sessions half. Biggest single visual divergence between the halves.
5. One <SessionCodeChip> and one <EnterSessionButton>. These are the two objects that literally sit on the seam between the halves, and today each has five and four incompatible renderings.

NON-UI OBSERVATIONS (per the brief, not filed as findings):
• src/app/conferences/ConferencesClient.tsx is imported by nothing — 585 lines of dead code (also filed as a finding since it actively misleads design review).
• src/components/DemoGate.tsx:12-15 comments that "the live committee-session product (/, /join, /create, …)" must stay open and lists '/' as a sessions route. '/' now renders StagefrontClient, the conferences landing. If DEMO_GATE_ENABLED (:11) is ever flipped back to true, the conferences homepage would be left ungated by that stale assumption.
• src/app/grain-dev/page.tsx is a texture playground shipped in production routing with no noindex that I could see.
• src/app/join/page.tsx:250 — the email-as-chairName fallback is filed above as a copy/privacy finding, but it is also a data-exposure issue worth a functional look: the address ends up in the URL, in committees.chair_names[] and in the messages table as the sender.
• The completeness critic's note that layout.tsx loads Playfair Display as ital@1 only is now STALE — layout.tsx:130 already requests `Playfair+Display:ital,wght@0,700;1,400` and the comment at :118-121 documents the fix. Noting it so it is not "fixed" a second time.
• src/components/Skeleton.tsx:8-12 contains the single best-written rule in the codebase ("An empty state is a conclusion about data that has actually resolved... never render an empty state as a stand-in for still loading"). It is worth promoting into AGENTS.md, because the sessions half violates it everywhere by having no loading states between the gavel spinner and the final render.


---

# Cross-cutting synthesis (partial)

51 inline `right:` against 7 `insetInlineStart` and three `rtl:` variants in the whole codebase. Run one mechanical pass to logical properties; the nav's active-item `borderLeft`, the fixed popovers at `right: 2rem`, the blog callouts' `borderLeft` and the gold `▌` accents are the visible casualties.
- `←` and `→` are not Bidi-mirrored characters, so the Arabic locale flipped forward CTAs to `←` but left the *back* buttons as `←` too — Next and Back point the same way in Arabic (`translations.ts:2887`, `:2947`, `:2948`, `:3002`, `:3010`).
- `voting_against_bar: '← {n} معارِض'` and `voting_for_bar: '{n} مؤيِّد ←'` — in English the two arrows point outward, which *is* the tug-of-war metaphor; in Arabic both point the same way, so the bars appear to push together.
- Two translation keys tell the user the gavel chip is "at the top-right of the session" in all four locales including Arabic — positional copy an RTL build can never keep true.
- The chair Settings drawer and `/create`'s language menu use a `fixed inset-0` click-catcher which, inside the transformed fit-root, is sized to the scaled root rather than the viewport — so clicks in the uncovered strip do not close it.
- Zero `@media print` and zero `@page` rules exist, yet the product generates things people demonstrably print: invoices, delegation placards, the public MUN CV, the scoreboard, study-guide rosters, a conference page for a permission slip. Printing any of them today emits the nav, the fixed grain, every shadow, and on session pages a transform-scaled root at the wrong size.
- 155 raw `<img>` elements against 2 uses of `next/image`: 140 have no `loading` attribute, 83 no `onError`, and 54 no width/height — so they reserve no space and shift the layout when they land. The `display:none` error handler appears 63 times.
- The session tree carries 523 inline `fontSize` values at ≤11px plus 236 Tailwind `text-[≤11px]` utilities, all multiplied by FitToScreen's fractional scale — on a 1024×768 projector a 9px label paints at 8.4 device px. This is the one product context where the screen is read from across a room, and the elements the room needs (session code 12px, queue positions 10px, ABSENT tag 10px at 35%) are the smallest on it while the 128px unmoderated clock is the largest.
- Native OS dialogs survive in four files: two `alert()`s on `/create`, three in Settings, a `window.confirm` + `window.alert` in Committees (which already imports `useConfirmModal` on line 11 and uses it correctly three times in the same file), and two `window.prompt` clipboard fallbacks.
- Six copy buttons, four acknowledgements: a ✓-swap at 2000ms (chair, Settings, ChairParticipant, AllocationCard, Committees), 1600ms (Live), 2200ms (account CV), a toast (manage dashboard), and **nothing at all** on the delegate header. `live/page.tsx:46` also sets `copied` unconditionally with the failure swallowed, so on an insecure origin it shows "Copied" for something that was never copied.
- Pluralisation is handled correctly in exactly one place (`DelegationPortalClient.tsx:657`); elsewhere `session_hours_until_delete` takes an `{s}` variable callers fill with `'s'` or `''` — leaking the English plural rule into Arabic, which has six plural forms.
- Date formatting splits three ways — `'en-GB'` day-first in 27 places, US month-first in three, browser-default in two, and the app's actual locale in exactly one (`chatTokens.ts:55`) — and times are 24-hour on organiser surfaces but browser-default on sessions, so a US chair reads "2:05 PM" in the scoreboard and "14:05" in the organiser's log for the same event.
- The same session state is named four ways: `PHASE_LABELS` in the delegate page (English, "Debate Closed"), a second translated `PHASE_LABEL` covering only 3 of 7 phases, `PHASE_LABELS` in LiveModals ("Adjourned"), and no map at all on the advisor page. An ended committee is "Debate Closed" to the delegate and "Adjourned" to the organiser.
- Eight back-affordance conventions ship ("← BACK", "← BACK TO HOME", "← BACK TO DASHBOARD", "Back to conferences", "← BACK TO MAP", "← Back to {acronym}", "START OVER", a bare ChevronLeft) — and `voting/[code]:537` is labelled "BACK TO HOME" while sending the user to `/sessions`.
- `/conferences/[slug]` — the deepest and most-linked public page — has no back affordance and no breadcrumb, and its only chrome is an overlay SiteNav whose logo goes to `/`.
- The same view lives at two URLs with two different chromes: `/my-conferences` standalone and `/account/conferences` embedded, with ProfileDropdown linking to one and the account layout to the other.
